Grammar & commentary

Madhyama Carita · मध्यमचरितम् · Chapter 2 of 13

महिषासुरसैन्यवधःThe Slaughter of Mahiṣāsura’s Armies

mahiṣāsurasainyavadhaḥ

69 mantras · 2.1–2.69

❁

Overview

Medhas begins the second story (2.1). For a hundred years the gods under Indra fought the asuras under Mahiṣa; the gods were routed and Mahiṣāsura made himself Indra (2.2–3). The defeated gods, with Brahmā at their head, went to Śiva and Viṣṇu and told them everything: Mahiṣa holds the offices of the Sun, Indra, Fire, Wind, Moon, Yama and Varuṇa, and the gods, cast out of heaven, wander the earth like mortals; they take refuge and ask that his death be planned (2.4–8). Viṣṇu and Śiva grew angry, and a great radiance (tejas) came from the faces of Viṣṇu, Brahmā and Śiva and from the bodies of the other gods; it merged into one, blazed like a mountain on fire, and became a woman whose light filled the three worlds (2.9–13). Each god’s radiance formed a part of her body, and the auspicious one, Śivā, arose (2.14–18); the gods rejoiced (2.19). They armed her with weapons drawn from their own — Śiva’s trident, Viṣṇu’s discus, Varuṇa’s conch and noose, Agni’s spear, the Wind’s bow and quivers, Indra’s thunderbolt and Airāvata’s bell, Yama’s staff, a rosary and a water-pot, Time’s sword and shield — and adorned her with jewels, garments and garlands; Himavat gave her a lion, Kubera a cup never empty of wine, Śeṣa a serpent-necklace (2.20–31). She roared again and again with laughter; the sky, the worlds, the seas, the earth and the mountains shook; the gods cried ‘Victory!’ to the lion-rider and the sages praised her (2.32–35). The asuras rose in arms; Mahiṣāsura rushed towards the sound and saw her filling the three worlds, her crown touching the sky, her thousand arms spread through the directions (2.35–39). The battle began, and the text calls the roll of Mahiṣa’s generals — Cikṣura, Cāmara, Udagra, Mahāhanu, Asiloman, Bāṣkala, Biḍāla and others — with forces counted in ayutas and crores, and Mahiṣāsura himself in their midst (2.39–47). Their weapons she cut down as if in play, unwearied, and loosed her own (2.48–51); her lion raged through their ranks like fire through a forest, and her breaths became hosts (gaṇas) by the hundred thousand, who fought and beat the drums of a ‘festival of war’ (2.51–55). With trident, mace, sword, bell and noose she struck the great asuras down; headless trunks fought on and danced and cried ‘Stand!’ (2.55–64). The field became impassable and ran with rivers of blood (2.65–66); in a moment Ambikā consumed the army as fire consumes straw and timber (2.67); the lion roared among the fallen (2.68), and the gods in heaven rained down flowers (2.69). Mahiṣāsura himself is left for chapter 3.

  • Refuge: the defeated gods turn for help
  • Tejas: the gods’ powers made one in the Goddess
  • The arming of the Devī with the gods’ own weapons
  • Līlā: the battle as effortless play
  • Her hosts are her own power
About this chapter
Forms of the Devī
The radiance of all the gods become a woman, filling the three worlds with her light (नारी, 2.13); Śivā, ‘the auspicious’, the name given her as her body is completed (2.18); the armed and adorned Devī who receives every god’s weapon (2.20–31); the lion-rider, सिंहवाहिनी, hailed with ‘Victory!’ (2.34); the cosmic form Mahiṣa sees, the earth bowed beneath her tread and her crown grazing the sky, a thousand arms in every direction (2.37–39); Caṇḍikā, ‘the fierce’ (2.49); Īśvarī, ‘the sovereign’, as she turns to the attack (2.51); and Ambikā, ‘the Mother’, the warrior whose breath becomes an army and who consumes the asura host (2.52, 2.67).
Significance of the events
The first carita showed the Goddess as Mahāmāyā within Viṣṇu; this chapter shows her taking a body and weapons. The gods, separately, have lost everything; their anger and their pooled radiance become one being, and the gifts they give her are drawn out of their own weapons. Medhas has already said that she is eternal and is called ‘born’ only when she appears for the gods’ purpose (1.64–66), so the chapter is read as her manifestation, not her creation; the gods’ own hymn in chapter 4 calls her form the gathered power of all the hosts of gods (4.3). The chapter destroys Mahiṣa’s armies and leaves the buffalo-asura himself for chapter 3.
Sanskrit patterns to watch
Narrative perfects in great number, with every kind of reduplication (ददौ, ददृशुः, चुक्षुभुः, तुष्टुवुः, निजघान) and the unreduplicated e-perfects (चेलुः, पेतुः, वेमुः); periphrastic perfects of derived stems (कथयामासुः, 2.5; पातयामास, 2.56); past participles in place of finite verbs (पराजितम्, गताः, सम्भूताः) and the past active participle दत्तवान् alternating with ददौ in the list of gifts; the instrumental of source in the forming of her body (विष्णुतेजसा, सौम्येन, 2.14–17); a locative absolute at the opening (महिषे … अधिपे, 2.2) and in the battle (छिन्ने … शिरसि, 2.62); a passive imperative as a polite request (विचिन्त्यताम्, 2.8); long catalogues that run across verse boundaries, some with no verb of their own (2.16, 2.24–29, 2.40–48); numbers in ayutas, niyutas and crores; and epic (ārṣa) forms — parasmaipada युयुधुः and भाषन्तः from ātmanepada roots, अददत्, विचिन्वति, the genitive of the recipient (तस्याः, 2.24).
The teaching, as distinct from the narrative
Literally, defeated gods appeal to the great gods, a woman is formed from their combined radiance and armed with their weapons, and she destroys an asura army with her lion and her hosts. As teaching, the tradition reads the chapter as showing that the divine powers which fail when they act apart are, gathered together, one power — and that this power is hers, not theirs: she is eternal and only takes visible form for the gods’ purpose (1.64–66), the weapons they give are drawn out of their own, and the army that fights for her is her own breath (2.52–53), as she herself says later that there is no one but her (10.5). Her action is play (लीलयैव, 2.50), without strain. The gods’ part is to take refuge (2.8) and to praise; the asuras’ part is to meet her as a disturbance to be put down (2.36).
Carita
Madhyama Carita (the middle episode, chapters 2–4). In the recitation tradition its presiding form is Mahālakṣmī: the Gita Press viniyoga recited before 2.1 names Viṣṇu its ṛṣi, Mahālakṣmī its deity, uṣṇik its metre, Śākambharī its śakti and Durgā its bīja, and the dhyāna pictures Mahālakṣmī, seated on a lotus, holding eighteen objects as the crusher of the buffalo (सैरिभमर्दिनी). This is liturgical tradition, not a statement of the Purāṇa text, which never uses the name Mahālakṣmī in this chapter.
Recited before the chapter

These lines belong to the recitation (the Gita Press pāṭha), not to the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa text, and are kept apart from the numbered mantras.

Viniyoga

ॐ मध्यमचरित्रस्य विष्णुर्ऋषिः, महालक्ष्मीर्देवता, उष्णिक् छन्दः, शाकम्भरी शक्तिः, दुर्गा बीजम्, वायुस्तत्त्वम्, यजुर्वेदः स्वरूपम्, श्रीमहालक्ष्मीप्रीत्यर्थं मध्यमचरित्रजपे विनियोगः।

ॐ madhyamacaritrasya viṣṇurṛṣiḥ, mahālakṣmīrdevatā, uṣṇik chandaḥ, śākambharī śaktiḥ, durgā bījam, vāyustattvam, yajurvedaḥ svarūpam, śrīmahālakṣmīprītyarthaṃ madhyamacaritrajape viniyogaḥ |

Dhyāna

ॐ अक्षस्रक्परशुं गदेषुकुलिशं पद्मं धनुष्कुण्डिकांदण्डं शक्तिमसिं च चर्म जलजं घण्टां सुराभाजनम्।शूलं पाशसुदर्शने च दधतीं हस्तैः प्रसन्नाननांसेवे सैरिभमर्दिनीमिह महालक्ष्मीं सरोजस्थिताम्॥

ॐ akṣasrakparaśuṃ gadeṣukuliśaṃ padmaṃ dhanuṣkuṇḍikāṃdaṇḍaṃ śaktimasiṃ ca carma jalajaṃ ghaṇṭāṃ surābhājanam |śūlaṃ pāśasudarśane ca dadhatīṃ hastaiḥ prasannānanāṃseve sairibhamardinīmiha mahālakṣmīṃ sarojasthitām ||

  2. उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

Translation

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    Visarga after i, before a vowel, is sounded as r.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

Anvaya · prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Medhas, still speaking to King Suratha and the merchant Samādhi, begins the second story: the Goddess’s war with Mahiṣāsura and his armies. This uvāca opens the middle carita (Madhyama Carita), which runs from chapter 2 to chapter 4.

Traditional interpretation

In the Gita Press pāṭha the middle carita has its own viniyoga and dhyāna, recited before this mantra: Viṣṇu is named its ṛṣi, Mahālakṣmī its deity, uṣṇik its metre, Śākambharī its śakti and Durgā its bīja. This is liturgical tradition, not part of the Purāṇa. The bījas ॐ ह्रीं that open the mantra in recitation belong to the same pāṭha.

Verse 2.2

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

देवासुरमभूद्युद्धं पूर्णमब्दशतं पुरा।महिषेऽसुराणामधिपे देवानां च पुरन्दरे॥⁠२⁠॥

devāsuramabhūdyuddhaṃ pūrṇamabdaśataṃ purā |mahiṣe'surāṇāmadhipe devānāṃ ca purandare ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

देवासुरम् · अभूत् · युद्धम् · पूर्णम् · अब्दशतम् · पुरामहिषे · असुराणाम् · अधिपे · देवानाम् · च · पुरन्दरे

devāsuram · abhūt · yuddham · pūrṇam · abdaśatam · purāmahiṣe · asurāṇām · adhipe · devānām · ca · purandare

Word by word

  1. देवासुरम्devāsuramof the gods and asuras, between gods and asurasn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies युद्धम्
  2. अभूत्abhūtthere was√भू (bhvādi, 1P ‘to be, become’), luṅ (aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  3. युद्धम्yuddhama warn. nom. sg. of युद्ध (√युध् + क्त, used as a noun ‘fighting’); subject of अभूत्
  4. पूर्णम्pūrṇamfull, entire√पॄ + क्त (‘filled’), n. acc. sg.; qualifies अब्दशतम्
  5. अब्दशतम्abdaśatama hundred yearsn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); accusative of duration of time
  6. पुराpurāformerly, long agoindeclinable (adverb of time)
  7. महिषेmahiṣeMahiṣa (being)m. loc. sg. of महिष, ‘buffalo’, here the asura’s name; locative absolute (सति understood)
  8. असुराणाम्asurāṇāmof the asurasm. gen. pl. of असुर; depends on अधिपे
  9. अधिपेadhipelord, overlordm. loc. sg. of अधिप (अधि + √पा ‘to protect’ + क); predicate in the locative absolute
  10. देवानाम्devānāmof the godsm. gen. pl. of देव; with अधिपे understood
  11. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  12. पुरन्दरेpurandarePurandara, i.e. Indra (being lord)m. loc. sg. of पुरन्दर; second locative absolute

Translation

Long ago there was a war of gods and asuras for a full hundred years, Mahiṣa being lord of the asuras and Purandara of the gods.

In readable EnglishLong ago the gods and the asuras fought for a full hundred years, when Mahiṣa was lord of the asuras and Indra lord of the gods.

Purandara is rendered by the familiar name Indra in tr.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • देवासुरम् devāsuram → देव + असुरम्

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā: देव + असुर → देवासुर.

  • अभूद्युद्धं abhūdyuddhaṃ → अभूत् + युद्धम्

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before the voiced y. The m of युद्धम् becomes anusvāra before the p of पूर्णम् (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • महिषेऽसुराणाम् mahiṣe'surāṇām → महिषे + असुराणाम्

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    Final e absorbs the initial a (avagraha). Even with this elision the pāda महिषेऽसुराणामधिपे counts nine syllables, one more than the eight of an anuṣṭubh pāda: it is hypermetric, as in every witness (see the metre notes).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • देवासुरम् devāsuram

    dvandva (itaretara), used attributively

    Members
    देव + असुर
    Vigraha
    देवाश्च असुराश्च देवासुराः, तेषां (युद्धम्) devāśca asurāśca devāsurāḥ, teṣāṃ (yuddham)
    Literally
    “the gods-and-asuras (war)”
    In the verse
    the war between the gods and the asuras
    Alternative
    It can also be taken as a samāhāra dvandva (neuter singular) in loose apposition to युद्धम्. The regular adjective of relation would be दैवासुर (with vṛddhi); the Purāṇas use the bare dvandva in the stock phrase देवासुरं युद्धम्, and the attributive reading fits the agreement with युद्धम्.

  • अब्दशतम् abdaśatam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अब्द + शत
    Vigraha
    अब्दानां शतम् abdānāṃ śatam
    Literally
    “a hundred of years”
    In the verse
    a full century

  • पुरन्दरे purandare

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    पुर् + दर
    Vigraha
    पुरः दारयति इति पुरन्दरः, तस्मिन् puraḥ dārayati iti purandaraḥ, tasmin
    Literally
    “splitter of strongholds”
    In the verse
    Indra, breaker of the enemies’ forts; the first member takes the augment m (पुरम्-दर)
Morphology & anvaya

  • महिषे mahiṣe

    Locative absolute (sati saptamī): महिषे असुराणाम् अधिपे (सति), ‘Mahiṣa being lord of the asuras’; the second, पुरन्दरे देवानाम् (अधिपे सति), shares the same predicate.

  • अब्दशतम् abdaśatam

    Accusative of duration: the time over which something lasts stands in the accusative, ‘for a hundred years’.

  • अभूत् abhūt

    Root aorist of √भू with augment a-, ‘there was’; as in 1.4, used for narrative past.

Anvaya · prose order

पुरा असुराणाम् अधिपे महिषे, देवानां च (अधिपे) पुरन्दरे (सति), पूर्णम् अब्दशतं देवासुरं युद्धम् अभूत्।

purā asurāṇām adhipe mahiṣe, devānāṃ ca (adhipe) purandare (sati), pūrṇam abdaśataṃ devāsuraṃ yuddham abhūt |

The main clause (देवासुरं युद्धम् अभूत्, with पूर्णम् अब्दशतम् for its length) comes first; two locative absolutes name the leaders on each side.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The second story opens like the first, with a time and a conflict: a hundred-year war, with Mahiṣa leading the asuras and Indra the gods. The first pāda of the second line is one syllable too long (nine, not eight) in every witness; the text is left as it is.

Traditional interpretation

Mahiṣa means ‘buffalo’; the asura’s buffalo form appears in chapter 3, where he fights the Goddess in that shape and in others. The tradition calls the Goddess of this carita Mahiṣāsuramardinī, ‘crusher of the buffalo-asura’.

Verse 2.3

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तत्रासुरैर्महावीर्यैर्देवसैन्यं पराजितम्।जित्वा च सकलान् देवानिन्द्रोऽभून्महिषासुरः॥⁠३⁠॥

tatrāsurairmahāvīryairdevasainyaṃ parājitam |jitvā ca sakalān devānindro'bhūnmahiṣāsuraḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तत्र · असुरैः · महावीर्यैः · देवसैन्यम् · पराजितम्जित्वा · च · सकलान् · देवान् · इन्द्रः · अभूत् · महिषासुरः

tatra · asuraiḥ · mahāvīryaiḥ · devasainyam · parājitamjitvā · ca · sakalān · devān · indraḥ · abhūt · mahiṣāsuraḥ

Word by word

  1. तत्रtatrathere, in that (war)indeclinable (adverb of place, taddhita त्रल् from तद्)
  2. असुरैःasuraiḥby the asurasm. instr. pl. of असुर; agent of पराजितम्
  3. महावीर्यैःmahāvīryaiḥof great valourm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies असुरैः
  4. देवसैन्यम्devasainyamthe army of the godsn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of the passive participle
  5. पराजितम्parājitamwas defeatedपरा + √जि (bhvādi, 1 ‘to conquer’) + क्त (past passive participle), n. nom. sg.; used as the verb (अभूत् understood)
  6. जित्वाjitvāhaving conquered√जि + क्त्वा (absolutive); its agent is महिषासुरः
  7. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  8. सकलान्sakalānallm. acc. pl. of सकल; qualifies देवान्
  9. देवान्devānthe godsm. acc. pl. of देव; object of जित्वा
  10. इन्द्रःindraḥIndra, i.e. lord of heavenm. nom. sg. of इन्द्र; predicate nominative with अभूत्
  11. अभूत्abhūtbecame√भू (bhvādi, 1P), luṅ (aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. महिषासुरःmahiṣāsuraḥMahiṣāsura, the buffalo-asuram. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of अभूत्

Translation

There the army of the gods was defeated by the asuras of great valour; and having conquered all the gods, Mahiṣāsura became Indra.

In readable EnglishIn that war the mighty asuras routed the army of the gods; and having conquered every god, Mahiṣāsura made himself Indra.

‘Made himself Indra’ renders इन्द्रः अभूत् ‘became Indra’, i.e. took Indra’s place as king of heaven.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तत्रासुरैर्महावीर्यैर्देवसैन्यं tatrāsurairmahāvīryairdevasainyaṃ → तत्र + असुरैः + महावीर्यैः + देवसैन्यम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    a + a → ā (तत्रासुरैः). After ai the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before the voiced m and d.

  • इन्द्रोऽभून्महिषासुरः indro'bhūnmahiṣāsuraḥ → इन्द्रः + अभूत् + महिषासुरः

    visarga sandhi; consonant sandhi (nasal) · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109); यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    -aḥ before a: the visarga becomes u, a + u → o, and the following a is elided (avagraha). Then the final t of अभूत् becomes the nasal n before the m of महिषासुरः.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महावीर्यैः mahāvīryaiḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    महत् + वीर्य
    Vigraha
    महत् वीर्यं येषां ते, तैः mahat vīryaṃ yeṣāṃ te, taiḥ
    Literally
    “those whose valour is great”
    In the verse
    mighty, heroic asuras; महत् becomes महा- in the compound

  • देवसैन्यम् devasainyam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    देव + सैन्य
    Vigraha
    देवानां सैन्यम् devānāṃ sainyam
    Literally
    “the army of the gods”
    In the verse
    the gods’ host

  • महिषासुरः mahiṣāsuraḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महिष + असुर
    Vigraha
    महिषः च असौ असुरः च mahiṣaḥ ca asau asuraḥ ca
    Literally
    “the asura who is Mahiṣa (the buffalo)”
    In the verse
    the buffalo-asura
    Alternative
    Also explained as a karmadhāraya with an elided middle member (महिषरूपः असुरः, ‘the asura in buffalo form’). Since महिष is his name in 2.2, the simple appositional reading fits.
Morphology & anvaya

  • पराजितम् parājitam

    Past passive participle used as a finite verb: ‘(was) defeated’. With परा, √जि means ‘to defeat’ (and takes ātmanepada when finite).

  • जित्वा jitvā

    Absolutive (क्त्वा) of √जि: ‘having conquered’. It shares its agent with the main verb: Mahiṣāsura conquered, then became Indra.

Anvaya · prose order

तत्र महावीर्यैः असुरैः देवसैन्यं पराजितम्। सकलान् देवान् जित्वा च महिषासुरः इन्द्रः अभूत्।

tatra mahāvīryaiḥ asuraiḥ devasainyaṃ parājitam | sakalān devān jitvā ca mahiṣāsuraḥ indraḥ abhūt |

Two sentences: a passive one (देवसैन्यं पराजितम्, agent in the instrumental) and an active one with an absolutive (जित्वा … महिषासुरः इन्द्रः अभूत्).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The war ends in the gods’ defeat, and Mahiṣa takes Indra’s place. ‘Indra’ here is an office: lordship over heaven, which the victor seizes.

Traditional interpretation

The gods’ complaint (2.6) spells out what the usurpation means: Mahiṣa occupies the offices (अधिकारान्) of the Sun, Fire, Wind, Moon, Yama and Varuṇa as well as Indra’s. Cosmic functions in the Purāṇas are posts held by gods, and here they have been taken over by a single asura.

Verse 2.4

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ततः पराजिता देवाः पद्मयोनिं प्रजापतिम्।पुरस्कृत्य गतास्तत्र यत्रेशगरुडध्वजौ॥⁠४⁠॥

tataḥ parājitā devāḥ padmayoniṃ prajāpatim |puraskṛtya gatāstatra yatreśagaruḍadhvajau ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ततः · पराजिताः · देवाः · पद्मयोनिम् · प्रजापतिम्पुरस्कृत्य · गताः · तत्र · यत्र · ईशगरुडध्वजौ

tataḥ · parājitāḥ · devāḥ · padmayonim · prajāpatimpuraskṛtya · gatāḥ · tatra · yatra · īśagaruḍadhvajau

Word by word

  1. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb, taddhita तसिल् from तद्)
  2. पराजिताःparājitāḥdefeatedपरा + √जि + क्त, m. nom. pl.; qualifies देवाः
  3. देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of गताः
  4. पद्मयोनिम्padmayonimthe lotus-born (Brahmā)m. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of पुरस्कृत्य
  5. प्रजापतिम्prajāpatimthe lord of creaturesm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to पद्मयोनिम्
  6. पुरस्कृत्यpuraskṛtyaplacing at their headपुरस् (gati) + √कृ (tanādi, 8 ‘to do’) + ल्यप् (absolutive with a prefix; तुक् augment after the short vowel)
  7. गताःgatāḥwent√गम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to go’) + क्त, m. nom. pl.; used as the finite verb
  8. तत्रtatrathere, to that placeindeclinable (adverb of place); correlative of यत्र
  9. यत्रyatrawhereindeclinable (relative adverb of place)
  10. ईशगरुडध्वजौīśagaruḍadhvajauthe Lord (Śiva) and the Garuḍa-bannered one (Viṣṇu)m. nom. dual; compound (see Samāsa); subject of the relative clause (स्थितौ ‘were’ understood)

Translation

Then the defeated gods, placing at their head the lotus-born Prajāpati, went there where the Lord and the Garuḍa-bannered one were.

In readable EnglishThen the defeated gods, with Brahmā the lotus-born creator at their head, went to where Śiva and Viṣṇu were.

ईश and गरुडध्वज are rendered by the names Śiva and Viṣṇu; ‘were’ is supplied.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • पराजिता देवाः parājitā devāḥ → पराजिताः + देवाः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced sound: the visarga (as रु) becomes y, and the y is dropped before a consonant, leaving -ā.

  • गतास्तत्र gatāstatra → गताः + तत्र

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • यत्रेश yatreśa → यत्र + ईश(गरुडध्वजौ)

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + ī → e.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • पद्मयोनिम् padmayonim

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    पद्म + योनि
    Vigraha
    पद्मं योनिः (उत्पत्तिस्थानम्) यस्य सः, तम् padmaṃ yoniḥ (utpattisthānam) yasya saḥ, tam
    Literally
    “he whose source is the lotus”
    In the verse
    Brahmā, born from the lotus of Viṣṇu’s navel

  • प्रजापतिम् prajāpatim

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    प्रजा + पति
    Vigraha
    प्रजानां पतिः, तम् prajānāṃ patiḥ, tam
    Literally
    “lord of offspring”
    In the verse
    the creator, Brahmā

  • ईशगरुडध्वजौ īśagaruḍadhvajau

    dvandva (itaretara)

    Members
    ईश + गरुडध्वज
    Vigraha
    ईशः च गरुडध्वजः च īśaḥ ca garuḍadhvajaḥ ca
    Literally
    “the Lord and the Garuḍa-bannered”
    In the verse
    Śiva and Viṣṇu; the dual counts the two members

  • गरुडध्वज garuḍadhvaja

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    गरुड + ध्वज
    Vigraha
    गरुडः ध्वजे यस्य सः garuḍaḥ dhvaje yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he who has Garuḍa on his banner”
    In the verse
    Viṣṇu
Morphology & anvaya

  • पुरस्कृत्य puraskṛtya

    Absolutive of पुरस् + √कृ: पुरस् is treated as a verbal prefix (gati), so the absolutive takes -य (ल्यप्), with t inserted after the short ṛ: पुरस्कृ-त्-य. ‘Having put in front’: the gods go with Brahmā leading them.

  • गताः gatāḥ

    Past participle of √गम् standing for a finite verb: ‘(they) went’.

Anvaya · prose order

ततः पराजिताः देवाः पद्मयोनिं प्रजापतिं पुरस्कृत्य, यत्र ईशगरुडध्वजौ (स्थितौ), तत्र गताः।

tataḥ parājitāḥ devāḥ padmayoniṃ prajāpatiṃ puraskṛtya, yatra īśagaruḍadhvajau (sthitau), tatra gatāḥ |

Main clause देवाः … गताः, with an absolutive phrase (पुरस्कृत्य) and a relative clause of place (यत्र …) answered by तत्र.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods go to the two great gods with Brahmā at their head. This is the usual Purāṇic pattern: when the lesser gods are defeated, they seek the higher powers.

Traditional interpretation

All three gods of the trimūrti are present. The Goddess who appears will come from their tejas first (2.10) and then from that of all the others (2.11). In the first carita Brahmā alone praised her; here all the gods together bring her forth.

Verse 2.5

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

यथावृत्तं तयोस्तद्वन्महिषासुरचेष्टितम्।त्रिदशाः कथयामासुर्देवाभिभवविस्तरम्॥⁠५⁠॥

yathāvṛttaṃ tayostadvanmahiṣāsuraceṣṭitam |tridaśāḥ kathayāmāsurdevābhibhavavistaram ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

यथावृत्तम् · तयोः · तद्वत् · महिषासुरचेष्टितम्त्रिदशाः · कथयामासुः · देवाभिभवविस्तरम्

yathāvṛttam · tayoḥ · tadvat · mahiṣāsuraceṣṭitamtridaśāḥ · kathayāmāsuḥ · devābhibhavavistaram

Word by word

  1. यथावृत्तम्yathāvṛttamjust as it happenedindeclinable; compound (see Samāsa), used adverbially
  2. तयोःtayoḥto those twopronoun तद्, m. gen. dual; the hearers of कथयामासुः (genitive for dative)
  3. तद्वत्tadvatlikewise, and in the same wayindeclinable (तद् + taddhita वति ‘like that’)
  4. महिषासुरचेष्टितम्mahiṣāsuraceṣṭitamthe doings of Mahiṣāsuran. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of कथयामासुः
  5. त्रिदशाःtridaśāḥthe Thirty, the godsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject
  6. कथयामासुःkathayāmāsuḥtold, related√कथ् (curādi, 10 ‘to tell’), liṭ (periphrastic perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  7. देवाभिभवविस्तरम्devābhibhavavistaramthe full account of the gods’ humiliationm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); second object of कथयामासुः

Translation

The Thirty related to those two, just as it happened, the doings of Mahiṣāsura and likewise the full account of the gods’ humiliation.

In readable EnglishThe gods told the two of them everything Mahiṣāsura had done, exactly as it happened, and the whole story of their own defeat.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तयोस्तद्वन्महिषासुर tayostadvanmahiṣāsura → तयोः + तद्वत् + महिषासुर(चेष्टितम्)

    visarga sandhi; consonant sandhi (nasal) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    Visarga before t becomes s (तयोस्तद्वत्); final t before the nasal m becomes n (तद्वन्महिषासुर).

  • कथयामासुर्देवाभिभव kathayāmāsurdevābhibhava → कथयामासुः + देवाभिभव(विस्तरम्)

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After u the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before the voiced d. Inside the compound, देव + अभिभव → देवाभिभव (अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः, 6.1.101).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • यथावृत्तम् yathāvṛttam

    avyayībhāva

    Members
    यथा + वृत्त
    Vigraha
    वृत्तम् अनतिक्रम्य vṛttam anatikramya
    Literally
    “not going beyond what happened”
    In the verse
    exactly as it occurred
    Alternative
    It could also be read as an adjective (‘as it had happened’) qualifying महिषासुरचेष्टितम्; the adverbial avyayībhāva is the usual use of यथावृत्तम्.

  • महिषासुरचेष्टितम् mahiṣāsuraceṣṭitam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    महिषासुर + चेष्टित
    Vigraha
    महिषासुरस्य चेष्टितम् mahiṣāsurasya ceṣṭitam
    Literally
    “the doing of Mahiṣāsura”
    In the verse
    all that Mahiṣāsura had done

  • त्रिदशाः tridaśāḥ

    bahuvrīhi (of number)

    Members
    त्रि + दश
    Vigraha
    त्रिगुणिताः दश परिमाणम् एषां ते triguṇitāḥ daśa parimāṇam eṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “those whose count is three tens”
    In the verse
    the gods, ‘the Thirty’ (a round number for the thirty-three gods)
    Alternative
    A traditional etymology instead takes दशा ‘stage of life’: the gods know only three stages and never grow old. The numerical sense is the usual one.

  • देवाभिभवविस्तरम् devābhibhavavistaram

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (outer), containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (देवाभिभव)

    Members
    देवाभिभव + विस्तर
    Vigraha
    देवानाम् अभिभवः देवाभिभवः; तस्य विस्तरः, तम् devānām abhibhavaḥ devābhibhavaḥ; tasya vistaraḥ, tam
    Literally
    “the detail of the overpowering of the gods”
    In the verse
    the whole story of how the gods were humbled
Morphology & anvaya

  • कथयामासुः kathayāmāsuḥ

    Periphrastic perfect: a derived (here curādi) stem कथय- cannot reduplicate, so it takes -आम् and the perfect of √अस् (आसुः, 3rd pl.): कथयाम् + आसुः.

  • तद्वत् tadvat

    तद् + the taddhita वति ‘like’: ‘in that way, likewise’. Here it links the two objects: Mahiṣāsura’s doings and, likewise, the gods’ defeat.

Anvaya · prose order

त्रिदशाः तयोः महिषासुरचेष्टितं तद्वत् देवाभिभवविस्तरं (च) यथावृत्तं कथयामासुः।

tridaśāḥ tayoḥ mahiṣāsuraceṣṭitaṃ tadvat devābhibhavavistaraṃ (ca) yathāvṛttaṃ kathayāmāsuḥ |

Subject (त्रिदशाः), hearers in the genitive (तयोः), two objects joined by तद्वत्, the manner (यथावृत्तम्), and the verb.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods report to Śiva and Viṣṇu. The next three verses (2.6–8) give their report in their own words.

Traditional interpretation

The gods here are called ‘the Thirty’ (त्रिदशाः), a common name of the gods in epic and Purāṇic Sanskrit. The text gives Mahiṣa’s offence twice: first in the narrator’s summary (2.3), then from the gods’ side as humiliation (अभिभव).

Verse 2.6

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

सूर्येन्द्राग्न्यनिलेन्दूनां यमस्य वरुणस्य च।अन्येषां चाधिकारान् स स्वयमेवाधितिष्ठति॥⁠६⁠॥

sūryendrāgnyanilendūnāṃ yamasya varuṇasya ca |anyeṣāṃ cādhikārān sa svayamevādhitiṣṭhati ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सूर्येन्द्राग्न्यनिलेन्दूनाम् · यमस्य · वरुणस्य · चअन्येषाम् · च · अधिकारान् · सः · स्वयम् · एव · अधितिष्ठति

sūryendrāgnyanilendūnām · yamasya · varuṇasya · caanyeṣām · ca · adhikārān · saḥ · svayam · eva · adhitiṣṭhati

Word by word

  1. सूर्येन्द्राग्न्यनिलेन्दूनाम्sūryendrāgnyanilendūnāmof the Sun, Indra, Agni (Fire), Anila (Wind) and the Moonm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on अधिकारान्
  2. यमस्यyamasyaof Yamam. gen. sg. of यम
  3. वरुणस्यvaruṇasyaof Varuṇam. gen. sg. of वरुण
  4. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  5. अन्येषाम्anyeṣāmof the otherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. gen. pl.
  6. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  7. अधिकारान्adhikārānoffices, rightful functionsm. acc. pl. of अधिकार (अधि + √कृ + घञ्); object of अधितिष्ठति
  8. सःsaḥhe (Mahiṣa)pronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; subject
  9. स्वयम्svayamhimselfindeclinable (reflexive adverb)
  10. एवevaalone, by himself (emphatic)indeclinable (emphatic particle)
  11. अधितिष्ठतिadhitiṣṭhatioccupies, presides overअधि + √स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’; present stem तिष्ठ-), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

Translation

Of the Sun, Indra, Fire, Wind and Moon, of Yama and Varuṇa, and of the others, the offices he himself alone occupies.

In readable EnglishHe has seized for himself the offices of the Sun, Indra, Fire, Wind and Moon, of Yama and Varuṇa, and of the other gods besides.

tr puts the verb first and renders the present ‘occupies’ as ‘has seized’, which is its sense here.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सूर्येन्द्राग्न्यनिलेन्दूनां sūryendrāgnyanilendūnāṃ → सूर्य + इन्द्र + अग्नि + अनिल + इन्दूनाम्

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa, savarṇa-dīrgha, yaṇ) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    a + i → e (सूर्येन्द्र, अनिलेन्दु); a + a → ā (इन्द्राग्नि); i before a vowel → y (अग्न्यनिल). The final m becomes anusvāra before y (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • चाधिकारान् cādhikārān → च + अधिकारान्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • स स्वयमेवाधितिष्ठति sa svayamevādhitiṣṭhati → सः + स्वयम् + एव + अधितिष्ठति

    visarga sandhi (special rule for सः); vowel sandhi · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    सः drops its visarga before a consonant. स्वयम् is only written together with एव; एव + अधितिष्ठति: a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सूर्येन्द्राग्न्यनिलेन्दूनाम् sūryendrāgnyanilendūnām

    dvandva (itaretara)

    Members
    सूर्य + इन्द्र + अग्नि + अनिल + इन्दु
    Vigraha
    सूर्यश्च इन्द्रश्च अग्निश्च अनिलश्च इन्दुश्च, तेषाम् sūryaśca indraśca agniśca anilaśca induśca, teṣām
    Literally
    “of Sun, Indra, Fire, Wind and Moon”
    In the verse
    the great gods whose functions keep the world running
Morphology & anvaya

  • अधितिष्ठति adhitiṣṭhati

    Present of अधि + √स्था, ‘to stand over, occupy’. With अधि the place one occupies is the object, in the accusative (अधिकारान्). The present tense makes the gods’ complaint vivid: he holds the posts now.

  • अधिकारान् adhikārān

    अधिकार (अधि + √कृ + घञ्) is ‘authority, office, function’: the share of the world’s working that belongs to each god.

Anvaya · prose order

सः (महिषः) स्वयम् एव सूर्येन्द्राग्न्यनिलेन्दूनां यमस्य वरुणस्य च अन्येषां च अधिकारान् अधितिष्ठति।

saḥ (mahiṣaḥ) svayam eva sūryendrāgnyanilendūnāṃ yamasya varuṇasya ca anyeṣāṃ ca adhikārān adhitiṣṭhati |

Subject, an emphatic स्वयम् एव, a long genitive list dependent on अधिकारान्, and the verb.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods’ speech to Śiva and Viṣṇu begins here (2.6–8). Mahiṣa has not only taken Indra’s throne (2.3); he has taken over every god’s function himself.

Traditional interpretation

The list reads as an inventory of the cosmos: light (Sun), rule (Indra), sacrifice (Agni), breath (Wind), the waters (Varuṇa), death (Yama), the moon. When one asura holds them all, the order of the world is in the hands of its enemy. The Goddess who answers this will receive gifts from these very gods (2.20–31).

Verse 2.7

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

स्वर्गान्निराकृताः सर्वे तेन देवगणा भुवि।विचरन्ति यथा मर्त्या महिषेण दुरात्मना॥⁠७⁠॥

svargānnirākṛtāḥ sarve tena devagaṇā bhuvi |vicaranti yathā martyā mahiṣeṇa durātmanā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

स्वर्गात् · निराकृताः · सर्वे · तेन · देवगणाः · भुविविचरन्ति · यथा · मर्त्याः · महिषेण · दुरात्मना

svargāt · nirākṛtāḥ · sarve · tena · devagaṇāḥ · bhuvivicaranti · yathā · martyāḥ · mahiṣeṇa · durātmanā

Word by word

  1. स्वर्गात्svargātfrom heavenm. abl. sg. of स्वर्ग; ablative of separation with निराकृताः
  2. निराकृताःnirākṛtāḥdriven out, expelledनिर् + आ + √कृ (tanādi, 8 ‘to do’) + क्त, m. nom. pl.; qualifies देवगणाः
  3. सर्वेsarveallpronominal adjective सर्व, m. nom. pl.; qualifies देवगणाः
  4. तेनtenaby himpronoun तद्, m. instr. sg.; agent of निराकृताः, taken up by महिषेण
  5. देवगणाःdevagaṇāḥthe hosts of godsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of विचरन्ति
  6. भुविbhuvion the earthf. loc. sg. of भू (ū-stem)
  7. विचरन्तिvicarantiwander aboutवि + √चर् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to move’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  8. यथाyathālike, just asindeclinable (particle of comparison)
  9. मर्त्याःmartyāḥmortalsm. nom. pl. of मर्त्य
  10. महिषेणmahiṣeṇaby Mahiṣam. instr. sg. of महिष; in apposition to तेन
  11. दुरात्मनाdurātmanāthe evil-mindedm. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies महिषेण

Translation

Driven out of heaven by him, by the evil-minded Mahiṣa, all the hosts of gods wander on the earth like mortals.

In readable EnglishCast out of heaven by that wicked Mahiṣa, all the gods now wander the earth like mortal men.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • स्वर्गान्निराकृताः svargānnirākṛtāḥ → स्वर्गात् + निराकृताः

    consonant sandhi (nasal) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    Final t before the nasal n becomes n.

  • देवगणा भुवि devagaṇā bhuvi → देवगणाः + भुवि

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced sound becomes -ā (the y from the visarga is dropped before a consonant). The same change gives मर्त्या महिषेण.

  • दुरात्मना durātmanā → दुस् + आत्मना

    visarga sandhi inside a compound · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    The prefix दुस् ‘bad’ becomes दुर् before the vowel ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • देवगणाः devagaṇāḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    देव + गण
    Vigraha
    देवानां गणाः devānāṃ gaṇāḥ
    Literally
    “the hosts of the gods”
    In the verse
    the gods in all their companies

  • दुरात्मना durātmanā

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    दुस् + आत्मन्
    Vigraha
    दुष्टः आत्मा यस्य सः, तेन duṣṭaḥ ātmā yasya saḥ, tena
    Literally
    “he whose self is wicked”
    In the verse
    the evil-hearted one
Morphology & anvaya

  • निराकृताः nirākṛtāḥ

    Past passive participle of निर् + आ + √कृ ‘to put away, repudiate’: ‘cast out’. Its agent stands in the instrumental, तेन … महिषेण दुरात्मना.

  • भुवि bhuvi

    Locative of the root-noun भू ‘earth’: before vowel endings ū becomes uv (भुवि, भुवः).

Anvaya · prose order

तेन दुरात्मना महिषेण स्वर्गात् निराकृताः सर्वे देवगणाः भुवि मर्त्याः यथा विचरन्ति।

tena durātmanā mahiṣeṇa svargāt nirākṛtāḥ sarve devagaṇāḥ bhuvi martyāḥ yathā vicaranti |

Subject (सर्वे देवगणाः) with a participial phrase (स्वर्गात् निराकृताः, agent तेन … महिषेण दुरात्मना), then the verb with a comparison (मर्त्याः यथा).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The second part of the complaint: the gods are exiles, wandering the earth like humans. The gods’ condition now matches Suratha’s, who also lost his kingdom and wandered alone (1.6–11).

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the text lets even the gods lose their place. Being dispossessed is not a mark of unworthiness here; it is the condition from which help is sought.

Verse 2.8

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

एतद्वः कथितं सर्वममरारिविचेष्टितम्।शरणं वः प्रपन्नाः स्मो वधस्तस्य विचिन्त्यताम्॥⁠८⁠॥

etadvaḥ kathitaṃ sarvamamarāriviceṣṭitam |śaraṇaṃ vaḥ prapannāḥ smo vadhastasya vicintyatām ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

एतत् · वः · कथितम् · सर्वम् · अमरारिविचेष्टितम्शरणम् · वः · प्रपन्नाः · स्मः · वधः · तस्य · विचिन्त्यताम्

etat · vaḥ · kathitam · sarvam · amarāriviceṣṭitamśaraṇam · vaḥ · prapannāḥ · smaḥ · vadhaḥ · tasya · vicintyatām

Word by word

  1. एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies अमरारिविचेष्टितम्
  2. वःvaḥto youpronoun युष्मद्, enclitic form, dat. pl.
  3. कथितम्kathitamhas been told√कथ् (curādi, 10 ‘to tell’) + क्त, n. nom. sg.; predicate
  4. सर्वम्sarvamall, the wholepronominal adjective सर्व, n. nom. sg.
  5. अमरारिविचेष्टितम्amarāriviceṣṭitamthe doings of the enemy of the immortalsn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of the passive
  6. शरणम्śaraṇamrefuge, protectionn. acc. sg. of शरण; object of प्रपन्नाः
  7. वःvaḥto youpronoun युष्मद्, enclitic form, acc. pl.; the person approached
  8. प्रपन्नाःprapannāḥhave come (for)प्र + √पद् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to go’) + क्त, m. nom. pl.; active sense
  9. स्मःsmaḥwe are√अस् (adādi, 2P ‘to be’), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  10. वधःvadhaḥthe killing, the deathm. nom. sg. of वध (√हन् + अप्, वध substitute); subject of विचिन्त्यताम्
  11. तस्यtasyahispronoun तद्, m. gen. sg.; objective genitive with वधः
  12. विचिन्त्यताम्vicintyatāmlet (it) be devisedवि + √चिन्त् (curādi, 10 ‘to think’), karmaṇi prayoga, loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana

Translation

This, all the doings of the enemy of the immortals, has been told to you. We have come to you for refuge; let his killing be devised.

In readable EnglishWe have told you all that the enemy of the gods has done. We have come to you for refuge: find a way to destroy him.

tr turns the two passives into active English.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • एतद्वः etadvaḥ → एतत् + वः

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before the voiced v.

  • शरणं वः śaraṇaṃ vaḥ → शरणम् + वः

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra. The same happens in कथितं सर्वम्.

  • स्मो वधस्तस्य smo vadhastasya → स्मः + वधः + तस्य

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    -aḥ before the voiced v becomes -o; the visarga of वधः before t becomes s.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अमरारिविचेष्टितम् amarāriviceṣṭitam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (outer), containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (अमरारि)

    Members
    अमरारि + विचेष्टित
    Vigraha
    अमराणाम् अरिः अमरारिः; तस्य विचेष्टितम् amarāṇām ariḥ amarāriḥ; tasya viceṣṭitam
    Literally
    “the doing of the enemy of the deathless”
    In the verse
    everything Mahiṣa has done

  • अमर amara

    nañ tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अ (न) + मर
    Vigraha
    न मरः na maraḥ
    Literally
    “not mortal”
    In the verse
    the gods, the deathless ones
Morphology & anvaya

  • वः vaḥ

    The enclitic वः stands for युष्मद् in the accusative, dative or genitive plural. The first वः goes with कथितम् (‘told to you’); the second is the person whose shelter is sought. The gods address two (Śiva and Viṣṇu, the dual of 2.4) in the plural; this is either the plural of respect or includes Brahmā, who leads them.

  • प्रपन्नाः prapannāḥ

    Past participle of प्र + √पद् with active sense (a verb of motion): ‘having approached’. शरणं प्रपद् is the fixed idiom ‘to go to (someone) for refuge’, with the person in the accusative.

  • विचिन्त्यताम् vicintyatām

    Passive imperative (third person): ‘let his death be thought out’. A polite way to put a request to superiors.

Anvaya · prose order

एतत् सर्वम् अमरारिविचेष्टितं वः कथितम्। वः शरणं प्रपन्नाः स्मः। तस्य वधः विचिन्त्यताम्।

etat sarvam amarāriviceṣṭitaṃ vaḥ kathitam | vaḥ śaraṇaṃ prapannāḥ smaḥ | tasya vadhaḥ vicintyatām |

Three short clauses: a passive statement (कथितम्), an active one (प्रपन्नाः स्मः) and a passive request (विचिन्त्यताम्).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods end their report and ask for help: they take refuge with the high gods and ask them to plan Mahiṣa’s death. They do not ask the gods to fight; they ask them to think (विचिन्त्यताम्). What answers is not a plan but anger, and from the anger the Goddess comes (2.9–13).

Traditional interpretation

शरणं प्रपद् ‘to go for refuge’ is the language of surrender (prapatti). Here the gods take refuge with Śiva and Viṣṇu; later the same language is turned to the Goddess herself: she is शरण्या ‘the refuge’ in the Nārāyaṇī hymn (11.10, 11.12), and Medhas ends by telling the king to go to her for refuge (13.4, तामुपैहि महाराज शरणं परमेश्वरीम्).

Verse 2.9

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

इत्थं निशम्य देवानां वचांसि मधुसूदनः।चकार कोपं शम्भुश्च भ्रुकुटीकुटिलाननौ॥⁠९⁠॥

itthaṃ niśamya devānāṃ vacāṃsi madhusūdanaḥ |cakāra kopaṃ śambhuśca bhrukuṭīkuṭilānanau ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

इत्थम् · निशम्य · देवानाम् · वचांसि · मधुसूदनःचकार · कोपम् · शम्भुः · च · भ्रुकुटीकुटिलाननौ

ittham · niśamya · devānām · vacāṃsi · madhusūdanaḥcakāra · kopam · śambhuḥ · ca · bhrukuṭīkuṭilānanau

Word by word

  1. इत्थम्itthamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  2. निशम्यniśamyahaving heardनि + √शम् (divādi, 4P) + ल्यप् (absolutive with a prefix)
  3. देवानाम्devānāmof the godsm. gen. pl. of देव; depends on वचांसि
  4. वचांसिvacāṃsithe wordsn. acc. pl. of वचस् (s-stem); object of निशम्य
  5. मधुसूदनःmadhusūdanaḥMadhusūdana, slayer of Madhu (Viṣṇu)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of चकार
  6. चकारcakāramade (anger), i.e. grew (angry)√कृ (tanādi, 8 ‘to do’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. कोपम्kopamangerm. acc. sg. of कोप; object of चकार
  8. शम्भुःśambhuḥŚambhu (Śiva)m. nom. sg. of शम्भु; second subject of चकार
  9. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  10. भ्रुकुटीकुटिलाननौbhrukuṭīkuṭilānanauboth with faces twisted by frowning browsm. nom. dual; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मधुसूदनः and शम्भुः together

Translation

Having heard thus the words of the gods, Madhusūdana grew angry, and Śambhu, both with faces twisted by frowning brows.

In readable EnglishHearing the gods speak in this way, Viṣṇu and Śiva grew angry, their faces dark with frowning brows.

tr uses the names Viṣṇu and Śiva for Madhusūdana and Śambhu.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • इत्थं निशम्य itthaṃ niśamya → इत्थम् + निशम्य

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; likewise देवानां वचांसि and कोपं शम्भुः.

  • शम्भुश्च śambhuśca → शम्भुः + च

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś.

  • भ्रुकुटीकुटिलाननौ bhrukuṭīkuṭilānanau → भ्रुकुटी + कुटिल + आननौ

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    कुटिल + आनन: a + ā → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • मधुसूदनः madhusūdanaḥ

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    मधु + सूदन
    Vigraha
    मधुं सूदयति इति madhuṃ sūdayati iti
    Literally
    “he who slays Madhu”
    In the verse
    Viṣṇu, named for his victory in the first carita (1.92–103)

  • भ्रुकुटीकुटिलाननौ bhrukuṭīkuṭilānanau

    bahuvrīhi containing a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (भ्रुकुटीकुटिल: भ्रुकुट्या कुटिलम्)

    Members
    भ्रुकुटीकुटिल + आनन
    Vigraha
    भ्रुकुट्या कुटिलम् आननं ययोः तौ bhrukuṭyā kuṭilam ānanaṃ yayoḥ tau
    Literally
    “the two whose faces are crooked with frowning”
    In the verse
    both scowling in anger
Morphology & anvaya

  • चकार cakāra

    Perfect of √कृ (reduplicated च-कार). कोपं चकार ‘made anger’ is the idiom for ‘grew angry’. The verb is singular with two subjects joined by च: it agrees with the nearer subject, while the adjective भ्रुकुटीकुटिलाननौ is dual and covers both.

  • निशम्य niśamya

    Absolutive of नि + √शम्, ‘having perceived, heard’ (compare निशामय in 1.2, from the causative of the same root).

Anvaya · prose order

इत्थं देवानां वचांसि निशम्य मधुसूदनः शम्भुः च भ्रुकुटीकुटिलाननौ (सन्तौ) कोपं चकार।

itthaṃ devānāṃ vacāṃsi niśamya madhusūdanaḥ śambhuḥ ca bhrukuṭīkuṭilānanau (santau) kopaṃ cakāra |

An absolutive phrase (इत्थं … निशम्य), two subjects with one dual adjective, and the verb with its object.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The response to the gods’ appeal is anger, not counsel. Viṣṇu is called Madhusūdana, a reminder of his victory over Madhu in the first carita, a victory won only when the Goddess released him from sleep.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the text makes anger the starting point of the manifestation. It is the anger of the powerful at the suffering of the worlds, and the Goddess who takes form from it is the answer to that suffering.

Verse 2.10

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ततोऽतिकोपपूर्णस्य चक्रिणो वदनात्ततः।निश्चक्राम महत्तेजो ब्रह्मणः शंकरस्य च॥⁠१०⁠॥

tato'tikopapūrṇasya cakriṇo vadanāttataḥ |niścakrāma mahattejo brahmaṇaḥ śaṃkarasya ca ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ततः · अतिकोपपूर्णस्य · चक्रिणः · वदनात् · ततःनिश्चक्राम · महत् · तेजः · ब्रह्मणः · शंकरस्य · च

tataḥ · atikopapūrṇasya · cakriṇaḥ · vadanāt · tataḥniścakrāma · mahat · tejaḥ · brahmaṇaḥ · śaṃkarasya · ca

Word by word

  1. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time, taddhita तसिल् from तद्)
  2. अतिकोपपूर्णस्यatikopapūrṇasyafilled with intense angerm. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies चक्रिणः
  3. चक्रिणःcakriṇaḥof the discus-bearer (Viṣṇu)m. gen. sg. of चक्रिन् (चक्र + taddhita इनि ‘possessing’); depends on वदनात्
  4. वदनात्vadanātfrom the face, from the mouthn. abl. sg. of वदन; ablative of source
  5. ततःtataḥfrom there, thenceindeclinable (taddhita तसिल्, ablative sense); resumes वदनात्
  6. निश्चक्रामniścakrāmacame forthनिस् + √क्रम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to step’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. महत्mahatgreatn. nom. sg. of महत्; qualifies तेजः
  8. तेजःtejaḥradiance, fiery energy (tejas)n. nom. sg. of तेजस् (s-stem); subject of निश्चक्राम
  9. ब्रह्मणःbrahmaṇaḥof Brahmām. gen. sg. of ब्रह्मन्; with वदनात् understood
  10. शंकरस्यśaṃkarasyaof Śaṅkara (Śiva)m. gen. sg. of शंकर; with वदनात् understood
  11. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)

Translation

Then, from the face of the discus-bearer, filled with intense anger, from there, came forth a great radiance, and from Brahmā and Śaṅkara.

In readable EnglishThen from the face of Viṣṇu, the bearer of the discus, burning with anger, a great radiance came forth, and from the faces of Brahmā and Śiva as well.

The second ततः is absorbed into ‘from’; ‘the faces’ is supplied for Brahmā and Śiva.

Textual verification / uncertain: The second ततः (end of the first line) is read as ‘from there’, reinforcing वदनात्. It can also be taken as a resumptive ‘then’, filling the line; the sense is the same.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ततोऽतिकोप tato'tikopa → ततः + अतिकोप(पूर्णस्य)

    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a: the visarga becomes u, a + u → o, and the following a is elided (avagraha).

  • चक्रिणो वदनात् cakriṇo vadanāt → चक्रिणः + वदनात्

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced v becomes -o; likewise तेजो ब्रह्मणः.

  • निश्चक्राम niścakrāma → निस् + चक्राम

    consonant sandhi at the prefix (palatalisation) · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    The s of the prefix निस् becomes ś next to c.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अतिकोपपूर्णस्य atikopapūrṇasya

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अतिकोप + पूर्ण
    Vigraha
    अतिकोपेन पूर्णः, तस्य atikopena pūrṇaḥ, tasya
    Literally
    “full with excessive anger”
    In the verse
    overflowing with wrath

  • अतिकोप atikopa

    karmadhāraya (with the prefix अति ‘beyond measure’)

    Members
    अति + कोप
    Vigraha
    अतिशयितः कोपः atiśayitaḥ kopaḥ
    Literally
    “anger beyond measure”
    In the verse
    intense anger
Morphology & anvaya

  • निश्चक्राम niścakrāma

    Perfect of निस् + √क्रम्: reduplicated च-क्राम (k → c in the reduplication). ‘Stepped out, issued forth’, of the tejas leaving the face.

  • ततः tataḥ

    The verse uses ततः twice. The first is ‘then’; the second, after वदनात्, is best taken in its ablative sense, ‘from there’, reinforcing the source. See Uncertain.

  • चक्रिणः cakriṇaḥ

    Genitive of चक्रिन् ‘one who has the discus’: the in-stem with the possessive taddhita -इन्.

Anvaya · prose order

ततः अतिकोपपूर्णस्य चक्रिणः वदनात् ततः ब्रह्मणः शंकरस्य च (वदनात्) महत् तेजः निश्चक्राम।

tataḥ atikopapūrṇasya cakriṇaḥ vadanāt tataḥ brahmaṇaḥ śaṃkarasya ca (vadanāt) mahat tejaḥ niścakrāma |

The subject महत् तेजः and the verb निश्चक्राम, with the source in the ablative (वदनात्) and three possessors in the genitive.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The tejas of the three great gods comes first, out of their faces, in their anger. Viṣṇu is named first and his anger is stressed (अतिकोप).

Traditional interpretation

Tejas is more than light: it is the fiery power and splendour that belongs to a god. Each god’s share of it is given a part of the Goddess’s body in 2.14–18, beginning with Śiva’s (the face) and Viṣṇu’s (the arms).

Verse 2.11

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

अन्येषां चैव देवानां शक्रादीनां शरीरतः।निर्गतं सुमहत्तेजस्तच्चैक्यं समगच्छत॥⁠११⁠॥

anyeṣāṃ caiva devānāṃ śakrādīnāṃ śarīrataḥ |nirgataṃ sumahattejastaccaikyaṃ samagacchata ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

अन्येषाम् · च · एव · देवानाम् · शक्रादीनाम् · शरीरतःनिर्गतम् · सुमहत् · तेजः · तत् · च · ऐक्यम् · समगच्छत

anyeṣām · ca · eva · devānām · śakrādīnām · śarīrataḥnirgatam · sumahat · tejaḥ · tat · ca · aikyam · samagacchata

Word by word

  1. अन्येषाम्anyeṣāmof the otherpronominal adjective अन्य, m. gen. pl.; qualifies देवानाम्
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  3. एवevaalso, indeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  4. देवानाम्devānāmgodsm. gen. pl. of देव; depends on शरीरतः
  5. शक्रादीनाम्śakrādīnāmŚakra (Indra) and the restm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to देवानाम्
  6. शरीरतःśarīrataḥfrom the bodiesindeclinable; शरीर + taddhita तसिल् with ablative sense
  7. निर्गतम्nirgatamcame outनिर् + √गम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to go’) + क्त, n. nom. sg.; qualifies तेजः, used as the verb
  8. सुमहत्sumahatvery greatn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तेजः
  9. तेजःtejaḥradiance, fiery energy (tejas)n. nom. sg. of तेजस्; subject
  10. तत्tatthat (radiance)pronoun तद्, n. nom. sg.; subject of समगच्छत
  11. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  12. ऐक्यम्aikyamoneness, unityn. acc. sg. of ऐक्य (एक + taddhita ष्यञ् ‘state of being one’); goal of समगच्छत
  13. समगच्छतsamagacchatacame together intoसम् + √गम् (bhvādi, 1 ‘to go’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada

Translation

And from the bodies of the other gods too, Śakra and the rest, came out a very great radiance; and that came together into oneness.

In readable EnglishFrom the bodies of the other gods as well, Indra and the rest, a great radiance poured forth, and all of it came together and became one.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • चैव caiva → च + एव

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    a + e → ai.

  • तेजस्तच्चैक्यं tejastaccaikyaṃ → तेजः + तत् + च + ऐक्यम्

    visarga sandhi; consonant sandhi (palatalisation); vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    Visarga before t becomes s; the t of तत् becomes c next to c (तच्च); च + ऐक्यम्: a + ai → ai. The final m becomes anusvāra before s (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शक्रादीनाम् śakrādīnām

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    शक्र + आदि
    Vigraha
    शक्रः आदिः येषां ते, तेषाम् śakraḥ ādiḥ yeṣāṃ te, teṣām
    Literally
    “those of whom Śakra is the first”
    In the verse
    Indra and all the other gods

  • सुमहत् sumahat

    tatpuruṣa with the prefix सु (prādi type)

    Members
    सु + महत्
    Vigraha
    सुष्ठु महत् suṣṭhu mahat
    Literally
    “well great”
    In the verse
    exceedingly great
Morphology & anvaya

  • समगच्छत samagacchata

    Imperfect of सम् + √गम् with augment a- (सम्-अ-गच्छ-त). With सम्, √गम् takes ātmanepada endings in the sense ‘to come together’. ऐक्यं समगच्छत: ‘went into oneness’, i.e. merged.

  • शरीरतः śarīrataḥ

    The taddhita -तः (तसिल्) gives an ablative sense: ‘from the body’. The singular is distributive: from the body of each god.

Anvaya · prose order

अन्येषां च एव शक्रादीनां देवानां शरीरतः सुमहत् तेजः निर्गतम्। तत् च ऐक्यं समगच्छत।

anyeṣāṃ ca eva śakrādīnāṃ devānāṃ śarīrataḥ sumahat tejaḥ nirgatam | tat ca aikyaṃ samagacchata |

Two clauses: the tejas came out (निर्गतम्, participle as verb), and it merged (समगच्छत).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

After the three great gods, all the others give their tejas, and it all merges. The Goddess will not be the tejas of any one god but of all of them together.

Traditional interpretation

The gods’ hymn in chapter 4 says this outright: the Goddess’s form is the gathered power of all the hosts of gods (4.3, निश्शेषदेवगणशक्तिसमूहमूर्त्या). The tradition reads this as the unity of the divine powers, which the gods could not achieve as separate beings but she embodies.

Verse 2.12

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

अतीव तेजसः कूटं ज्वलन्तमिव पर्वतम्।ददृशुस्ते सुरास्तत्र ज्वालाव्याप्तदिगन्तरम्॥⁠१२⁠॥

atīva tejasaḥ kūṭaṃ jvalantamiva parvatam |dadṛśuste surāstatra jvālāvyāptadigantaram ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

अतीव · तेजसः · कूटम् · ज्वलन्तम् · इव · पर्वतम्ददृशुः · ते · सुराः · तत्र · ज्वालाव्याप्तदिगन्तरम्

atīva · tejasaḥ · kūṭam · jvalantam · iva · parvatamdadṛśuḥ · te · surāḥ · tatra · jvālāvyāptadigantaram

Word by word

  1. अतीवatīvaexceedingly, immenseindeclinable (adverb of degree; अति + इव, a fixed form)
  2. तेजसःtejasaḥof radiancen. gen. sg. of तेजस्; depends on कूटम्
  3. कूटम्kūṭama mass, a peakn. acc. sg. of कूट; object of ददृशुः
  4. ज्वलन्तम्jvalantamblazing√ज्वल् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to blaze’) + शतृ (present participle), m. acc. sg.; qualifies पर्वतम्
  5. इवivalikeindeclinable (particle of comparison)
  6. पर्वतम्parvatama mountainm. acc. sg. of पर्वत; standard of comparison
  7. ददृशुःdadṛśuḥsaw√दृश् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to see’; पश्य- in the present), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  8. तेtethosepronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; qualifies सुराः
  9. सुराःsurāḥgodsm. nom. pl. of सुर; subject of ददृशुः
  10. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
  11. ज्वालाव्याप्तदिगन्तरम्jvālāvyāptadigantaramwhose flames filled the space between the quartersn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies कूटम्

Translation

Those gods saw there an immense mass of radiance, like a blazing mountain, whose flames filled the space of the quarters.

In readable EnglishThere the gods saw a vast mass of light like a mountain on fire, its flames filling every direction.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • अतीव atīva → अति + इव

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha), lexicalised · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    i + i → ī. The combination is a fixed adverb, ‘exceedingly’, and stays one word in the padaccheda.

  • ददृशुस्ते सुरास्तत्र dadṛśuste surāstatra → ददृशुः + ते + सुराः + तत्र

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s, twice.

  • दिगन्तरम् digantaram → दिक् (दिश्) + अन्तरम्

    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    दिश् at the end of a member becomes दिक्, and k is voiced to g before the vowel a.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • ज्वालाव्याप्तदिगन्तरम् jvālāvyāptadigantaram

    bahuvrīhi containing a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (ज्वालाव्याप्त) and a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (दिगन्तर)

    Members
    ज्वालाव्याप्त + दिगन्तर
    Vigraha
    ज्वालाभिः व्याप्तं दिगन्तरं येन तत् jvālābhiḥ vyāptaṃ digantaraṃ yena tat
    Literally
    “that by which the space of the quarters is pervaded with flames”
    In the verse
    filling every direction with its flames
    Alternative
    Grammatically it could also qualify पर्वतम् (m. acc. sg. has the same ending); it is the mass of tejas, not the mountain of the simile, that the gods see filling the quarters.

  • दिगन्तर digantara

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    दिश् + अन्तर
    Vigraha
    दिशाम् अन्तरम् diśām antaram
    Literally
    “the space between the directions”
    In the verse
    all of space
Morphology & anvaya

  • ज्वलन्तम् jvalantam

    Present participle of √ज्वल्, accusative singular masculine, agreeing with पर्वतम्: ‘a mountain on fire’.

  • ददृशुः dadṛśuḥ

    Perfect of √दृश् (reduplicated द-दृश्), third person plural: ‘they saw’.

Anvaya · prose order

ते सुराः तत्र ज्वलन्तं पर्वतम् इव अतीव तेजसः कूटं ज्वालाव्याप्तदिगन्तरं ददृशुः।

te surāḥ tatra jvalantaṃ parvatam iva atīva tejasaḥ kūṭaṃ jvālāvyāptadigantaraṃ dadṛśuḥ |

Subject (ते सुराः), verb (ददृशुः), and an object (तेजसः कूटम्) with a simile and a bahuvrīhi describing it.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Before it takes a form, the merged tejas is seen as a burning mountain that fills space. The gods are spectators of what they have given.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: what the gods could not do apart becomes visible when their powers are pooled. The image is of overwhelming, undirected force, waiting for a form.

Verse 2.13

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

अतुलं तत्र तत्तेजः सर्वदेवशरीरजम्।एकस्थं तदभून्नारी व्याप्तलोकत्रयं त्विषा॥⁠१३⁠॥

atulaṃ tatra tattejaḥ sarvadevaśarīrajam |ekasthaṃ tadabhūnnārī vyāptalokatrayaṃ tviṣā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

अतुलम् · तत्र · तत् · तेजः · सर्वदेवशरीरजम्एकस्थम् · तत् · अभूत् · नारी · व्याप्तलोकत्रयम् · त्विषा

atulam · tatra · tat · tejaḥ · sarvadevaśarīrajamekastham · tat · abhūt · nārī · vyāptalokatrayam · tviṣā

Word by word

  1. अतुलम्atulammatchlessn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तेजः
  2. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
  3. तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies तेजः
  4. तेजःtejaḥradiance (tejas)n. nom. sg. of तेजस्; subject of अभूत्
  5. सर्वदेवशरीरजम्sarvadevaśarīrajamborn from the bodies of all the godsn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तेजः
  6. एकस्थम्ekasthamgathered in one placen. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तत् (तेजः)
  7. तत्tatitpronoun तद्, n. nom. sg.; resumes तेजः as subject of अभूत्
  8. अभूत्abhūtbecame√भू (bhvādi, 1P), luṅ (aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. नारीnārīa womanf. nom. sg. of नारी; predicate nominative with अभूत्
  10. व्याप्तलोकत्रयम्vyāptalokatrayamwhich pervaded the three worldsn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तत् (तेजः)
  11. त्विषाtviṣāwith its brilliancef. instr. sg. of त्विष् (consonant stem); instrument of व्याप्त-

Translation

There that matchless radiance, born from the bodies of all the gods, gathered into one, which had pervaded the three worlds with its brilliance, became a woman.

In readable EnglishThere that matchless radiance, born from the bodies of all the gods, gathered into one place and, filling the three worlds with its light, became a woman.

tr keeps व्याप्तलोकत्रयम् with the radiance, as the neuter requires, but turns it into a participial phrase.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तदभून्नारी tadabhūnnārī → तत् + अभूत् + नारी

    consonant sandhi (voicing; nasal) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    Final t is voiced to d before the vowel a; the final t of अभूत् becomes n before the nasal n.

  • व्याप्तलोकत्रयं त्विषा vyāptalokatrayaṃ tviṣā → व्याप्तलोकत्रयम् + त्विषा

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; likewise अतुलं तत्र and एकस्थं तत्.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अतुलम् atulam

    bahuvrīhi (negative, with न)

    Members
    अ (न) + तुला
    Vigraha
    न विद्यते तुला यस्य तत् na vidyate tulā yasya tat
    Literally
    “that which has no equal-weight”
    In the verse
    incomparable; the final ā of तुला is shortened in the compound

  • सर्वदेवशरीरजम् sarvadevaśarīrajam

    upapada tatpuruṣa (ज from √जन् + ड); inside it सर्वदेवशरीर is a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (सर्वदेवानां शरीराणि), and सर्वदेव a karmadhāraya (सर्वे देवाः)

    Members
    सर्वदेवशरीर + ज
    Vigraha
    सर्वदेवशरीरेभ्यः जातम् sarvadevaśarīrebhyaḥ jātam
    Literally
    “born from the bodies of all the gods”
    In the verse
    drawn from every god at once

  • एकस्थम् ekastham

    upapada tatpuruṣa (स्थ from √स्था + क)

    Members
    एक + स्थ
    Vigraha
    एकस्मिन् तिष्ठति इति ekasmin tiṣṭhati iti
    Literally
    “standing in one”
    In the verse
    concentrated, gathered into one place

  • व्याप्तलोकत्रयम् vyāptalokatrayam

    bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (लोकत्रय: लोकानां त्रयम्)

    Members
    व्याप्त + लोकत्रय
    Vigraha
    व्याप्तं लोकत्रयं येन तत् vyāptaṃ lokatrayaṃ yena tat
    Literally
    “that by which the triad of worlds is pervaded”
    In the verse
    filling the three worlds
Morphology & anvaya

  • व्याप्तलोकत्रयम् vyāptalokatrayam

    Neuter, so it qualifies तत् तेजः, not नारी (a feminine would be व्याप्तलोकत्रया, as the Goddess herself is called in 2.37, व्याप्तलोकत्रयां त्विषा). The radiance that already filled the three worlds became a woman.

  • अभूत् abhūt

    Aorist of √भू with a predicate nominative: तत् … नारी अभूत्, ‘it became a woman’. The neuter subject and the feminine predicate stand side by side: the transformation is in the grammar.

Anvaya · prose order

तत्र सर्वदेवशरीरजम् अतुलं तत् तेजः एकस्थं (सत्), त्विषा व्याप्तलोकत्रयं तत् नारी अभूत्।

tatra sarvadevaśarīrajam atulaṃ tat tejaḥ ekasthaṃ (sat), tviṣā vyāptalokatrayaṃ tat nārī abhūt |

A neuter subject (तत् तेजः, resumed by the second तत्) with its qualifiers, a feminine predicate (नारी) and the verb अभूत्.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The central moment of the chapter: the combined tejas becomes a woman. The verse is carefully neuter until the last noun: it is the radiance (तेजः, n.) that is matchless, gathered and world-filling, and that radiance ‘became a woman’ (नारी अभूत्).

Traditional interpretation

Medhas has already said that the Goddess is eternal and is called ‘born’ only when she becomes manifest for the gods’ purpose (1.64–66: देवानां कार्यसिद्ध्यर्थमाविर्भवति सा यदा). The tradition therefore reads this verse as a manifestation, not a creation: the gods’ tejas gives her a visible form; it does not bring her into being.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) opens with a similar emergence: name 4, चिदग्निकुण्डसम्भूता, ‘she who arose from the fire-pit of consciousness’, and name 5, देवकार्यसमुद्यता, ‘she who is intent on the gods’ purpose’. There she rises from a sacrificial fire, here from the gods’ combined tejas; the two accounts are distinct.

Verse 2.14

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

यदभूच्छाम्भवं तेजस्तेनाजायत तन्मुखम्।याम्येन चाभवन् केशा बाहवो विष्णुतेजसा॥⁠१४⁠॥

yadabhūcchāmbhavaṃ tejastenājāyata tanmukham |yāmyena cābhavan keśā bāhavo viṣṇutejasā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

यत् · अभूत् · शाम्भवम् · तेजः · तेन · अजायत · तन्मुखम्याम्येन · च · अभवन् · केशाः · बाहवः · विष्णुतेजसा

yat · abhūt · śāmbhavam · tejaḥ · tena · ajāyata · tanmukhamyāmyena · ca · abhavan · keśāḥ · bāhavaḥ · viṣṇutejasā

Word by word

  1. यत्yatwhichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. sg.; correlative तेन
  2. अभूत्abhūtwas√भू (bhvādi, 1P), luṅ (aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  3. शाम्भवम्śāmbhavamof Śambhu (Śiva)n. nom. sg. of शाम्भव (शम्भु + taddhita अण्, with vṛddhi); qualifies तेजः
  4. तेजःtejaḥradiancen. nom. sg. of तेजस्; subject of the relative clause
  5. तेनtenaby thatpronoun तद्, n. instr. sg.; instrument of अजायत
  6. अजायतajāyatawas born, was formed√जन् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to be born’; present stem जाय-), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  7. तन्मुखम्tanmukhamher facen. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of अजायत
  8. याम्येनyāmyenaby that of Yaman. instr. sg. of याम्य (यम + taddhita ण्य ‘belonging to Yama’); with तेजसा understood
  9. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  10. अभवन्abhavancame to be√भू (bhvādi, 1P), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  11. केशाःkeśāḥthe hair (of the head)m. nom. pl. of केश; subject of अभवन्
  12. बाहवःbāhavaḥthe armsm. nom. pl. of बाहु (u-stem); second subject of अभवन्
  13. विष्णुतेजसाviṣṇutejasāby the radiance of Viṣṇun. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

Translation

The radiance that was Śambhu’s — by that her face was formed; and by Yama’s the hair came to be; the arms by the radiance of Viṣṇu.

In readable EnglishFrom Śiva’s radiance her face was formed; from Yama’s, her hair; from Viṣṇu’s, her arms.

tr compresses the relative construction (यत् … तेन) into a simple ‘from’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • यदभूच्छाम्भवं yadabhūcchāmbhavaṃ → यत् + अभूत् + शाम्भवम्

    consonant sandhi (voicing; t + ś → cch) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40), शश्छोऽटि (8.4.63)

    t before a vowel is voiced to d; t before ś becomes c, and the ś after it becomes ch: अभूत् शाम्भवम् → अभूच्छाम्भवम्.

  • तेजस्तेनाजायत tejastenājāyata → तेजः + तेन + अजायत

    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Visarga before t becomes s; a + a → ā.

  • चाभवन् cābhavan → च + अभवन्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • बाहवो विष्णुतेजसा bāhavo viṣṇutejasā → बाहवः + विष्णुतेजसा

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced v becomes -o. Before the voiced b, केशाः loses its visarga (केशा बाहवः).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • तन्मुखम् tanmukham

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    तद् + मुख
    Vigraha
    तस्याः मुखम् tasyāḥ mukham
    Literally
    “the face of her”
    In the verse
    the woman’s face; inside the compound the pronoun appears as तद् whatever its gender, and t becomes n before m (यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा, 8.4.45)
    Alternative
    It could also be read as two words, तत् मुखम् ‘that face’; the compound reading matches तदङ्गुल्यः in 2.16, where the pronoun cannot agree with its noun and must be a compound member.

  • विष्णुतेजसा viṣṇutejasā

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    विष्णु + तेजस्
    Vigraha
    विष्णोः तेजः, तेन viṣṇoḥ tejaḥ, tena
    Literally
    “by the radiance of Viṣṇu”
    In the verse
    from Viṣṇu’s share of the tejas
Morphology & anvaya

  • अजायत ajāyata

    Imperfect of √जन् (stem जाय-, divādi, ātmanepada): ‘was born’. The parts of the body are ‘born’ from each god’s tejas.

  • शाम्भवम् śāmbhavam

    Taddhita of relation with vṛddhi of the first vowel: शम्भु → शाम्भव ‘of Śambhu’. याम्य (from यम) is formed the same way with -य; both stand for ‘the tejas of …’.

Anvaya · prose order

यत् शाम्भवं तेजः अभूत्, तेन तन्मुखम् अजायत। याम्येन (तेजसा) च केशाः अभवन्, विष्णुतेजसा बाहवः (अभवन्)।

yat śāmbhavaṃ tejaḥ abhūt, tena tanmukham ajāyata | yāmyena (tejasā) ca keśāḥ abhavan, viṣṇutejasā bāhavaḥ (abhavan) |

A relative clause (यत् … अभूत्) answered by तेन; then two short clauses with the instrumental of the source.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The second movement begins: each god’s tejas forms one part of the woman’s body (2.14–18). Śiva gives the face, Yama the hair, Viṣṇu the arms.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the pairings invite reflection (the arms that will hold the weapons from Viṣṇu the preserver, the dark hair from Yama, lord of death), but the text itself states only the correspondence and gives no reasons.

Verse 2.15

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

सौम्येन स्तनयोर्युग्मं मध्यं चैन्द्रेण चाभवत्।वारुणेन च जङ्घोरू नितम्बस्तेजसा भुवः॥⁠१५⁠॥

saumyena stanayoryugmaṃ madhyaṃ caindreṇa cābhavat |vāruṇena ca jaṅghorū nitambastejasā bhuvaḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सौम्येन · स्तनयोः · युग्मम् · मध्यम् · च · ऐन्द्रेण · च · अभवत्वारुणेन · च · जङ्घोरू · नितम्बः · तेजसा · भुवः

saumyena · stanayoḥ · yugmam · madhyam · ca · aindreṇa · ca · abhavatvāruṇena · ca · jaṅghorū · nitambaḥ · tejasā · bhuvaḥ

Word by word

  1. सौम्येनsaumyenaby that of Soma (the Moon)n. instr. sg. of सौम्य (सोम + taddhita य, with vṛddhi); with तेजसा understood
  2. स्तनयोःstanayoḥof the breastsm. gen. dual of स्तन; depends on युग्मम्
  3. युग्मम्yugmamthe pairn. nom. sg. of युग्म; subject (अभवत् understood)
  4. मध्यम्madhyamthe waistn. nom. sg. of मध्य; subject of अभवत्
  5. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  6. ऐन्द्रेणaindreṇaby that of Indran. instr. sg. of ऐन्द्र (इन्द्र + taddhita अण्, with vṛddhi); with तेजसा understood
  7. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  8. अभवत्abhavatcame to be√भू (bhvādi, 1P), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. वारुणेनvāruṇenaby that of Varuṇan. instr. sg. of वारुण (वरुण + taddhita अण्); with तेजसा understood
  10. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  11. जङ्घोरूjaṅghorūthe shanks and thighsm. nom. dual; compound (see Samāsa)
  12. नितम्बःnitambaḥthe hipsm. nom. sg. of नितम्ब
  13. तेजसाtejasāby the radiancen. instr. sg. of तेजस्
  14. भुवःbhuvaḥof the Earthf. gen. sg. of भू (ū-stem); depends on तेजसा

Translation

By Soma’s (radiance) the pair of breasts, and by Indra’s the waist came to be; and by Varuṇa’s the shanks and thighs; the hips by the radiance of the Earth.

In readable EnglishFrom the Moon’s radiance her breasts were formed, from Indra’s her waist, from Varuṇa’s her legs, and from the Earth’s radiance her hips.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • स्तनयोर्युग्मं stanayoryugmaṃ → स्तनयोः + युग्मम्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After o the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before the voiced y. The m becomes anusvāra before the next consonant (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • चैन्द्रेण चाभवत् caindreṇa cābhavat → च + ऐन्द्रेण + च + अभवत्

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi; savarṇa-dīrgha) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ai → ai; a + a → ā.

  • जङ्घोरू jaṅghorū → जङ्घा + ऊरू

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    ā + ū → o.

  • नितम्बस्तेजसा nitambastejasā → नितम्बः + तेजसा

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • जङ्घोरू jaṅghorū

    dvandva (itaretara)

    Members
    जङ्घा + ऊरु
    Vigraha
    जङ्घे च ऊरू च jaṅghe ca ūrū ca
    Literally
    “shank(s) and thigh(s)”
    In the verse
    the legs, below and above the knee; the compound takes the gender of its last member (ऊरु, m.)
    Alternative
    Names of body parts are usually joined in a samāhāra dvandva (neuter singular, जङ्घोरु); the text has the dual जङ्घोरू, an itaretara dvandva counting the two kinds of limb.
Morphology & anvaya

  • जङ्घोरू jaṅghorū

    Nominative dual of the itaretara dvandva: two members, so dual, although each limb is itself a pair. Epic usage; the classical rule would prefer the neuter collective.

  • अभवत् abhavat

    Imperfect singular of √भू. It agrees with the nearest subject (मध्यम्), and is understood with the other subjects of the verse.

  • भुवः bhuvaḥ

    Genitive singular of the feminine root-noun भू ‘Earth’ (ū → uv before vowel endings): the Earth as a goddess giving her tejas.

Anvaya · prose order

सौम्येन (तेजसा) स्तनयोः युग्मम् (अभवत्), ऐन्द्रेण च मध्यम् अभवत्, वारुणेन च जङ्घोरू (अभवताम्), भुवः तेजसा नितम्बः (अभवत्)।

saumyena (tejasā) stanayoḥ yugmam (abhavat), aindreṇa ca madhyam abhavat, vāruṇena ca jaṅghorū (abhavatām), bhuvaḥ tejasā nitambaḥ (abhavat) |

Four parallel clauses, each with the source in the instrumental and the body part as subject; अभवत् is stated once and understood in the rest.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The forming of the body continues: breasts from Soma, waist from Indra, legs from Varuṇa, hips from the Earth. The givers include the Earth, a goddess, alongside the gods.

Traditional interpretation

The Purāṇa describes the Goddess’s body plainly, part by part, and the passage is recited as it stands. Its point is that no part of her is the gift of a single deity: she is assembled from the whole pantheon, the Earth included.

Verse 2.16

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ब्रह्मणस्तेजसा पादौ तदङ्गुल्योऽर्कतेजसा।वसूनां च कराङ्गुल्यः कौबेरेण च नासिका॥⁠१६⁠॥

brahmaṇastejasā pādau tadaṅgulyo'rkatejasā |vasūnāṃ ca karāṅgulyaḥ kaubereṇa ca nāsikā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ब्रह्मणः · तेजसा · पादौ · तदङ्गुल्यः · अर्कतेजसावसूनाम् · च · कराङ्गुल्यः · कौबेरेण · च · नासिका

brahmaṇaḥ · tejasā · pādau · tadaṅgulyaḥ · arkatejasāvasūnām · ca · karāṅgulyaḥ · kaubereṇa · ca · nāsikā

Word by word

  1. ब्रह्मणःbrahmaṇaḥof Brahmām. gen. sg. of ब्रह्मन्; depends on तेजसा
  2. तेजसाtejasāby the radiancen. instr. sg. of तेजस्; instrument (source) of the supplied verb
  3. पादौpādauthe two feetm. nom. dual of पाद; subject (अभवताम् understood)
  4. तदङ्गुल्यःtadaṅgulyaḥtheir toes (the digits of those feet)f. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject (अभवन् understood)
  5. अर्कतेजसाarkatejasāby the radiance of Arka (the Sun)n. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  6. वसूनाम्vasūnāmof the Vasusm. gen. pl. of वसु (u-stem), the group of eight gods; with तेजसा understood
  7. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  8. कराङ्गुल्यःkarāṅgulyaḥthe fingersf. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject
  9. कौबेरेणkaubereṇaby that of Kuberan. instr. sg. of कौबेर (कुबेर + taddhita अण्, with vṛddhi); with तेजसा understood
  10. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  11. नासिकाnāsikāthe nosef. nom. sg. of नासिका; subject

Translation

By the radiance of Brahmā the feet, their toes by the radiance of the Sun; and by the Vasus’ the fingers, and by Kubera’s the nose.

In readable EnglishFrom Brahmā’s radiance came her feet, from the Sun’s her toes, from the Vasus’ her fingers, and from Kubera’s her nose.

‘Came’ is supplied; the verse has no verb of its own.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ब्रह्मणस्तेजसा brahmaṇastejasā → ब्रह्मणः + तेजसा

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • तदङ्गुल्योऽर्कतेजसा tadaṅgulyo'rkatejasā → तदङ्गुल्यः + अर्कतेजसा

    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a: o, with the following a elided (avagraha). Inside the compound, तद् + अङ्गुल्यः keeps its voiced d before the vowel (झलां जशोऽन्ते, 8.2.39).

  • कराङ्गुल्यः karāṅgulyaḥ → कर + अङ्गुल्यः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • तदङ्गुल्यः tadaṅgulyaḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    तद् + अङ्गुलि
    Vigraha
    तयोः (पादयोः) अङ्गुल्यः tayoḥ (pādayoḥ) aṅgulyaḥ
    Literally
    “the digits of those (feet)”
    In the verse
    the toes; तद् here points back to पादौ, and must be a compound member, since a separate तत् could not agree with the feminine plural अङ्गुल्यः

  • अर्कतेजसा arkatejasā

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अर्क + तेजस्
    Vigraha
    अर्कस्य तेजः, तेन arkasya tejaḥ, tena
    Literally
    “by the radiance of the Sun”
    In the verse
    from the Sun’s share of the tejas

  • कराङ्गुल्यः karāṅgulyaḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    कर + अङ्गुलि
    Vigraha
    करयोः अङ्गुल्यः karayoḥ aṅgulyaḥ
    Literally
    “the digits of the hands”
    In the verse
    the fingers, set against the toes (तदङ्गुल्यः) in the line above
Morphology & anvaya

  • तदङ्गुल्यः tadaṅgulyaḥ

    Nominative plural of the feminine i-stem अङ्गुलि: i becomes y before the ending -अः (अङ्गुल्-यः). The same form ends कराङ्गुल्यः.

  • वसूनाम् vasūnām

    Genitive plural of the u-stem वसु, with the long ū and n of the ending -नाम्: the Vasus as a group gave the fingers.

Anvaya · prose order

ब्रह्मणः तेजसा पादौ (अभवताम्), अर्कतेजसा तदङ्गुल्यः (अभवन्), वसूनां च (तेजसा) कराङ्गुल्यः, कौबेरेण च (तेजसा) नासिका (अभवत्)।

brahmaṇaḥ tejasā pādau (abhavatām), arkatejasā tadaṅgulyaḥ (abhavan), vasūnāṃ ca (tejasā) karāṅgulyaḥ, kaubereṇa ca (tejasā) nāsikā (abhavat) |

Four nominal clauses with the verb supplied from 2.14–15 (अभवत् / अभवन्); the source is always in the instrumental.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The list continues. Brahmā, the creator, gives the feet; the Sun the toes; the Vasus the fingers; Kubera, lord of wealth, the nose. The verse has no verb: the pattern of 2.14–15 carries it.

Traditional interpretation

Brahmā, who led the gods to Śiva and Viṣṇu (2.4), gives the Goddess her feet. The tradition makes much of the Goddess’s feet as the place of refuge and worship, and here the creator’s own tejas forms them.

Verse 2.17

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तस्यास्तु दन्ताः सम्भूताः प्राजापत्येन तेजसा।नयनत्रितयं जज्ञे तथा पावकतेजसा॥⁠१७⁠॥

tasyāstu dantāḥ sambhūtāḥ prājāpatyena tejasā |nayanatritayaṃ jajñe tathā pāvakatejasā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तस्याः · तु · दन्ताः · सम्भूताः · प्राजापत्येन · तेजसानयनत्रितयम् · जज्ञे · तथा · पावकतेजसा

tasyāḥ · tu · dantāḥ · sambhūtāḥ · prājāpatyena · tejasānayanatritayam · jajñe · tathā · pāvakatejasā

Word by word

  1. तस्याःtasyāḥherpronoun तद्, f. gen. sg.; depends on दन्ताः
  2. तुtuand, moreoverindeclinable (particle of transition)
  3. दन्ताःdantāḥthe teethm. nom. pl. of दन्त; subject
  4. सम्भूताःsambhūtāḥcame into beingसम् + √भू + क्त, m. nom. pl.; used as the verb
  5. प्राजापत्येनprājāpatyenaof Prajāpatin. instr. sg. of प्राजापत्य (प्रजापति + taddhita ण्य, with vṛddhi); qualifies तेजसा
  6. तेजसाtejasāby the radiancen. instr. sg. of तेजस्
  7. नयनत्रितयम्nayanatritayamthe three eyesn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of जज्ञे
  8. जज्ञेjajñewas born√जन् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to be born’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  9. तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  10. पावकतेजसाpāvakatejasāby the radiance of Pāvaka (Fire)n. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

Translation

Her teeth came into being by the radiance of Prajāpati; likewise the triad of eyes was born by the radiance of Fire.

In readable EnglishHer teeth were formed from the radiance of Prajāpati, and her three eyes, likewise, from the radiance of Fire.

Textual verification / uncertain: प्राजापत्येन तेजसा: which Prajāpati is meant is not stated. Since Brahmā’s tejas has already given the feet (2.16), the Prajāpati here is probably another (one of the lords of creatures, such as Dakṣa) or the Prajāpatis as a class; it is translated simply as ‘Prajāpati’. The reading itself is not in doubt.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तस्यास्तु tasyāstu → तस्याः + तु

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • नयनत्रितयं जज्ञे nayanatritayaṃ jajñe → नयनत्रितयम् + जज्ञे

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • नयनत्रितयम् nayanatritayam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    नयन + त्रितय
    Vigraha
    नयनानां त्रितयम् nayanānāṃ tritayam
    Literally
    “the triad of eyes”
    In the verse
    her three eyes; त्रितय is त्रि + the taddhita तयप् ‘a group of three’

  • पावकतेजसा pāvakatejasā

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    पावक + तेजस्
    Vigraha
    पावकस्य तेजः, तेन pāvakasya tejaḥ, tena
    Literally
    “by the radiance of the Purifier”
    In the verse
    from Agni’s share of the tejas
Morphology & anvaya

  • जज्ञे jajñe

    Perfect of √जन्, ātmanepada (ज-ज्ञ्-ए, with loss of the root vowel): ‘was born’. The singular agrees with the collective नयनत्रितयम्.

  • सम्भूताः sambhūtāḥ

    Past participle of सम् + √भू used as a finite verb: ‘(were) produced’.

  • प्राजापत्येन prājāpatyena

    Taddhita of relation from प्रजापति, ‘belonging to Prajāpati’. See Uncertain.

Anvaya · prose order

तस्याः तु दन्ताः प्राजापत्येन तेजसा सम्भूताः। तथा पावकतेजसा नयनत्रितयं जज्ञे।

tasyāḥ tu dantāḥ prājāpatyena tejasā sambhūtāḥ | tathā pāvakatejasā nayanatritayaṃ jajñe |

Two clauses, each with a subject, a verb (participle; perfect) and the source in the instrumental.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning
