The teeth come from Prajāpati’s tejas and the three eyes from Agni’s. The Goddess has three eyes, like Śiva.

Traditional interpretation

Three eyes are a mark the Goddess shares with Śiva, whose third eye is traditionally the eye of fire. Here all three of her eyes are formed from Agni’s tejas.

Verse 2.18

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

भ्रुवौ च संध्ययोस्तेजः श्रवणावनिलस्य च।अन्येषां चैव देवानां सम्भवस्तेजसां शिवा॥⁠१८⁠॥

bhruvau ca saṃdhyayostejaḥ śravaṇāvanilasya ca |anyeṣāṃ caiva devānāṃ sambhavastejasāṃ śivā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

भ्रुवौ · च · संध्ययोः · तेजः · श्रवणौ · अनिलस्य · चअन्येषाम् · च · एव · देवानाम् · सम्भवः · तेजसाम् · शिवा

bhruvau · ca · saṃdhyayoḥ · tejaḥ · śravaṇau · anilasya · caanyeṣām · ca · eva · devānām · sambhavaḥ · tejasām · śivā

Word by word

  1. भ्रुवौbhruvauthe two eyebrowsf. nom. dual of भ्रू (ū-stem)
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  3. संध्ययोःsaṃdhyayoḥof the two twilightsf. gen. dual of संध्या (dawn and dusk, personified); depends on तेजः
  4. तेजःtejaḥ(were) the radiancen. nom. sg. of तेजस्; predicate nominative
  5. श्रवणौśravaṇauthe two earsm. nom. dual of श्रवण
  6. अनिलस्यanilasyaof Anila (the Wind)m. gen. sg. of अनिल; with तेजः understood
  7. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  8. अन्येषाम्anyeṣāmof the otherpronominal adjective अन्य, m. gen. pl.; qualifies देवानाम्
  9. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  10. एवevaalso, indeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  11. देवानाम्devānāmgodsm. gen. pl. of देव; depends on तेजसाम्
  12. सम्भवःsambhavaḥthe coming-into-being, the issuem. nom. sg. of सम्भव (सम् + √भू + अप्); predicate of शिवा
  13. तेजसाम्tejasāmof the radiancesn. gen. pl. of तेजस्; depends on सम्भवः
  14. शिवाśivāŚivā, the auspicious one (the Goddess)f. nom. sg. of शिवा; subject

Translation

And the brows (were) the radiance of the two Twilights, and the ears (that) of the Wind; and Śivā was the issue of the radiances of the other gods as well.

In readable EnglishHer brows were the radiance of the two Twilights, her ears that of the Wind; and so the auspicious Goddess arose from the radiance of all the other gods as well.

tr renders सम्भवः … शिवा as ‘the auspicious Goddess arose from’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • संध्ययोस्तेजः saṃdhyayostejaḥ → संध्ययोः + तेजः

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s; likewise सम्भवस्तेजसाम्.

  • श्रवणावनिलस्य śravaṇāvanilasya → श्रवणौ + अनिलस्य

    vowel sandhi (āv) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)

    Final au before a vowel becomes āv. Here the v is kept (the optional loss of y/v, 8.3.19, applies to the ay/av from e/o, not to āv).

  • चैव caiva → च + एव

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    a + e → ai.

  • तेजसां शिवा tejasāṃ śivā → तेजसाम् + शिवा

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • भ्रुवौ bhruvau

    Nominative dual of the feminine root-noun भ्रू ‘brow’: before vowel endings ū becomes uv (भ्रुव्-औ).

  • तेजः tejaḥ

    The construction changes in this verse: instead of an instrumental of source (तेजसा) the radiance stands in the nominative as a predicate, ‘the brows (were) the twilights’ radiance’.

  • सम्भवः sambhavaḥ

    Action noun from सम् + √भू, ‘coming together, origin’. Predicated of शिवा: ‘Śivā was the issue of the radiances’. The masculine noun and the feminine subject do not need to agree: they are subject and predicate.

Anvaya · prose order

भ्रुवौ च संध्ययोः तेजः, श्रवणौ च अनिलस्य (तेजः)। अन्येषां च एव देवानां तेजसां सम्भवः शिवा (अभूत्)।

bhruvau ca saṃdhyayoḥ tejaḥ, śravaṇau ca anilasya (tejaḥ) | anyeṣāṃ ca eva devānāṃ tejasāṃ sambhavaḥ śivā (abhūt) |

Two nominal sentences of the form ‘X (was) Y’s radiance’, then a summary sentence with शिवा as subject and सम्भवः as predicate.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The description ends with a summary: the rest of the gods contributed too, and the being who arose from all their radiance is called Śivā, ‘the auspicious’. The two Twilights (संध्ये, dawn and dusk) are counted among the givers.

Traditional interpretation

Śivā is the feminine of śiva, ‘auspicious, benign’, and a name of the Goddess as Śiva’s consort. This is the first time the text uses it: the being formed from anger-born radiance is named ‘the auspicious one’.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the same name, शिवा (name 53), and श्रीशिवा (name 998). The name is common to many Śākta texts; the two works are distinct.

Verse 2.19

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ततः समस्तदेवानां तेजोराशिसमुद्भवाम्।तां विलोक्य मुदं प्रापुरमरा महिषार्दिताः॥⁠१९⁠॥

tataḥ samastadevānāṃ tejorāśisamudbhavām |tāṃ vilokya mudaṃ prāpuramarā mahiṣārditāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ततः · समस्तदेवानाम् · तेजोराशिसमुद्भवाम्ताम् · विलोक्य · मुदम् · प्रापुः · अमराः · महिषार्दिताः

tataḥ · samastadevānām · tejorāśisamudbhavāmtām · vilokya · mudam · prāpuḥ · amarāḥ · mahiṣārditāḥ

Word by word

  1. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
  2. समस्तदेवानाम्samastadevānāmof all the godsm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on तेजोराशि- inside the next compound
  3. तेजोराशिसमुद्भवाम्tejorāśisamudbhavāmher who had arisen from the mass of radiancef. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ताम्
  4. ताम्tāmherpronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; object of विलोक्य
  5. विलोक्यvilokyahaving seenवि + √लोक् (curādi, 10 ‘to look’) + ल्यप् (absolutive with a prefix)
  6. मुदम्mudamjoyf. acc. sg. of मुद् (consonant stem); object of प्रापुः
  7. प्रापुःprāpuḥattained, feltप्र + √आप् (svādi, 5P ‘to obtain’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  8. अमराःamarāḥthe immortals, the godsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject
  9. महिषार्दिताःmahiṣārditāḥoppressed by Mahiṣam. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अमराः

Translation

Then, having beheld her, who had arisen from the mass of radiance of all the gods, the immortals, oppressed by Mahiṣa, attained joy.

In readable EnglishThen the gods, so long oppressed by Mahiṣa, looked on her who had arisen from the gathered radiance of them all, and they were filled with joy.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तेजोराशि tejorāśi → तेजस् + राशि

    visarga sandhi inside a compound · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    The s of तेजस् becomes रु and then u before the voiced r; a + u → o.

  • प्रापुरमरा महिषार्दिताः prāpuramarā mahiṣārditāḥ → प्रापुः + अमराः + महिषार्दिताः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    After u the visarga is sounded r before the vowel a. -āḥ before the voiced m loses its visarga (via y, which is dropped before a consonant).

  • महिषार्दिताः mahiṣārditāḥ → महिष + अर्दिताः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • समस्तदेवानाम् samastadevānām

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    समस्त + देव
    Vigraha
    समस्ताः देवाः, तेषाम् samastāḥ devāḥ, teṣām
    Literally
    “of the all-gods”
    In the verse
    of all the gods together

  • तेजोराशिसमुद्भवाम् tejorāśisamudbhavām

    bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (तेजोराशि: तेजसां राशिः)

    Members
    तेजोराशि + समुद्भव
    Vigraha
    तेजोराशेः समुद्भवः यस्याः सा, ताम् tejorāśeḥ samudbhavaḥ yasyāḥ sā, tām
    Literally
    “she whose origin is the heap of radiance”
    In the verse
    born of the gods’ gathered tejas
    Alternative
    Also possible as a pañcamī tatpuruṣa with an adjectival समुद्भवा ‘arisen from the mass of radiance’; the bahuvrīhi is the regular way such a compound qualifies a feminine.

  • अमराः amarāḥ

    nañ tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अ (न) + मर
    Vigraha
    न मराः na marāḥ
    Literally
    “not mortal”
    In the verse
    the gods

  • महिषार्दिताः mahiṣārditāḥ

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa

    Members
    महिष + अर्दित
    Vigraha
    महिषेण अर्दिताः mahiṣeṇa arditāḥ
    Literally
    “afflicted by Mahiṣa”
    In the verse
    the gods whom Mahiṣa had oppressed
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्रापुः prāpuḥ

    Perfect of प्र + √आप्: the reduplicating a merges with the root’s ā, so the perfect stem is आप्- (3rd pl. आपुः, with the prefix प्रापुः), ‘they obtained’. मुदं प्राप् is the idiom ‘to feel joy’.

  • समस्तदेवानाम् samastadevānām

    A genitive outside the compound that qualifies one of its members (तेजोराशि ‘the mass of radiance of all the gods’). Strict grammar frowns on this, but Purāṇic and epic verse uses it freely.

  • विलोक्य vilokya

    Absolutive of वि + √लोक्, ‘having beheld’; its agent is अमराः, the subject of the main verb.

Anvaya · prose order

ततः महिषार्दिताः अमराः समस्तदेवानां तेजोराशिसमुद्भवां तां विलोक्य मुदं प्रापुः।

tataḥ mahiṣārditāḥ amarāḥ samastadevānāṃ tejorāśisamudbhavāṃ tāṃ vilokya mudaṃ prāpuḥ |

Subject (महिषार्दिताः अमराः), an absolutive phrase with its object (तां … विलोक्य), and the main verb with its object (मुदं प्रापुः).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods see the woman formed from their pooled tejas and rejoice. Their joy closes the account of her birth and opens the giving of gifts (2.20–31).

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the gods rejoice at something greater than any of them, which is also their own power given back in a new form.

Verse 2.20

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

शूलं शूलाद्विनिष्कृष्य ददौ तस्यै पिनाकधृक्।चक्रं च दत्तवान् कृष्णः समुत्पाद्य स्वचक्रतः॥⁠२०⁠॥

śūlaṃ śūlādviniṣkṛṣya dadau tasyai pinākadhṛk |cakraṃ ca dattavān kṛṣṇaḥ samutpādya svacakrataḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

शूलम् · शूलात् · विनिष्कृष्य · ददौ · तस्यै · पिनाकधृक्चक्रम् · च · दत्तवान् · कृष्णः · समुत्पाद्य · स्वचक्रतः

śūlam · śūlāt · viniṣkṛṣya · dadau · tasyai · pinākadhṛkcakram · ca · dattavān · kṛṣṇaḥ · samutpādya · svacakrataḥ

Word by word

  1. शूलम्śūlama tridentn. acc. sg. of शूल; object of विनिष्कृष्य and ददौ
  2. शूलात्śūlātfrom (his own) tridentn. abl. sg. of शूल; ablative of source
  3. विनिष्कृष्यviniṣkṛṣyahaving drawn outवि + निस् + √कृष् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to drag’) + ल्यप् (absolutive with prefixes)
  4. ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3P ‘to give’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  5. तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.; indirect object
  6. पिनाकधृक्pinākadhṛkthe bearer of Pināka (Śiva)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of ददौ
  7. चक्रम्cakrama discusn. acc. sg. of चक्र; object of दत्तवान्
  8. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  9. दत्तवान्dattavāngave√दा + क्तवतु (past active participle), m. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
  10. कृष्णःkṛṣṇaḥKṛṣṇa (Viṣṇu)m. nom. sg. of कृष्ण; subject of दत्तवान्
  11. समुत्पाद्यsamutpādyahaving producedसम् + उद् + √पद् (causative stem पादि- ‘to bring forth’) + ल्यप्
  12. स्वचक्रतःsvacakrataḥfrom his own discusindeclinable; compound स्वचक्र (see Samāsa) + taddhita तसिल् with ablative sense

Translation

Drawing out a trident from (his own) trident, the bearer of Pināka gave it to her; and Kṛṣṇa, producing a discus from his own discus, gave it.

In readable EnglishŚiva, bearer of the Pināka bow, drew a trident out of his own trident and gave it to her; and Viṣṇu brought forth a discus from his own discus and gave it to her.

tr names the givers Śiva and Viṣṇu for पिनाकधृक् and कृष्ण, and repeats ‘to her’.

Textual verification / uncertain: Kṛṣṇa here is a name of Viṣṇu (the discus-bearer of 2.10), not specifically the avatāra. पिनाकधृक्: the final member -धृक् ‘holding’ is common at the end of compounds; its derivation (from √धृ, or by analogy with forms of √धृष्) is not certain. GRETIL’s Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa carries additional verse material at this point (recension note in corrections.json); the Gita Press text is followed.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • शूलं शूलाद्विनिष्कृष्य śūlaṃ śūlādviniṣkṛṣya → शूलम् + शूलात् + विनिष्कृष्य

    anusvāra; consonant sandhi (voicing) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; final t becomes d before the voiced v.

  • विनिष्कृष्य viniṣkṛṣya → वि + निस् + कृष्य

    consonant sandhi at the prefix · a rule of internal sandhi (the s of निस् becomes ṣ before k after i)

    The prefix निस् before the k of कृष् is written निष्; compare निश्चक्राम (2.10), where it becomes निश् before c.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • पिनाकधृक् pinākadhṛk

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    पिनाक + धृक्
    Vigraha
    पिनाकं धरति इति pinākaṃ dharati iti
    Literally
    “he who holds Pināka”
    In the verse
    Śiva, whose bow is called Pināka; -धृक् ‘holding’ is an agent noun used at the end of compounds

  • स्वचक्र svacakra

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    स्व + चक्र
    Vigraha
    स्वं चक्रम् svaṃ cakram
    Literally
    “own discus”
    In the verse
    Viṣṇu’s own discus, Sudarśana
Morphology & anvaya

  • दत्तवान् dattavān

    Past active participle (क्तवतु) of √दा, used as a finite past verb: ‘(he) gave’. The text alternates it with the perfect ददौ through the list of gifts.

  • समुत्पाद्य samutpādya

    Absolutive of the causative of सम् + उद् + √पद्, ‘having caused to arise’: each god produces a copy of his own weapon from it and gives the copy.

  • पिनाकधृक् pinākadhṛk

    Nominative singular of a consonant-stem agent noun (-धृक्), the same in pause; see Uncertain for its derivation.

Anvaya · prose order

पिनाकधृक् शूलात् शूलं विनिष्कृष्य तस्यै ददौ। कृष्णः च स्वचक्रतः चक्रं समुत्पाद्य (तस्यै) दत्तवान्।

pinākadhṛk śūlāt śūlaṃ viniṣkṛṣya tasyai dadau | kṛṣṇaḥ ca svacakrataḥ cakraṃ samutpādya (tasyai) dattavān |

Two parallel sentences: giver, an absolutive phrase (how the weapon was made), the gift, and the verb.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gifts begin. Each weapon is drawn out of the god’s own weapon: the gods do not give theirs away but make a second one from it. Śiva gives the trident and Viṣṇu the discus.

Traditional interpretation

The dhyāna of the middle carita in the Gita Press pāṭha (recited before 2.1) pictures Mahālakṣmī holding these gifts: trident, discus (सुदर्शन), conch, spear, bow, thunderbolt, bell, staff, noose, rosary, water-pot, sword and shield among them. The chapter supplies the story behind that iconography.

Verse 2.21

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

शङ्खं च वरुणः शक्तिं ददौ तस्यै हुताशनः।मारुतो दत्तवांश्चापं बाणपूर्णे तथेषुधी॥⁠२१⁠॥

śaṅkhaṃ ca varuṇaḥ śaktiṃ dadau tasyai hutāśanaḥ |māruto dattavāṃścāpaṃ bāṇapūrṇe tatheṣudhī ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

शङ्खम् · च · वरुणः · शक्तिम् · ददौ · तस्यै · हुताशनःमारुतः · दत्तवान् · चापम् · बाणपूर्णे · तथा · इषुधी

śaṅkham · ca · varuṇaḥ · śaktim · dadau · tasyai · hutāśanaḥmārutaḥ · dattavān · cāpam · bāṇapūrṇe · tathā · iṣudhī

Word by word

  1. शङ्खम्śaṅkhama conchm. acc. sg. of शङ्ख; object (ददौ understood with वरुणः)
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  3. वरुणःvaruṇaḥVaruṇam. nom. sg. of वरुण, lord of the waters; subject
  4. शक्तिम्śaktima spear (śakti)f. acc. sg. of शक्ति; object of ददौ
  5. ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  6. तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.
  7. हुताशनःhutāśanaḥHutāśana, the eater of oblations (Agni)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of ददौ
  8. मारुतःmārutaḥMāruta, the Windm. nom. sg. of मारुत; subject of दत्तवान्
  9. दत्तवान्dattavāngave√दा + क्तवतु, m. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
  10. चापम्cāpama bowm./n. acc. sg. of चाप; object
  11. बाणपूर्णेbāṇapūrṇefull of arrowsf. acc. dual; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies इषुधी
  12. तथाtathāand alsoindeclinable (adverb, here conjunctive)
  13. इषुधीiṣudhītwo quiversf. acc. dual of इषुधि; object of दत्तवान्

Translation

And Varuṇa (gave) a conch; Hutāśana gave her a spear; Māruta gave a bow and also two quivers full of arrows.

In readable EnglishVaruṇa gave her a conch, and Fire a spear; the Wind gave her a bow and two quivers full of arrows.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • मारुतो दत्तवांश्चापं māruto dattavāṃścāpaṃ → मारुतः + दत्तवान् + चापम्

    visarga sandhi; consonant sandhi (n + c) · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87); नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o. Final n before c becomes anusvāra + ś: दत्तवान् चापम् → दत्तवांश्चापम्.

  • तथेषुधी tatheṣudhī → तथा + इषुधी

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    ā + i → e.

  • शङ्खं च śaṅkhaṃ ca → शङ्खम् + च

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; likewise शक्तिं ददौ.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • हुताशनः hutāśanaḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    हुत + अशन
    Vigraha
    हुतम् अशनं यस्य सः hutam aśanaṃ yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose food is the oblation”
    In the verse
    Agni, the fire that consumes what is offered
    Alternative
    Also analysed as an upapada-type tatpuruṣa, हुतम् अश्नाति इति ‘he who eats the oblation’ (अशन as an agent noun); the sense is the same.

  • बाणपूर्णे bāṇapūrṇe

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa

    Members
    बाण + पूर्ण
    Vigraha
    बाणैः पूर्णे bāṇaiḥ pūrṇe
    Literally
    “filled with arrows”
    In the verse
    fully stocked
Morphology & anvaya

  • इषुधी iṣudhī

    Accusative dual of इषुधि ‘quiver’ (‘that in which arrows are placed’). The noun is masculine or feminine; the feminine adjective बाणपूर्णे shows it is feminine here. Two quivers, one for each side.

  • बाणपूर्णे bāṇapūrṇe

    Feminine accusative dual of the a-stem adjective (-ā stem in the feminine, dual -ए), agreeing with इषुधी.

Anvaya · prose order

वरुणः च शङ्खं (तस्यै ददौ), हुताशनः तस्यै शक्तिं ददौ। मारुतः चापं तथा बाणपूर्णे इषुधी दत्तवान्।

varuṇaḥ ca śaṅkhaṃ (tasyai dadau), hutāśanaḥ tasyai śaktiṃ dadau | mārutaḥ cāpaṃ tathā bāṇapūrṇe iṣudhī dattavān |

Three gifts: ददौ is stated once for Varuṇa’s and Agni’s gifts; Māruta’s bow and quivers take दत्तवान्.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Three more gifts: the conch from Varuṇa, lord of the waters (the conch is a sea creature), the spear (śakti) from Agni, and from the Wind a bow and two full quivers.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: each gift fits its giver. The sea gives the conch, fire the spear, and the swift wind the bow and arrows that fly through it.

Verse 2.22

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

वज्रमिन्द्रः समुत्पाद्य कुलिशादमराधिपः।ददौ तस्यै सहस्राक्षो घण्टामैरावताद् गजात्॥⁠२२⁠॥

vajramindraḥ samutpādya kuliśādamarādhipaḥ |dadau tasyai sahasrākṣo ghaṇṭāmairāvatād gajāt ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

वज्रम् · इन्द्रः · समुत्पाद्य · कुलिशात् · अमराधिपःददौ · तस्यै · सहस्राक्षः · घण्टाम् · ऐरावतात् · गजात्

vajram · indraḥ · samutpādya · kuliśāt · amarādhipaḥdadau · tasyai · sahasrākṣaḥ · ghaṇṭām · airāvatāt · gajāt

Word by word

  1. वज्रम्vajrama thunderbolt (vajra)m./n. acc. sg. of वज्र; object of समुत्पाद्य and ददौ
  2. इन्द्रःindraḥIndram. nom. sg. of इन्द्र; subject of ददौ
  3. समुत्पाद्यsamutpādyahaving producedसम् + उद् + √पद् (causative) + ल्यप्
  4. कुलिशात्kuliśātfrom (his own) thunderboltm./n. abl. sg. of कुलिश; ablative of source
  5. अमराधिपःamarādhipaḥlord of the immortalsm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to इन्द्रः
  6. ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.
  8. सहस्राक्षःsahasrākṣaḥthe thousand-eyed (Indra)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to इन्द्रः
  9. घण्टाम्ghaṇṭāma bellf. acc. sg. of घण्टा; second object of ददौ
  10. ऐरावतात्airāvatātfrom Airāvatam. abl. sg. of ऐरावत, Indra’s elephant; in apposition to गजात्
  11. गजात्gajāt(his) elephantm. abl. sg. of गज; ablative of source

Translation

Indra, lord of the immortals, producing a thunderbolt from (his own) thunderbolt, gave it to her, the thousand-eyed one, and a bell from the elephant Airāvata.

In readable EnglishIndra, the thousand-eyed lord of the gods, brought forth a thunderbolt from his own and gave it to her, together with a bell from his elephant Airāvata.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • कुलिशादमराधिपः kuliśādamarādhipaḥ → कुलिशात् + अमराधिपः

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t is voiced to d before a vowel; likewise ऐरावताद् गजात् before the voiced g. Inside the compound, अमर + अधिप → अमराधिप (अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः, 6.1.101).

  • सहस्राक्षो घण्टाम् sahasrākṣo ghaṇṭām → सहस्राक्षः + घण्टाम्

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced gh becomes -o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अमराधिपः amarādhipaḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अमर + अधिप
    Vigraha
    अमराणाम् अधिपः amarāṇām adhipaḥ
    Literally
    “overlord of the immortals”
    In the verse
    Indra, king of the gods (the title Mahiṣa had usurped, 2.3)

  • सहस्राक्षः sahasrākṣaḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    सहस्र + अक्षि
    Vigraha
    सहस्रम् अक्षीणि यस्य सः sahasram akṣīṇi yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he who has a thousand eyes”
    In the verse
    Indra; अक्षि becomes अक्ष at the end of a bahuvrīhi (a samāsānta affix)
Morphology & anvaya

  • समुत्पाद्य samutpādya

    As in 2.20: absolutive of the causative of सम् + उद् + √पद्. Indra makes a vajra from his own kuliśa (both words mean ‘thunderbolt’).

  • गजात् gajāt

    Ablative of source: the bell is taken from Airāvata, the elephant, who wore it. ऐरावतात् and गजात् stand in apposition.

Anvaya · prose order

अमराधिपः सहस्राक्षः इन्द्रः कुलिशात् वज्रं समुत्पाद्य तस्यै ददौ, ऐरावतात् गजात् घण्टां (च ददौ)।

amarādhipaḥ sahasrākṣaḥ indraḥ kuliśāt vajraṃ samutpādya tasyai dadau, airāvatāt gajāt ghaṇṭāṃ (ca dadau) |

One subject with two epithets, an absolutive phrase, the verb ददौ, and a second object (घण्टाम्) with its source.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Indra gives a thunderbolt made from his own and the bell of his elephant Airāvata. The god whose throne Mahiṣa took is named by his full titles: lord of the immortals, the thousand-eyed.

Traditional interpretation

The bell will be used in the battle: its sound stupefies the asuras (2.56, घण्टास्वनविमोहितान्), and the gods later pray that it protect them (11.27).

Verse 2.23

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

कालदण्डाद्यमो दण्डं पाशं चाम्बुपतिर्ददौ।प्रजापतिश्चाक्षमालां ददौ ब्रह्मा कमण्डलुम्॥⁠२३⁠॥

kāladaṇḍādyamo daṇḍaṃ pāśaṃ cāmbupatirdadau |prajāpatiścākṣamālāṃ dadau brahmā kamaṇḍalum ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

कालदण्डात् · यमः · दण्डम् · पाशम् · च · अम्बुपतिः · ददौप्रजापतिः · च · अक्षमालाम् · ददौ · ब्रह्मा · कमण्डलुम्

kāladaṇḍāt · yamaḥ · daṇḍam · pāśam · ca · ambupatiḥ · dadauprajāpatiḥ · ca · akṣamālām · dadau · brahmā · kamaṇḍalum

Word by word

  1. कालदण्डात्kāladaṇḍātfrom (his) staff of deathm. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); ablative of source
  2. यमःyamaḥYamam. nom. sg. of यम, lord of death; subject (ददौ understood)
  3. दण्डम्daṇḍama staffm. acc. sg. of दण्ड; object
  4. पाशम्pāśama noosem. acc. sg. of पाश; object of ददौ
  5. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  6. अम्बुपतिःambupatiḥthe lord of the waters (Varuṇa)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of ददौ
  7. ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. प्रजापतिःprajāpatiḥPrajāpatim. nom. sg.; compound (प्रजानां पतिः, as in 2.4); subject of ददौ
  9. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  10. अक्षमालाम्akṣamālāma rosary (of rudrākṣa beads)f. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of ददौ
  11. ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. ब्रह्माbrahmāBrahmām. nom. sg. of ब्रह्मन्; subject of the second ददौ
  13. कमण्डलुम्kamaṇḍaluma water-potm. acc. sg. of कमण्डलु (u-stem); object

Translation

Yama (gave) a staff from (his) staff of death, and the lord of the waters gave a noose; and Prajāpati gave a rosary; Brahmā a water-pot.

In readable EnglishYama gave her a staff drawn from his own staff of death, and Varuṇa, lord of the waters, a noose; Prajāpati gave her a rosary, and Brahmā a water-pot.

Textual verification / uncertain: प्रजापतिश्चाक्षमालां ददौ ब्रह्मा कमण्डलुम्: with ददौ stated twice, the verse is taken as naming two givers, Prajāpati (as in 2.17, distinct from Brahmā) and Brahmā. It can also be read as one giver, ‘Brahmā the Prajāpati gave a rosary and a water-pot’, since Brahmā is called प्रजापति in 2.4. The reading itself is not in doubt.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • कालदण्डाद्यमो दण्डं kāladaṇḍādyamo daṇḍaṃ → कालदण्डात् + यमः + दण्डम्

    consonant sandhi (voicing); visarga sandhi · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    Final t becomes d before the voiced y; -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.

  • चाम्बुपतिर्ददौ cāmbupatirdadau → च + अम्बुपतिः + ददौ

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    a + a → ā; after i the visarga is sounded r before the voiced d.

  • प्रजापतिश्चाक्षमालां prajāpatiścākṣamālāṃ → प्रजापतिः + च + अक्षमालाम्

    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Visarga before c becomes ś; च + अक्षमालाम्: a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • कालदण्डात् kāladaṇḍāt

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    काल + दण्ड
    Vigraha
    कालस्य दण्डः, तस्मात् kālasya daṇḍaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally
    “from the staff of Kāla (Time, Death)”
    In the verse
    from Yama’s own staff of death
    Alternative
    Also a karmadhāraya, कालः एव दण्डः ‘the staff that is death’; either way it is Yama’s own weapon.

  • अम्बुपतिः ambupatiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अम्बु + पति
    Vigraha
    अम्बूनां पतिः ambūnāṃ patiḥ
    Literally
    “lord of the waters”
    In the verse
    Varuṇa

  • अक्षमालाम् akṣamālām

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अक्ष + माला
    Vigraha
    अक्षाणां माला, ताम् akṣāṇāṃ mālā, tām
    Literally
    “a garland of akṣa beads”
    In the verse
    a rosary for counting recitations
Morphology & anvaya

  • ब्रह्मा brahmā

    Nominative singular of the masculine n-stem ब्रह्मन् (the god Brahmā), as distinct from the neuter ब्रह्म (the Absolute).

  • कमण्डलुम् kamaṇḍalum

    Accusative of the u-stem कमण्डलु, the ascetic’s water-pot, Brahmā’s emblem.

Anvaya · prose order

यमः कालदण्डात् दण्डं (तस्यै ददौ), अम्बुपतिः च पाशं ददौ। प्रजापतिः च अक्षमालां ददौ, ब्रह्मा कमण्डलुं (ददौ)।

yamaḥ kāladaṇḍāt daṇḍaṃ (tasyai dadau), ambupatiḥ ca pāśaṃ dadau | prajāpatiḥ ca akṣamālāṃ dadau, brahmā kamaṇḍaluṃ (dadau) |

Four gifts in two lines; ददौ is stated twice and understood with the other two givers.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Yama gives a staff, Varuṇa a noose (his second gift, after the conch in 2.21), Prajāpati a rosary and Brahmā a water-pot. The last two are not weapons but the emblems of an ascetic and of the creator.

Traditional interpretation

The noose (पाश) is Varuṇa’s ancient emblem, the bond with which he binds wrongdoers. In the battle the Goddess will use it to bind asuras and drag them along (2.57, पाशेन बद्ध्वा).

Verse 2.24

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

समस्तरोमकूपेषु निजरश्मीन् दिवाकरः।कालश्च दत्तवान् खड्गं तस्याश्चर्म च निर्मलम्॥⁠२४⁠॥

samastaromakūpeṣu nijaraśmīn divākaraḥ |kālaśca dattavān khaḍgaṃ tasyāścarma ca nirmalam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

समस्तरोमकूपेषु · निजरश्मीन् · दिवाकरःकालः · च · दत्तवान् · खड्गम् · तस्याः · चर्म · च · निर्मलम्

samastaromakūpeṣu · nijaraśmīn · divākaraḥkālaḥ · ca · dattavān · khaḍgam · tasyāḥ · carma · ca · nirmalam

Word by word

  1. समस्तरोमकूपेषुsamastaromakūpeṣuinto all the pores of her skinm. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); locative of the place where the rays were set
  2. निजरश्मीन्nijaraśmīnhis own raysm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object (ददौ understood)
  3. दिवाकरःdivākaraḥthe Day-maker, the Sunm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject
  4. कालःkālaḥKāla (Time)m. nom. sg. of काल; subject of दत्तवान्
  5. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  6. दत्तवान्dattavāngave√दा + क्तवतु, m. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
  7. खड्गम्khaḍgama swordm. acc. sg. of खड्ग; object
  8. तस्याःtasyāḥto herpronoun तद्, f. gen. sg.; genitive used for the dative of the recipient
  9. चर्मcarmaa shieldn. acc. sg. of चर्मन् (lit. ‘hide’, a leather shield); object
  10. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  11. निर्मलम्nirmalamspotlessn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies चर्म

Translation

Into all the pores (of her skin) the Day-maker (set) his own rays; and Kāla gave her a sword and a spotless shield.

In readable EnglishThe Sun filled every pore of her body with his own rays, and Time gave her a sword and a gleaming shield.

‘Filled’ is supplied for the missing verb of the first line.

Textual verification / uncertain: The first line has no finite verb; ददौ (or a verb of placing) is understood. Kāla is taken as Time personified, distinct from Yama (2.23), who is named separately.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • कालश्च kālaśca → कालः + च

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c becomes ś; likewise तस्याश्चर्म.

  • खड्गं तस्याश्चर्म khaḍgaṃ tasyāścarma → खड्गम् + तस्याः + चर्म

    anusvāra; visarga sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Final m before t becomes anusvāra; the visarga of तस्याः before c becomes ś.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • समस्तरोमकूपेषु samastaromakūpeṣu

    karmadhāraya containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (रोमकूप: रोम्णां कूपाः)

    Members
    समस्त + रोमकूप
    Vigraha
    समस्ताः रोमकूपाः, तेषु samastāḥ romakūpāḥ, teṣu
    Literally
    “in all the hair-wells”
    In the verse
    in every pore of her body

  • निजरश्मीन् nijaraśmīn

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    निज + रश्मि
    Vigraha
    निजाः रश्मयः, तान् nijāḥ raśmayaḥ, tān
    Literally
    “own rays”
    In the verse
    the Sun’s own light

  • दिवाकरः divākaraḥ

    upapada tatpuruṣa (कर from √कृ + ट)

    Members
    दिवा + कर
    Vigraha
    दिवा करोति इति divā karoti iti
    Literally
    “he who makes the day”
    In the verse
    the Sun

  • निर्मलम् nirmalam

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    निर् + मल
    Vigraha
    निर्गतं मलं यस्मात् तत् nirgataṃ malaṃ yasmāt tat
    Literally
    “that from which stain has gone”
    In the verse
    flawless, gleaming
Morphology & anvaya

  • तस्याः tasyāḥ

    Genitive of the recipient: with verbs of giving, epic and Purāṇic Sanskrit often uses the genitive where the classical language prefers the dative (contrast तस्यै in 2.20–22).

  • चर्म carma

    Accusative (= nominative) singular of the neuter n-stem चर्मन्: the final n is lost (चर्म).

Anvaya · prose order

दिवाकरः समस्तरोमकूपेषु निजरश्मीन् (ददौ)। कालः च खड्गं निर्मलं चर्म च तस्याः दत्तवान्।

divākaraḥ samastaromakūpeṣu nijaraśmīn (dadau) | kālaḥ ca khaḍgaṃ nirmalaṃ carma ca tasyāḥ dattavān |

The first line has no verb of its own (ददौ from 2.23 is understood); the second is a full sentence with two objects.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The Sun’s gift is not a weapon but his light, set into every pore of her skin: the Goddess shines from within. Kāla, Time, gives the sword and shield.

Traditional interpretation

Kāla here is Time personified, a power that ends all things, and so a fitting giver of the sword. The sword and shield are among the weapons named in the dhyāna of the middle carita (असिं च चर्म).

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) calls the Goddess सर्वायुधधरा, ‘bearer of all weapons’ (name 530). In the Devī Māhātmya the same idea is told as a story: every weapon she holds was given to her by a god.

Verse 2.25

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

क्षीरोदश्चामलं हारमजरे च तथाम्बरे।चूडामणिं तथा दिव्यं कुण्डले कटकानि च॥⁠२५⁠॥

kṣīrodaścāmalaṃ hāramajare ca tathāmbare |cūḍāmaṇiṃ tathā divyaṃ kuṇḍale kaṭakāni ca ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

क्षीरोदः · च · अमलम् · हारम् · अजरे · च · तथा · अम्बरेचूडामणिम् · तथा · दिव्यम् · कुण्डले · कटकानि · च

kṣīrodaḥ · ca · amalam · hāram · ajare · ca · tathā · ambarecūḍāmaṇim · tathā · divyam · kuṇḍale · kaṭakāni · ca

Word by word

  1. क्षीरोदःkṣīrodaḥthe Ocean of Milkm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of a ददौ supplied from the surrounding verses
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); adds the Ocean to the givers of 2.20–24
  3. अमलम्amalamspotless, purem. acc. sg. of अमल (‘without stain’); qualifies हारम्
  4. हारम्hārama necklacem. acc. sg. of हार (a-stem); object of the supplied ददौ
  5. अजरेajareunaging, that never wear outn. acc. du. of अजर (‘free from decay’); qualifies अम्बरे
  6. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  7. तथाtathāalso, likewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner, used as a connective)
  8. अम्बरेambaretwo garmentsn. acc. du. of अम्बर (lit. ‘garment’; also ‘sky’); object
  9. चूडामणिम्cūḍāmaṇima crest-jewelm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object
  10. तथाtathāalsoindeclinable (connective)
  11. दिव्यम्divyamdivine, heavenlym. acc. sg. of दिव्य (taddhita from दिव् ‘heaven’); qualifies चूडामणिम्
  12. कुण्डलेkuṇḍalea pair of earringsn. acc. du. of कुण्डल; object
  13. कटकानिkaṭakānibraceletsn. acc. pl. of कटक (m./n., ‘ring, bracelet’); object
  14. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)

Translation

And the Milk-Ocean (gave) a spotless necklace and likewise two unaging garments, likewise a divine crest-jewel, two earrings and bracelets,

In readable EnglishThe Ocean of Milk gave her a spotless necklace, a pair of garments that never wear out, a heavenly crest-jewel, earrings and bracelets,

‘Gave her’ is supplied from the catalogue’s verb ददौ; the sentence continues into 2.26–27.

Textual verification / uncertain: The verse has no verb, and the boundary between the Ocean’s gifts and Viśvakarman’s (2.27) is not marked in the text; the translation follows the common reading (the Ocean gives everything to the rings of 2.27a).

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • क्षीरोदश्चामलं kṣīrodaścāmalaṃ → क्षीरोदः + च + अमलम्

    visarga sandhi and vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś (क्षीरोदश्च); then a + a → ā (चामलं). The final m of अमलम् is anusvāra before the h of हारम् (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • हारमजरे hāramajare → हारम् + अजरे

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    Final m stays m before a vowel and is written together with it.

  • तथाम्बरे tathāmbare → तथा + अम्बरे

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā.

  • क्षीरोद kṣīroda → क्षीर + उद

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • क्षीरोदः kṣīrodaḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    क्षीर + उद (= उदक)
    Vigraha
    क्षीरम् उदकं यस्य सः kṣīram udakaṃ yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose water is milk”
    In the verse
    the Ocean of Milk, personified as a giver
    Alternative
    Also analysed as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, क्षीरस्य उदः ‘the water of milk’, i.e. the milk-sea; the bahuvrīhi suits a personified giver. उद stands for उदक as the final member of such compounds.

  • चूडामणिम् cūḍāmaṇim

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    चूडा + मणि
    Vigraha
    चूडायाः मणिः (तम्) cūḍāyāḥ maṇiḥ (tam)
    Literally
    “the jewel of the crest”
    In the verse
    a jewel worn on the crown of the head
    Alternative
    Also saptamī tatpuruṣa, चूडायां मणिः ‘the jewel (worn) on the crest’; the sense is the same.
Morphology & anvaya

  • अम्बरे ambare

    Neuter dual of an a-stem (nom./acc. -e): ‘two garments’, the upper and the lower cloth. With अजरे it is a pair of everlasting robes.

  • कुण्डले kuṇḍale

    Neuter dual again: earrings come as a pair, so the dual is the natural number. A dual in -e is pragṛhya, but here both duals stand before consonants, so no hiatus arises.

Anvaya · prose order

क्षीरोदः च अमलं हारम्, अजरे अम्बरे च, तथा दिव्यं चूडामणिम्, कुण्डले कटकानि च (ददौ)।

kṣīrodaḥ ca amalaṃ hāram, ajare ambare ca, tathā divyaṃ cūḍāmaṇim, kuṇḍale kaṭakāni ca (dadau) |

The verse has a subject and a string of objects but no verb of its own; ददौ, the verb of the whole catalogue (2.20–31), is supplied. The list of ornaments runs on into 2.26 and 2.27a.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

After the weapons come the ornaments. The Ocean of Milk, the store of treasures in Purāṇic myth, dresses the newly formed Goddess: necklace, a pair of imperishable garments, crest-jewel, earrings and bracelets. The verse names a giver but no verb; the list continues for a verse and a half.

Traditional interpretation

Where the list of the Ocean’s gifts ends is not marked. The usual reading lets it run to the finger-rings of 2.27a, so that Viśvakarman’s gift begins with the axe; others give the jewellery after the garments to the craftsman Viśvakarman, whose verb ददौ follows in 2.27. The Sanskrit allows both.

Verse 2.26

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

अर्धचन्द्रं तथा शुभ्रं केयूरान् सर्वबाहुषु।नूपुरौ विमलौ तद्वद् ग्रैवेयकमनुत्तमम्॥⁠२६⁠॥

ardhacandraṃ tathā śubhraṃ keyūrān sarvabāhuṣu |nūpurau vimalau tadvad graiveyakamanuttamam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

अर्धचन्द्रम् · तथा · शुभ्रम् · केयूरान् · सर्वबाहुषुनूपुरौ · विमलौ · तद्वत् · ग्रैवेयकम् · अनुत्तमम्

ardhacandram · tathā · śubhram · keyūrān · sarvabāhuṣunūpurau · vimalau · tadvat · graiveyakam · anuttamam

Word by word

  1. अर्धचन्द्रम्ardhacandrama crescent (ornament)m. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of the supplied ददौ
  2. तथाtathāalsoindeclinable (connective)
  3. शुभ्रम्śubhrambright, gleaming whitem. acc. sg. of शुभ्र; qualifies अर्धचन्द्रम्
  4. केयूरान्keyūrānarmletsm. acc. pl. of केयूर; object
  5. सर्वबाहुषुsarvabāhuṣuon all (her) armsm. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
  6. नूपुरौnūpuraua pair of ankletsm. acc. du. of नूपुर; object
  7. विमलौvimalauspotless, shiningm. acc. du. of विमल; qualifies नूपुरौ
  8. तद्वत्tadvatlikewise, in the same wayindeclinable (तद् + taddhita -vat ‘like that’)
  9. ग्रैवेयकम्graiveyakama neck-ornament, a collarn. acc. sg. of ग्रैवेयक (taddhita from ग्रीवा ‘neck’: ‘what belongs to the neck’); object
  10. अनुत्तमम्anuttamamunsurpassed, the finestn. acc. sg. of अनुत्तम; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ग्रैवेयकम्

Translation

likewise a bright half-moon, armlets on all the arms, likewise two spotless anklets, an unsurpassed neck-ornament,

In readable Englisha gleaming crescent, armlets for all her arms, a pair of shining anklets and a peerless collar,

The sentence continues from 2.25 and into 2.27.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • अर्धचन्द्रं तथा ardhacandraṃ tathā → अर्धचन्द्रम् + तथा

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; likewise शुभ्रं before k.

  • तद्वद् ग्रैवेयकम् tadvad graiveyakam → तद्वत् + ग्रैवेयकम्

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before the voiced g. The Gita Press writes the d with a virāma and a space.

  • ग्रैवेयकमनुत्तमम् graiveyakamanuttamam → ग्रैवेयकम् + अनुत्तमम्

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    Final m is simply written together with the following vowel.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अर्धचन्द्रम् ardhacandram

    tatpuruṣa (ekadeśin type: the part named first, ‘half of the moon’)

    Members
    अर्ध + चन्द्र
    Vigraha
    अर्धं चन्द्रस्य (तम्) ardhaṃ candrasya (tam)
    Literally
    “the half-moon”
    In the verse
    a crescent-shaped ornament, worn on the head or brow
    Alternative
    Also taken as a karmadhāraya, अर्धः चासौ चन्द्रः च ‘a half moon’; the meaning is the same.

  • सर्वबाहुषु sarvabāhuṣu

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    सर्व + बाहु
    Vigraha
    सर्वे च ते बाहवः च, तेषु sarve ca te bāhavaḥ ca, teṣu
    Literally
    “on all the arms”
    In the verse
    on every one of her many arms

  • अनुत्तमम् anuttamam

    bahuvrīhi (with the negative particle)

    Members
    न + उत्तम
    Vigraha
    न विद्यते उत्तमः यस्मात् तत् na vidyate uttamaḥ yasmāt tat
    Literally
    “that than which there is no higher”
    In the verse
    peerless, of the highest kind
Morphology & anvaya

  • तद्वत् tadvat

    The taddhita -vat of comparison added to तद्: ‘like that, likewise’. It is an indeclinable and must not be confused with the possessive -vat of हिमवत् (2.29).

  • नूपुरौ nūpurau

    Masculine dual (-au): anklets, like earrings, are named as a pair.

Anvaya · prose order

तथा शुभ्रम् अर्धचन्द्रम्, सर्वबाहुषु केयूरान्, तद्वत् विमलौ नूपुरौ, अनुत्तमं ग्रैवेयकं (च ददौ)।

tathā śubhram ardhacandram, sarvabāhuṣu keyūrān, tadvat vimalau nūpurau, anuttamaṃ graiveyakaṃ (ca dadau) |

A continuation of the list begun in 2.25, all accusatives of the supplied verb; सर्वबाहुषु says where the armlets are worn.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The list of ornaments continues from head to foot: a crescent for the head, armlets for ‘all her arms’, anklets and a collar. The plural arms anticipate 2.39, where she fills the directions with a thousand arms.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

The same ornament vocabulary describes the Goddess’s form in the Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa): 31, कनकाङ्गदकेयूरकमनीयभुजान्विता (arms lovely with golden aṅgadas and keyūras), and 32, रत्नग्रैवेयचिन्ताकलोलमुक्ताफलान्विता (with a jewelled graiveya and pearls). This is a shared iconographic vocabulary, not a borrowing between the two texts.

Verse 2.27

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

अङ्गुलीयकरत्नानि समस्तास्वङ्गुलीषु च।विश्वकर्मा ददौ तस्यै परशुं चातिनिर्मलम्॥⁠२७⁠॥

aṅgulīyakaratnāni samastāsvaṅgulīṣu ca |viśvakarmā dadau tasyai paraśuṃ cātinirmalam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

अङ्गुलीयकरत्नानि · समस्तासु · अङ्गुलीषु · चविश्वकर्मा · ददौ · तस्यै · परशुम् · च · अतिनिर्मलम्

aṅgulīyakaratnāni · samastāsu · aṅgulīṣu · caviśvakarmā · dadau · tasyai · paraśum · ca · atinirmalam

Word by word

  1. अङ्गुलीयकरत्नानिaṅgulīyakaratnānithe finest rings, jewelled ringsn. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of the supplied ददौ
  2. समस्तासुsamastāsuon allf. loc. pl. of समस्त (सम् + √अस् + kta, ‘put together, entire’); qualifies अङ्गुलीषु
  3. अङ्गुलीषुaṅgulīṣuon the fingersf. loc. pl. of अङ्गुली (ī-stem)
  4. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); closes the list begun in 2.25
  5. विश्वकर्माviśvakarmāViśvakarman, the craftsman of the godsm. nom. sg. of विश्वकर्मन् (n-stem); compound (see Samāsa); subject of ददौ
  6. ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to give’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.; indirect object
  8. परशुम्paraśuman axem. acc. sg. of परशु (u-stem); object of ददौ
  9. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); links परशुम् to the further gifts of 2.28a
  10. अतिनिर्मलम्atinirmalamexceedingly bright, flawlessm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies परशुम्

Translation

and jewel-rings on all the fingers. Viśvakarman gave to her an exceedingly spotless axe and

In readable Englishand the finest rings for every finger. Viśvakarman gave her a gleaming axe,

The first line is taken as the last of the Ocean’s gifts (see 2.25); the second line runs on into 2.28.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • समस्तास्वङ्गुलीषु samastāsvaṅgulīṣu → समस्तासु + अङ्गुलीषु

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    Final u before a dissimilar vowel becomes v: -āsu a- → -āsva-.

  • चातिनिर्मलम् cātinirmalam → च + अतिनिर्मलम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā. The m of परशुम् before c is anusvāra (परशुं, 8.3.23).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अङ्गुलीयकरत्नानि aṅgulīyakaratnāni

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अङ्गुलीयक + रत्न
    Vigraha
    अङ्गुलीयकानां रत्नानि aṅgulīyakānāṃ ratnāni
    Literally
    “the jewels among finger-rings”
    In the verse
    the finest rings (रत्न at the end of a compound means ‘the best of its kind’)
    Alternative
    Also read as ‘gem-rings’, रत्नमयानि अङ्गुलीयकानि, a madhyamapadalopin karmadhāraya with the members reversed; but the head of the compound is रत्न, which favours ‘the jewels among rings’.

  • विश्वकर्मा viśvakarmā

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    विश्व + कर्मन्
    Vigraha
    विश्वं कर्म यस्य सः viśvaṃ karma yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose work is everything”
    In the verse
    the maker of all things, the divine artisan

  • अतिनिर्मलम् atinirmalam

    tatpuruṣa (prādi); निर्मल is itself a bahuvrīhi, निर्गतः मलः यस्मात् तत्

    Members
    अति + निर्मल
    Vigraha
    अतिशयेन निर्मलम् atiśayena nirmalam
    Literally
    “beyond stainless”
    In the verse
    gleaming, without a flaw
Morphology & anvaya

  • ददौ dadau

    Perfect of √दा: reduplicated दद- with the ending -au that roots in -ā take in the 1st and 3rd singular perfect (ददौ ‘he gave’). The catalogue’s refrain verb.

  • विश्वकर्मा viśvakarmā

    Nominative singular of an n-stem: the final n is dropped and the vowel lengthened (कर्मन् → कर्मा), as in राजा.

Anvaya · prose order

समस्तासु अङ्गुलीषु अङ्गुलीयकरत्नानि च (क्षीरोदः ददौ)। विश्वकर्मा तस्यै अतिनिर्मलं परशुं च (अनेकरूपाणि अस्त्राणि अभेद्यं दंशनं च) ददौ।

samastāsu aṅgulīṣu aṅgulīyakaratnāni ca (kṣīrodaḥ dadau) | viśvakarmā tasyai atinirmalaṃ paraśuṃ ca (anekarūpāṇi astrāṇi abhedyaṃ daṃśanaṃ ca) dadau |

The first line ends the Ocean’s list (2.25–26); the second begins a new giver, Viśvakarman, whose objects continue into the first line of 2.28.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Rings for every finger complete the jewellery. Viśvakarman, the craftsman of the gods, gives the axe and, in the next line, missiles and armour.

Traditional interpretation

The axe (परशु) appears in the madhyama-carita dhyāna (Gita Press pāṭha), where Mahālakṣmī holds अक्षस्रक्परशुं … — rosary and axe are the first two objects named. The dhyāna gathers the gifts of this chapter into eighteen hands.

Verse 2.28

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

अस्त्राण्यनेकरूपाणि तथाभेद्यं च दंशनम्।अम्लानपङ्कजां मालां शिरस्युरसि चापराम्॥⁠२८⁠॥

astrāṇyanekarūpāṇi tathābhedyaṃ ca daṃśanam |amlānapaṅkajāṃ mālāṃ śirasyurasi cāparām ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

अस्त्राणि · अनेकरूपाणि · तथा · अभेद्यम् · च · दंशनम्अम्लानपङ्कजाम् · मालाम् · शिरसि · उरसि · च · अपराम्

astrāṇi · anekarūpāṇi · tathā · abhedyam · ca · daṃśanamamlānapaṅkajām · mālām · śirasi · urasi · ca · aparām

Word by word

  1. अस्त्राणिastrāṇimissilesn. acc. pl. of अस्त्र (√अस् ‘to throw’ + ṣṭran: a weapon hurled or discharged); object of ददौ (2.27)
  2. अनेकरूपाणिanekarūpāṇiof many formsn. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अस्त्राणि
  3. तथाtathāand alsoindeclinable (connective)
  4. अभेद्यम्abhedyamimpenetrablen. acc. sg. of अभेद्य (negative न + √भिद् ‘to split’ + gerundive ṇyat: ‘not to be pierced’); qualifies दंशनम्
  5. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  6. दंशनम्daṃśanamarmour, a coat of mailn. acc. sg. of दंशन (√दंश् + lyuṭ); object of ददौ (2.27)
  7. अम्लानपङ्कजाम्amlānapaṅkajāmof unfading lotusesf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मालाम्
  8. मालाम्mālāma garlandf. acc. sg. of माला; object of अददत् (2.29)
  9. शिरसिśirasifor the head, on the headn. loc. sg. of शिरस् (s-stem)
  10. उरसिurasifor the breast, on the breastn. loc. sg. of उरस् (s-stem)
  11. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  12. अपराम्aparāmanother (garland)f. acc. sg. of the pronominal adjective अपर; माला is understood

Translation

missiles of many forms, and likewise an impenetrable armour; a garland of unfading lotuses for the head, and another for the breast —

In readable Englishmissiles of many kinds and armour that nothing can pierce. A garland of unfading lotuses for her head and another for her breast

The second line is the object of ‘the Ocean gave’ in 2.29; the translation leaves the verb to that verse.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • अस्त्राण्यनेकरूपाणि astrāṇyanekarūpāṇi → अस्त्राणि + अनेकरूपाणि

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    Final i before a dissimilar vowel becomes y.

  • तथाभेद्यं tathābhedyaṃ → तथा + अभेद्यम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā.

  • शिरस्युरसि śirasyurasi → शिरसि + उरसि

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    i + u → yu.

  • चापराम् cāparām → च + अपराम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अनेकरूपाणि anekarūpāṇi

    bahuvrīhi (अनेक itself a nañ tatpuruṣa, न एकम्)

    Members
    अनेक + रूप
    Vigraha
    अनेकानि रूपाणि येषां तानि anekāni rūpāṇi yeṣāṃ tāni
    Literally
    “having not-one forms”
    In the verse
    of many kinds

  • अम्लानपङ्कजाम् amlānapaṅkajām

    bahuvrīhi; अम्लान is a nañ tatpuruṣa (न म्लानम्, √म्लै + kta) and पङ्कज an upapada tatpuruṣa (पङ्के जायते इति)

    Members
    अम्लान + पङ्कज
    Vigraha
    अम्लानानि पङ्कजानि यस्याः सा, ताम् amlānāni paṅkajāni yasyāḥ sā, tām
    Literally
    “whose mud-born (flowers) are unwithered”
    In the verse
    a garland of lotuses that never fade
Morphology & anvaya

  • अभेद्यम् abhedyam

    Gerundive (ṇyat) of √भिद् with the negative prefix: ‘not to be split’ — the armour cannot be pierced.

  • शिरसि śirasi

    Locative of the s-stem शिरस्, like उरसि from उरस्: the locative tells where each garland is to be worn.

Anvaya · prose order

(विश्वकर्मा) अनेकरूपाणि अस्त्राणि तथा अभेद्यं दंशनं च (ददौ)। (जलधिः) शिरसि अम्लानपङ्कजां मालाम्, उरसि च अपरां (मालाम्, अददत्)।

(viśvakarmā) anekarūpāṇi astrāṇi tathā abhedyaṃ daṃśanaṃ ca (dadau) | (jaladhiḥ) śirasi amlānapaṅkajāṃ mālām, urasi ca aparāṃ (mālām, adadat) |

The first line completes Viśvakarman’s gift (verb ददौ in 2.27); the second line lists objects of अददत्, whose subject जलधिः stands in 2.29.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Viśvakarman’s gifts end with missiles and impenetrable armour. The garlands — one for the head, one for the breast — belong to the next giver, the Ocean, named in 2.29.

Traditional interpretation

Unfading lotuses (अम्लान) are a stock sign of the divine: what the gods wear does not wither. The lotus also stands in the madhyama-carita dhyāna (पद्मं), and she is pictured there as seated on a lotus (सरोजस्थिताम्).

Verse 2.29

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

अददज्जलधिस्तस्यै पङ्कजं चातिशोभनम्।हिमवान् वाहनं सिंहं रत्नानि विविधानि च॥⁠२९⁠॥

adadajjaladhistasyai paṅkajaṃ cātiśobhanam |himavān vāhanaṃ siṃhaṃ ratnāni vividhāni ca ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

अददत् · जलधिः · तस्यै · पङ्कजम् · च · अतिशोभनम्हिमवान् · वाहनम् · सिंहम् · रत्नानि · विविधानि · च

adadat · jaladhiḥ · tasyai · paṅkajam · ca · atiśobhanamhimavān · vāhanam · siṃham · ratnāni · vividhāni · ca

Word by word

  1. अददत्adadatgave√दा (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to give’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; an epic thematic form for classical अददात्
  2. जलधिःjaladhiḥthe Oceanm. nom. sg. of जलधि; compound (see Samāsa); subject of अददत्
  3. तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.
  4. पङ्कजम्paṅkajama lotusn. acc. sg. of पङ्कज (‘mud-born’); object
  5. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); adds the lotus to the garlands of 2.28
  6. अतिशोभनम्atiśobhanamexceedingly beautifuln. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies पङ्कजम्
  7. हिमवान्himavānHimavat, the Himālayam. nom. sg. of हिमवत् (हिम + possessive matup, ‘having snow’); subject of a supplied ददौ / अददत्
  8. वाहनम्vāhanamas a mount, a vehiclen. acc. sg. of वाहन (√वह् causative + lyuṭ, ‘that which carries’); predicative to सिंहम्
  9. सिंहम्siṃhama lionm. acc. sg. of सिंह; object
  10. रत्नानिratnānijewelsn. acc. pl. of रत्न; object
  11. विविधानिvividhāniof various kindsn. acc. pl. of विविध; qualifies रत्नानि
  12. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)

Translation

the Ocean gave to her, and an exceedingly beautiful lotus. Himavat (gave) a lion as mount, and various jewels.

In readable Englishthe Ocean gave her, with a lotus of surpassing beauty. Himavat gave her a lion to ride, and jewels of every kind.

‘Gave’ is supplied for Himavat.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • अददज्जलधिस्तस्यै adadajjaladhistasyai → अददत् + जलधिः + तस्यै

    consonant sandhi (palatalisation) and visarga sandhi · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40) with झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Final t before j becomes j (t voiced to d, and the dental made palatal next to the palatal j): अददज्जलधिः. Then the visarga before t becomes s: जलधिस्तस्यै.

  • चातिशोभनम् cātiśobhanam → च + अतिशोभनम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • हिमवान् वाहनं himavān vāhanaṃ → हिमवान् + वाहनम्

    no change · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) for वाहनं; final n before v is unchanged

    Final n before v stays n; only the m of वाहनम् becomes anusvāra before s (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • जलधिः jaladhiḥ

    upapada tatpuruṣa (√धा + ki)

    Members
    जल + धि
    Vigraha
    जलानि धीयन्ते अस्मिन् इति jalāni dhīyante asmin iti
    Literally
    “where the waters are held”
    In the verse
    the ocean

  • अतिशोभनम् atiśobhanam

    tatpuruṣa (prādi)

    Members
    अति + शोभन
    Vigraha
    अतिशयेन शोभनम् atiśayena śobhanam
    Literally
    “beyond beautiful”
    In the verse
    exceedingly lovely
Morphology & anvaya

  • अददत् adadat

    Imperfect of √दा. Classical grammar has अददात् (reduplicated stem ददा-); the epics often inflect √दा on a thematic stem दद-, giving अददत्. The metre (eight syllables, अ-द-दज्-ज-ल-धिस्-तस्-यै) is unaffected either way, so this is a matter of usage, not of metre.

  • हिमवान् himavān

    Nominative singular of the possessive -vat stem हिमवत्: the vowel is lengthened and n inserted (-वान्), as in भगवान्.

Anvaya · prose order

जलधिः तस्यै (शिरसि अम्लानपङ्कजां मालाम्, उरसि च अपराम्,) अतिशोभनं पङ्कजं च अददत्। हिमवान् वाहनं सिंहं विविधानि रत्नानि च (ददौ)।

jaladhiḥ tasyai (śirasi amlānapaṅkajāṃ mālām, urasi ca aparām,) atiśobhanaṃ paṅkajaṃ ca adadat | himavān vāhanaṃ siṃhaṃ vividhāni ratnāni ca (dadau) |

अददत् governs the garlands of 2.28b and the lotus; Himavat’s verb is supplied. वाहनम् is predicative: ‘a lion as her mount’.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The Ocean (जलधि, distinct from the Ocean of Milk of 2.25) gives garlands and a lotus. Himavat gives the lion that will carry her and fight beside her (2.51; it battles Cāmara in 3.14–16).

Traditional interpretation

Himavat is, in Purāṇic tradition, the father of Pārvatī; here the mountain simply gives what belongs to the mountains — the lion and its jewels. The lion-mount gives her the name सिंहवाहिनी in 2.34.

Verse 2.30

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ददावशून्यं सुरया पानपात्रं धनाधिपः।शेषश्च सर्वनागेशो महामणिविभूषितम्॥⁠३०⁠॥

dadāvaśūnyaṃ surayā pānapātraṃ dhanādhipaḥ |śeṣaśca sarvanāgeśo mahāmaṇivibhūṣitam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ददौ · अशून्यम् · सुरया · पानपात्रम् · धनाधिपःशेषः · च · सर्वनागेशः · महामणिविभूषितम्

dadau · aśūnyam · surayā · pānapātram · dhanādhipaḥśeṣaḥ · ca · sarvanāgeśaḥ · mahāmaṇivibhūṣitam

Word by word

  1. ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3U), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  2. अशून्यम्aśūnyamnever emptyn. acc. sg. of अशून्य (negative न + शून्य); qualifies पानपात्रम्
  3. सुरयाsurayāof wine, with winef. instr. sg. of सुरा; construed with अशून्यम् (‘not empty of wine’)
  4. पानपात्रम्pānapātrama drinking cupn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of ददौ
  5. धनाधिपःdhanādhipaḥthe Lord of Wealth (Kubera)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of ददौ
  6. शेषःśeṣaḥŚeṣa, the cosmic serpentm. nom. sg. of शेष; subject of ददौ in 2.31
  7. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  8. सर्वनागेशःsarvanāgeśaḥlord of all serpentsm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to शेषः
  9. महामणिविभूषितम्mahāmaṇivibhūṣitamadorned with great jewelsm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies नागहारम् in 2.31

Translation

The Lord of Wealth gave a drinking-cup never empty of wine. And Śeṣa, the lord of all serpents, (gave) a … adorned with great jewels

In readable EnglishKubera, Lord of Wealth, gave her a drinking-cup never empty of wine; and Śeṣa, king of all serpents, a jewel-studded

The second line runs into 2.31, where the noun (नागहारम्) and the verb stand.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ददावशून्यं dadāvaśūnyaṃ → ददौ + अशून्यम्

    vowel sandhi (ayādi) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)

    Final au before a vowel becomes āv: ददौ + अ- → ददाव-.

  • धनाधिपः dhanādhipaḥ → धन + अधिपः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • शेषश्च śeṣaśca → शेषः + च

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c → s → ś.

  • सर्वनागेशो महामणि sarvanāgeśo mahāmaṇi → सर्वनागेशः + महामणि-

    visarga sandhi; and guṇa inside the compound · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced m becomes -o. Inside the compound नाग + ईश → नागेश (a + ī → e).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • पानपात्रम् pānapātram

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    पान + पात्र
    Vigraha
    पानस्य पात्रम् pānasya pātram
    Literally

    In the verse

    Alternative

  • धनाधिपः dhanādhipaḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members

    Vigraha
    धनानाम् अधिपः dhanānām adhipaḥ
    Literally

    In the verse
    Kubera, the god of wealth (compare कौबेरेण in 2.16)

  • सर्वनागेशः sarvanāgeśaḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (सर्वनाग a karmadhāraya, सर्वे च ते नागाः च)

    Members
    सर्वनाग + ईश
    Vigraha
    सर्वेषां नागानाम् ईशः sarveṣāṃ nāgānām īśaḥ
    Literally
    “lord of all the serpents”
    In the verse
    Śeṣa as king of the Nāgas

  • महामणिविभूषितम् mahāmaṇivibhūṣitam

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (महामणि a karmadhāraya, महान् चासौ मणिः च)

    Members
    महामणि + विभूषित
    Vigraha
    महामणिभिः विभूषितम् mahāmaṇibhiḥ vibhūṣitam
    Literally
    “adorned with great gems”
    In the verse
    jewel-studded
Morphology & anvaya

  • अशून्यम् aśūnyam

    A litotes: ‘not empty of wine’ means ‘always full’. The thing it is not empty of is in the instrumental (सुरया).

  • महामणिविभूषितम् mahāmaṇivibhūṣitam

    Accusative, though it stands beside the nominatives शेषः … सर्वनागेशः: it anticipates नागहारम्, the object in the next verse.

Anvaya · prose order

धनाधिपः सुरया अशून्यं पानपात्रं ददौ। सर्वनागेशः शेषः च महामणिविभूषितं (नागहारं तस्यै ददौ)।

dhanādhipaḥ surayā aśūnyaṃ pānapātraṃ dadau | sarvanāgeśaḥ śeṣaḥ ca mahāmaṇivibhūṣitaṃ (nāgahāraṃ tasyai dadau) |

The first line is complete. The second line begins a sentence finished in 2.31: Śeṣa is its subject, and the accusative adjective waits for its noun नागहारम्.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Kubera, keeper of the gods’ treasure, gives a cup that is never empty of wine. The cup returns in battle: before slaying Mahiṣa she drinks and says ‘Roar, roar for a moment, fool, while I drink the honey-wine’ (3.38).

Traditional interpretation

The wine-vessel is among the eighteen objects of the madhyama-carita dhyāna (सुराभाजनम्, Gita Press pāṭha). In the Śākta tradition the cup is commonly read as the Goddess’s exhilaration in battle, not as an ordinary indulgence; the text itself says only what is given.

Verse 2.31

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

नागहारं ददौ तस्यै धत्ते यः पृथिवीमिमाम्।अन्यैरपि सुरैर्देवी भूषणैरायुधैस्तथा॥⁠३१⁠॥

nāgahāraṃ dadau tasyai dhatte yaḥ pṛthivīmimām |anyairapi surairdevī bhūṣaṇairāyudhaistathā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

नागहारम् · ददौ · तस्यै · धत्ते · यः · पृथिवीम् · इमाम्अन्यैः · अपि · सुरैः · देवी · भूषणैः · आयुधैः · तथा

nāgahāram · dadau · tasyai · dhatte · yaḥ · pṛthivīm · imāmanyaiḥ · api · suraiḥ · devī · bhūṣaṇaiḥ · āyudhaiḥ · tathā

Word by word

  1. नागहारम्nāgahārama serpent-necklacem. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of ददौ
  2. ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3U), liṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; subject शेषः (2.30)
  3. तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.
  4. धत्तेdhattebears, holds up√धा (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to place, hold’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  5. यःyaḥwhorelative pronoun यद्, m. nom. sg.; refers to Śeṣa
  6. पृथिवीम्pṛthivīmthe earthf. acc. sg. of पृथिवी; object of धत्ते
  7. इमाम्imāmthispronoun इदम्, f. acc. sg.; qualifies पृथिवीम्
  8. अन्यैःanyaiḥby otherpronominal adjective अन्य, m. instr. pl.; qualifies सुरैः
  9. अपिapialso, tooindeclinable (particle)
  10. सुरैःsuraiḥby the godsm. instr. pl. of सुर; agent of सम्मानिता (2.32)
  11. देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी; subject of सम्मानिता … ननाद (2.32)
  12. भूषणैःbhūṣaṇaiḥwith ornamentsn. instr. pl. of भूषण; instrument
  13. आयुधैःāyudhaiḥwith weaponsn. instr. pl. of आयुध; instrument
  14. तथाtathāand likewiseindeclinable (connective)

Translation

serpent-necklace he gave to her — he who bears this earth. By other gods too, with ornaments and with weapons likewise, the Goddess —

In readable Englishserpent-necklace — Śeṣa, who bears this earth. And the Goddess, honoured by the other gods as well with ornaments and weapons,

The second line is completed by सम्मानिता in 2.32; ‘honoured’ is brought forward for English order.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • पृथिवीमिमाम् pṛthivīmimām → पृथिवीम् + इमाम्

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    Final m is written together with the following vowel.

  • अन्यैरपि anyairapi → अन्यैः + अपि

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    Visarga after a vowel other than a/ā, before a vowel or voiced consonant, appears as r: अन्यैरपि, सुरैर्देवी, भूषणैरायुधैः.

  • आयुधैस्तथा āyudhaistathā → आयुधैः + तथा

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • नागहारम् nāgahāram

    karmadhāraya (of identification: the serpent is the necklace)

    Members
    नाग + हार
    Vigraha
    नागः एव हारः (तम्) nāgaḥ eva hāraḥ (tam)
    Literally
    “the serpent-necklace”
    In the verse
    a necklace that is a serpent, given by the serpent-king
    Alternative
    Also ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, नागानां हारः ‘the necklace of the Nāgas’ — a jewelled necklace from their treasury. महामणिविभूषितम् (2.30) fits either: Nāgas are known for the jewels on their hoods.
Morphology & anvaya

  • धत्ते dhatte

    Present ātmanepada of √धा: reduplicated दध्- + ते, with the aspiration moved forward to the first syllable (दध् + ते → धत्ते). The present tense describes Śeṣa’s permanent office: he who bears this earth.

  • देवी devī

    The nominative here is the subject of the next verse’s सम्मानिता … ननाद; the instrumentals सुरैः, भूषणैः, आयुधैः are the agent and the means of that participle.

Anvaya · prose order

यः इमां पृथिवीं धत्ते, (सः शेषः) तस्यै नागहारं ददौ। अन्यैः अपि सुरैः भूषणैः तथा आयुधैः देवी (सम्मानिता)।

yaḥ imāṃ pṛthivīṃ dhatte, (saḥ śeṣaḥ) tasyai nāgahāraṃ dadau | anyaiḥ api suraiḥ bhūṣaṇaiḥ tathā āyudhaiḥ devī (sammānitā) |

The first line closes the sentence begun in 2.30 with a relative clause on Śeṣa. The second line opens a new sentence whose participle and verb (सम्मानिता … ननाद) stand in 2.32.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Śeṣa, the serpent who holds up the earth, gives a jewelled serpent-necklace. Then the list is closed with a summary: the other gods too honoured her with ornaments and weapons.

Traditional interpretation

Śākta readers see in the catalogue of gifts (2.20–31) the gods handing back to the Goddess powers that are hers: several weapons are drawn out of the gods’ own weapons (2.20–23), as her body was drawn out of their light (2.13–18).

Verse 2.32

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

सम्मानिता ननादोच्चैः साट्टहासं मुहुर्मुहुः।तस्या नादेन घोरेण कृत्स्नमापूरितं नभः॥⁠३२⁠॥

sammānitā nanādoccaiḥ sāṭṭahāsaṃ muhurmuhuḥ |tasyā nādena ghoreṇa kṛtsnamāpūritaṃ nabhaḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सम्मानिता · ननाद · उच्चैः · साट्टहासम् · मुहुः · मुहुःतस्याः · नादेन · घोरेण · कृत्स्नम् · आपूरितम् · नभः

sammānitā · nanāda · uccaiḥ · sāṭṭahāsam · muhuḥ · muhuḥtasyāḥ · nādena · ghoreṇa · kṛtsnam · āpūritam · nabhaḥ

Word by word

  1. सम्मानिताsammānitāhonouredf. nom. sg. of सम्मानित (सम् + √मन्, causative मानय- ‘to honour’, + kta); agrees with देवी (2.31)
  2. ननादnanādaroared√नद् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to sound, roar’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  3. उच्चैःuccaiḥloudlyindeclinable (adverb, ‘aloud, high’)
  4. साट्टहासम्sāṭṭahāsamwith loud laughterindeclinable (adverbial n. acc. sg.); compound (see Samāsa)
  5. मुहुःmuhuḥagainindeclinable (adverb)
  6. मुहुःmuhuḥand againindeclinable; repeated (āmreḍita) for frequency: ‘again and again’
  7. तस्याःtasyāḥherpronoun तद्, f. gen. sg.
  8. नादेनnādenaby the roarm. instr. sg. of नाद; agent of आपूरितम्
  9. घोरेणghoreṇaterriblem. instr. sg. of घोर; qualifies नादेन
  10. कृत्स्नम्kṛtsnamthe wholen. nom. sg. of कृत्स्न; qualifies नभः
  11. आपूरितम्āpūritamwas filledn. nom. sg. of आपूरित (आ + √पूर्, causative/curādi पूरय- ‘to fill’, + kta); predicate (भूतम् supplied)
  12. नभःnabhaḥthe skyn. nom. sg. of नभस् (s-stem); subject

Translation

honoured, roared loudly with loud laughter again and again. By her terrible roar the whole sky was filled.

In readable Englishroared aloud again and again, with peals of laughter. The whole sky was filled with her terrible roar.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ननादोच्चैः nanādoccaiḥ → ननाद + उच्चैः

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

  • मुहुर्मुहुः muhurmuhuḥ → मुहुः + मुहुः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    Visarga after u before the voiced m appears as r.

  • तस्या नादेन tasyā nādena → तस्याः + नादेन

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced sound: the visarga becomes y, which is then dropped before a consonant, leaving -ā.

  • कृत्स्नमापूरितं kṛtsnamāpūritaṃ → कृत्स्नम् + आपूरितम्

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    Final m joins the following vowel; the m of आपूरितम् becomes anusvāra before n.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • साट्टहासम् sāṭṭahāsam

    bahuvrīhi (with सह, which becomes स-), used adverbially in the neuter accusative; अट्टहास is a karmadhāraya, अट्टः (‘excessive’) हासः

    Members
    सह + अट्टहास
    Vigraha
    अट्टहासेन सह वर्तते यथा तथा aṭṭahāsena saha vartate yathā tathā
    Literally
    “accompanied by loud laughter”
    In the verse
    with peals of laughter
    Alternative
    Also explained as an avyayībhāva with सह (‘together with laughter’); either way the form is adverbial and the sense the same.
Morphology & anvaya

  • ननाद nanāda

    Perfect of √नद् with reduplication न-नाद (the root vowel lengthened in the 3rd singular). The same root gives नाद ‘roar’ in the next line.

  • आपूरितम् āpūritam

    Past participle of the causative stem पूरय- (‘to fill’) with आ ‘completely’. It is a passive participle used as a finite verb: ‘(the sky) was filled’.

Anvaya · prose order

(अन्यैः अपि सुरैः भूषणैः आयुधैः तथा) सम्मानिता (देवी) साट्टहासं मुहुः मुहुः उच्चैः ननाद। तस्याः घोरेण नादेन कृत्स्नं नभः आपूरितम्।

(anyaiḥ api suraiḥ bhūṣaṇaiḥ āyudhaiḥ tathā) sammānitā (devī) sāṭṭahāsaṃ muhuḥ muhuḥ uccaiḥ nanāda | tasyāḥ ghoreṇa nādena kṛtsnaṃ nabhaḥ āpūritam |

Two sentences: the first finishes 2.31b (देवी … सम्मानिता ननाद); the second is passive, with the roar as agent.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Armed and adorned, the Goddess’s first act is a roar mixed with laughter. The roar fills the sky; the next two verses follow its effect through the worlds.

Traditional interpretation

Her laughter in the face of battle is a recurring mark of this carita: she fights without effort and is unwearied (अनायस्तानना, 2.50). Laughter here shows sovereignty, not mockery.

Verse 2.33

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

अमायतातिमहता प्रतिशब्दो महानभूत् ।चुक्षुभुः सकला लोकाः समुद्राश्च चकम्पिरे॥⁠३३⁠॥

amāyatātimahatā pratiśabdo mahānabhūt |cukṣubhuḥ sakalā lokāḥ samudrāśca cakampire ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

अमायता · अतिमहता · प्रतिशब्दः · महान् · अभूत्चुक्षुभुः · सकलाः · लोकाः · समुद्राः · च · चकम्पिरे

amāyatā · atimahatā · pratiśabdaḥ · mahān · abhūtcukṣubhuḥ · sakalāḥ · lokāḥ · samudrāḥ · ca · cakampire

Word by word

  1. अमायताamāyatāuncontainable, that no space could holdm. instr. sg. of अमायत्, the negative (न) of मायत्, present participle of √मा ‘to be measured, to be contained’ (on the stem माय-); qualifies the roar (नादेन, 2.32) understood
  2. अतिमहताatimahatāby the exceedingly great (roar)m. instr. sg. of अतिमहत् (prādi compound, ‘very great’); instrument of cause
  3. प्रतिशब्दःpratiśabdaḥan echom. nom. sg. of प्रतिशब्द (प्रति + शब्द, ‘counter-sound’); subject of अभूत्
  4. महान्mahāngreat, mightym. nom. sg. of महत्; qualifies प्रतिशब्दः
  5. अभूत्abhūtarose, there was√भू, luṅ (aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  6. चुक्षुभुःcukṣubhuḥwere shaken√क्षुभ् (divādi, 4P ‘to be agitated’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  7. सकलाःsakalāḥallm. nom. pl. of सकल; qualifies लोकाः
  8. लोकाःlokāḥthe worldsm. nom. pl. of लोक; subject of चुक्षुभुः
  9. समुद्राःsamudrāḥthe oceansm. nom. pl. of समुद्र; subject of चकम्पिरे
  10. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  11. चकम्पिरेcakampiretrembled√कम्प् (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to tremble’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, ātmanepada

Translation

By the uncontainable, exceedingly great (roar) a mighty echo arose. All the worlds were shaken, and the oceans trembled.

In readable EnglishFrom that boundless, immense roar a mighty echo rang out; all the worlds were shaken and the oceans trembled.

Textual verification / uncertain: अमायता is taken as the instrumental of a negated present participle (‘uncontainable’). The same letters can be divided अमायत अतिमहता, with अमायत an imperfect ātmanepada of √मा (‘it was measured / contained’); but that leaves the clause without a negative and without a clear subject, so the participle reading is followed.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • अमायतातिमहता amāyatātimahatā → अमायता + अतिमहता

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā. The same spelling also results from अमायत + अतिमहता (see Flags).

  • प्रतिशब्दो महानभूत् pratiśabdo mahānabhūt → प्रतिशब्दः + महान् + अभूत्

    visarga sandhi; final n before a vowel · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced m → -o. The n of महान् is not doubled before अभूत् because the vowel before it is long (ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम्, 8.3.32, needs a short vowel).

  • सकला लोकाः sakalā lokāḥ → सकलाः + लोकाः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced consonant loses its visarga.

  • समुद्राश्च samudrāśca → समुद्राः + च

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c → ś.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अतिमहता atimahatā

    tatpuruṣa (prādi)

    Members
    अति + महत्
    Vigraha
    अतिशयेन महत्, तेन atiśayena mahat, tena
    Literally
    “beyond great”
    In the verse
    immense
Morphology & anvaya

  • अमायता amāyatā

    Instrumental of a negated present participle: ‘(by the roar) not being contained’, i.e. a sound that no space could hold. It and अतिमहता qualify the roar of 2.32, which is not repeated.

  • चुक्षुभुः cukṣubhuḥ

    Perfect 3rd plural of √क्षुभ्: reduplication चु- (k → c in the reduplicating syllable) + क्षुभ् + -उः.

  • चकम्पिरे cakampire

    Perfect 3rd plural ātmanepada (-इरे) of √कम्प्; contrast parasmaipada चुक्षुभुः beside it.

Anvaya · prose order

अमायता अतिमहता (नादेन) महान् प्रतिशब्दः अभूत्। सकलाः लोकाः चुक्षुभुः, समुद्राः च चकम्पिरे।

amāyatā atimahatā (nādena) mahān pratiśabdaḥ abhūt | sakalāḥ lokāḥ cukṣubhuḥ, samudrāḥ ca cakampire |

Three clauses: the echo caused by the roar (instrumental of cause), the worlds shaken, the seas trembling.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The roar is too great to be held anywhere; its echo sets the worlds and oceans shaking. The effects are listed outward: sky (2.32), worlds and seas (2.33), earth and mountains (2.34).

Traditional interpretation

The roar is the first sign the asuras receive of her: it is this sound that Mahiṣa rushes towards in 2.37 (अभ्यधावत तं शब्दम्).

Verse 2.34

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

चचाल वसुधा चेलुः सकलाश्च महीधराः।जयेति देवाश्च मुदा तामूचुः सिंहवाहिनीम्॥⁠३४⁠॥

cacāla vasudhā celuḥ sakalāśca mahīdharāḥ |jayeti devāśca mudā tāmūcuḥ siṃhavāhinīm ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

चचाल · वसुधा · चेलुः · सकलाः · च · महीधराःजय · इति · देवाः · च · मुदा · ताम् · ऊचुः · सिंहवाहिनीम्

cacāla · vasudhā · celuḥ · sakalāḥ · ca · mahīdharāḥjaya · iti · devāḥ · ca · mudā · tām · ūcuḥ · siṃhavāhinīm

Word by word

  1. चचालcacālashook√चल् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to move, shake’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  2. वसुधाvasudhāthe earthf. nom. sg. of वसुधा; compound (see Samāsa); subject of चचाल
  3. चेलुःceluḥquaked√चल्, liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  4. सकलाःsakalāḥallm. nom. pl. of सकल; qualifies महीधराः
  5. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  6. महीधराःmahīdharāḥthe mountainsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of चेलुः
  7. जयjayabe victorious! victory!√जि (bhvādi, 1P ‘to conquer’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. इतिitithus (closing the quotation)indeclinable (particle of quotation)
  9. देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of ऊचुः
  10. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  11. मुदाmudāwith joyf. instr. sg. of मुद् (consonant stem); instrument of manner
  12. ताम्tāmto herpronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; the person addressed (√वच् takes two accusatives)
  13. ऊचुःūcuḥsaid, cried√वच् (adādi, 2P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  14. सिंहवाहिनीम्siṃhavāhinīmher who rides the lionf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to ताम्

Translation

The earth shook, and all the mountains quaked. And the gods with joy said ‘Be victorious!’ to her, the lion-rider.

In readable EnglishThe earth shook and all the mountains quaked, and the gods cried out in joy to the lion-rider, ‘Victory to you!’

‘Victory to you’ renders the imperative जय (‘be victorious’).

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सकलाश्च sakalāśca → सकलाः + च

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c → ś; the same in देवाश्च.

  • जयेति jayeti → जय + इति

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + i → e.

  • तामूचुः tāmūcuḥ → ताम् + ऊचुः

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    Final m joins the following vowel.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • वसुधा vasudhā

    upapada tatpuruṣa (√धा + kvip-type affix)

    Members
    वसु + धा
    Vigraha
    वसूनि दधाति इति vasūni dadhāti iti
    Literally
    “she who holds wealth”
    In the verse
    the earth

  • महीधराः mahīdharāḥ

    upapada tatpuruṣa (√धृ + ac)

    Members
    मही + धर
    Vigraha
    महीं धरन्ति इति mahīṃ dharanti iti
    Literally
    “earth-holders”
    In the verse
    the mountains, which hold the earth firm

  • सिंहवाहिनीम् siṃhavāhinīm

    upapada tatpuruṣa (√वह् + ṇini, feminine in -ī)

    Members
    सिंह + वाहिनी
    Vigraha
    सिंहेन वहति इति सिंहवाहिनी, ताम् siṃhena vahati iti siṃhavāhinī, tām
    Literally
    “she who travels by a lion”
    In the verse
    the lion-rider — the lion just given by Himavat (2.29)
    Alternative
    Often glossed as सिंहः वाहनं यस्याः सा, a bahuvrīhi ‘she whose mount is a lion’; strictly that analysis gives सिंहवाहना, and the -इनी ending marks the upapada formation.
Morphology & anvaya

  • चेलुः celuḥ

    Perfect 3rd plural of √चल् without reduplication: a root of the shape C-a-C with a single consonant on each side substitutes e for a and drops the reduplication in the weak forms (चेलुः, not *चचलुः). Contrast the singular चचाल at the start of the line.

  • ऊचुः ūcuḥ

    Perfect 3rd plural of √वच् with samprasāraṇa: व → उ, and the reduplicated उ + उ gives ऊ (ऊचुः ‘they said’); compare उवाच.

  • जय jaya

    Imperative 2nd singular of √जि on the stem जय-: the acclamation ‘Be victorious!’, quoted with इति.

Anvaya · prose order

वसुधा चचाल, सकलाः महीधराः च चेलुः। देवाः च मुदा तां सिंहवाहिनीं ‘जय’ इति ऊचुः।

vasudhā cacāla, sakalāḥ mahīdharāḥ ca celuḥ | devāḥ ca mudā tāṃ siṃhavāhinīṃ ‘jaya’ iti ūcuḥ |

Two clauses of upheaval, then the gods’ acclamation: ‘जय’ इति is the quoted word, ताम् the person addressed.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The roar reaches the earth and the mountains, and the gods answer it with one word: जय. सिंहवाहिनी is the first epithet the gods give her after her arming.

Traditional interpretation

जय, ‘be victorious’, is at once an acclamation and a prayer. The same imperative is the refrain of the Argalā Stotra recited before the text in the Gita Press pāṭha (जय त्वं देवि चामुण्डे …), a separate liturgical hymn.

Verse 2.35

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तुष्टुवुर्मुनयश्चैनां भक्तिनम्रात्ममूर्तयः।दृष्ट्वा समस्तं संक्षुब्धं त्रैलोक्यममरारयः॥⁠३५⁠॥

tuṣṭuvurmunayaścaināṃ bhaktinamrātmamūrtayaḥ |dṛṣṭvā samastaṃ saṃkṣubdhaṃ trailokyamamarārayaḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तुष्टुवुः · मुनयः · च · एनाम् · भक्तिनम्रात्ममूर्तयःदृष्ट्वा · समस्तम् · संक्षुब्धम् · त्रैलोक्यम् · अमरारयः

tuṣṭuvuḥ · munayaḥ · ca · enām · bhaktinamrātmamūrtayaḥdṛṣṭvā · samastam · saṃkṣubdham · trailokyam · amarārayaḥ

Word by word

  1. तुष्टुवुःtuṣṭuvuḥpraised√स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  2. मुनयःmunayaḥthe sagesm. nom. pl. of मुनि (i-stem); subject of तुष्टुवुः
  3. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); joins the sages to the gods of 2.34
  4. एनाम्enāmherpronoun एनद् (the unemphatic form of एतद्/इदम् used for something already mentioned), f. acc. sg.; object
  5. भक्तिनम्रात्ममूर्तयःbhaktinamrātmamūrtayaḥtheir bodies bowed in devotionm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मुनयः
  6. दृष्ट्वाdṛṣṭvāhaving seenabsolutive (ktvā) of √दृश् ‘to see’; its agent is अमरारयः
  7. समस्तम्samastamentire, the wholen. acc. sg. of समस्त; qualifies त्रैलोक्यम्
  8. संक्षुब्धम्saṃkṣubdhamin turmoil, shakenn. acc. sg. of संक्षुब्ध (सम् + √क्षुभ् + kta); predicative with त्रैलोक्यम्
  9. त्रैलोक्यम्trailokyamthe three worldsn. acc. sg. of त्रैलोक्य (taddhita from त्रिलोक, ‘the triad of worlds’); object of दृष्ट्वा
  10. अमरारयःamarārayaḥthe enemies of the immortals (the asuras)m. nom. pl. of अमरारि (i-stem); compound (see Samāsa); subject of समुत्तस्थुः (2.36)

Translation

And the sages, their own bodies bowed with devotion, praised her. Having seen the whole three worlds agitated, the enemies of the immortals —

In readable EnglishThe sages, bowing low in devotion, sang her praise. But the enemies of the gods, seeing the three worlds in turmoil,

‘But’ marks the change of scene, which the Sanskrit makes by a new subject; the sentence continues into 2.36.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तुष्टुवुर्मुनयश्चैनां tuṣṭuvurmunayaścaināṃ → तुष्टुवुः + मुनयः + च + एनाम्

    visarga sandhi and vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    Visarga after u before the voiced m appears as r (तुष्टुवुर्); visarga before c → ś (मुनयश्च); a + e → ai (चैनां).

  • भक्तिनम्रात्ममूर्तयः bhaktinamrātmamūrtayaḥ → भक्तिनम्र + आत्ममूर्तयः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • त्रैलोक्यममरारयः trailokyamamarārayaḥ → त्रैलोक्यम् + अमरारयः

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    Final m joins the following vowel; inside the compound अमर + अरि → अमरारि (a + a → ā, 6.1.101).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • भक्तिनम्रात्ममूर्तयः bhaktinamrātmamūrtayaḥ

    bahuvrīhi, containing a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (भक्तिनम्र = भक्त्या नम्रः) and a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (आत्ममूर्ति = आत्मनः मूर्तिः)

    Members
    भक्तिनम्र + आत्ममूर्ति
    Vigraha
    भक्त्या नम्राः आत्ममूर्तयः येषां ते bhaktyā namrāḥ ātmamūrtayaḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “they whose own bodies were bent by devotion”
    In the verse
    the sages, bowing low in devotion

  • अमरारयः amarārayaḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (अमर itself न म्रियते इति, ‘undying’)

    Members
    अमर + अरि
    Vigraha
    अमराणाम् अरयः amarāṇām arayaḥ
    Literally
