    “the foes of the immortals”
    In the verse
    the asuras
Morphology & anvaya

  • तुष्टुवुः tuṣṭuvuḥ

    Perfect 3rd plural of √स्तु: reduplication तु- with the root’s s made ṣ after the reduplicating u (तु-ष्टु-), and u → uv before the ending -उः.

  • एनाम् enām

    The enclitic pronoun एन- is used only for a person or thing already named (anvādeśa): ‘her’, the Goddess of 2.34.

  • दृष्ट्वा dṛṣṭvā

    Absolutive of √दृश् (ś + tvā → ṣṭvā). Its subject is the subject of the main verb: the asuras saw, and then rose (2.36).

Anvaya · prose order

भक्तिनम्रात्ममूर्तयः मुनयः च एनां तुष्टुवुः। अमरारयः समस्तं त्रैलोक्यं संक्षुब्धं दृष्ट्वा (2.36: सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्याः समुत्तस्थुः)।

bhaktinamrātmamūrtayaḥ munayaḥ ca enāṃ tuṣṭuvuḥ | amarārayaḥ samastaṃ trailokyaṃ saṃkṣubdhaṃ dṛṣṭvā (2.36: sannaddhākhilasainyāḥ samuttasthuḥ) |

The first line closes the scene of praise. The second line starts a new sentence: the absolutive clause with its subject अमरारयः, whose main verb comes in 2.36.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Two answers to the roar. The sages praise her with bowed bodies; the asuras, seeing the worlds in upheaval, prepare for war (2.36). The same event is heard as grace by the one side and as threat by the other.

Modern reflection

A modern reflection: the verse sets reverence and alarm side by side as responses to one and the same power. What a person sees in an upheaval says something about where they stand.

Verse 2.36

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्यास्ते समुत्तस्थुरुदायुधाः।आः किमेतदिति क्रोधादाभाष्य महिषासुरः॥⁠३६⁠॥

sannaddhākhilasainyāste samuttasthurudāyudhāḥ |āḥ kimetaditi krodhādābhāṣya mahiṣāsuraḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्याः · ते · समुत्तस्थुः · उदायुधाःआः · किम् · एतत् · इति · क्रोधात् · आभाष्य · महिषासुरः

sannaddhākhilasainyāḥ · te · samuttasthuḥ · udāyudhāḥāḥ · kim · etat · iti · krodhāt · ābhāṣya · mahiṣāsuraḥ

Word by word

  1. सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्याःsannaddhākhilasainyāḥwith all their armies armed and readym. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ते
  2. तेtetheypronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; resumes अमरारयः (2.35)
  3. समुत्तस्थुःsamuttasthuḥrose upसम् + उद् + √स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  4. उदायुधाःudāyudhāḥwith weapons raisedm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ते
  5. आःāḥAh! (in anger)indeclinable (interjection of anger or surprise)
  6. किम्kimwhat?interrogative pronoun किम्, n. nom. sg.
  7. एतत्etat(is) thispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.
  8. इतिitithus (closing the quotation)indeclinable (particle of quotation)
  9. क्रोधात्krodhātin anger, out of angerm. abl. sg. of क्रोध; ablative of cause
  10. आभाष्यābhāṣyahaving exclaimedabsolutive (lyap) of आ + √भाष् ‘to speak, address’
  11. महिषासुरःmahiṣāsuraḥMahiṣāsuram. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of आभाष्य and of अभ्यधावत (2.37)

Translation

they, their entire armies armed, rose up with weapons raised. Having exclaimed in anger, ‘Ah! What is this?’, Mahiṣāsura —

In readable Englishrose up, weapons raised, their whole army in arms. ‘Ha! What is this?’ cried Mahiṣāsura in fury,

The absolutive आभाष्य (‘having exclaimed’) is rendered as a finite verb; the sentence runs on into 2.37.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्यास्ते sannaddhākhilasainyāste → सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्याः + ते

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s. Inside the compound सन्नद्ध + अखिल → सन्नद्धाखिल (a + a → ā, 6.1.101).

  • समुत्तस्थुरुदायुधाः samuttasthurudāyudhāḥ → समुत्तस्थुः + उदायुधाः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    Visarga after u before a vowel appears as r.

  • किमेतदिति kimetaditi → किम् + एतत् + इति

    final m before a vowel; consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final m joins the vowel; final t becomes d before the vowel of इति.

  • क्रोधादाभाष्य krodhādābhāṣya → क्रोधात् + आभाष्य

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t → d before a vowel.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्याः sannaddhākhilasainyāḥ

    bahuvrīhi (अखिल itself a nañ bahuvrīhi, न विद्यते खिलम् यस्य, ‘without remainder’)

    Members
    सन्नद्ध + अखिल + सैन्य
    Vigraha
    सन्नद्धानि अखिलानि सैन्यानि येषां ते sannaddhāni akhilāni sainyāni yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “they whose entire armies were girded”
    In the verse
    with their whole forces armed and in array

  • उदायुधाः udāyudhāḥ

    bahuvrīhi (prādi type: the prefix उद् stands for उद्यत ‘raised’)

    Members
    उद् + आयुध
    Vigraha
    उद्यतानि आयुधानि येषां ते udyatāni āyudhāni yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “they whose weapons were up”
    In the verse
    brandishing their weapons

  • महिषासुरः mahiṣāsuraḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महिष + असुर
    Vigraha
    महिषः च असौ असुरः च mahiṣaḥ ca asau asuraḥ ca
    Literally
    “the buffalo-asura”
    In the verse
    the asura who has the form of a buffalo
    Alternative
    Also read as a madhyamapadalopin karmadhāraya, महिषरूपः असुरः ‘the asura in buffalo form’; the sense is the same.
Morphology & anvaya

  • समुत्तस्थुः samuttasthuḥ

    Perfect of √स्था (reduplicated त-स्थ्-, the s dropped in the reduplicating syllable) with सम् + उद्. उद् + स्था loses its s (उत्था-), so the d of उद् appears as t: सम्-उत्-तस्थुः.

  • आभाष्य ābhāṣya

    Absolutive with a prefix takes -य (lyap), not -त्वा: आ + √भाष् → आभाष्य ‘having called out’.

Anvaya · prose order

(अमरारयः) ते सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्याः उदायुधाः समुत्तस्थुः। महिषासुरः ‘आः, किम् एतत्’ इति क्रोधात् आभाष्य (2.37: … अभ्यधावत)।

(amarārayaḥ) te sannaddhākhilasainyāḥ udāyudhāḥ samuttasthuḥ | mahiṣāsuraḥ ‘āḥ, kim etat’ iti krodhāt ābhāṣya (2.37: … abhyadhāvata) |

The first line completes 2.35b. The second gives Mahiṣāsura’s cry, quoted with इति, as an absolutive clause; his main verb is in 2.37.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The asuras arm themselves, and Mahiṣāsura appears in the narrative for the first time in person, with an angry question: ‘What is this?’ He does not yet know what the sound is.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: Mahiṣa meets the Goddess first as a disturbance to be put down — ‘What is this?’ — not as a power to be understood. The question is asked in anger, and anger does not wait for the answer.

Verse 2.37

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

अभ्यधावत तं शब्दमशेषैरसुरैर्वृतः।स ददर्श ततो देवीं व्याप्तलोकत्रयां त्विषा॥⁠३७⁠॥

abhyadhāvata taṃ śabdamaśeṣairasurairvṛtaḥ |sa dadarśa tato devīṃ vyāptalokatrayāṃ tviṣā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

अभ्यधावत · तम् · शब्दम् · अशेषैः · असुरैः · वृतःसः · ददर्श · ततः · देवीम् · व्याप्तलोकत्रयाम् · त्विषा

abhyadhāvata · tam · śabdam · aśeṣaiḥ · asuraiḥ · vṛtaḥsaḥ · dadarśa · tataḥ · devīm · vyāptalokatrayām · tviṣā

Word by word

  1. अभ्यधावतabhyadhāvatarushed towardsअभि + √धाव् (bhvādi, 1U ‘to run’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  2. तम्tamthatpronoun तद्, m. acc. sg.; qualifies शब्दम्
  3. शब्दम्śabdamsoundm. acc. sg. of शब्द; goal of अभ्यधावत
  4. अशेषैःaśeṣaiḥby all, by the whole host ofm. instr. pl. of अशेष (‘without remainder’); qualifies असुरैः
  5. असुरैःasuraiḥby the asurasm. instr. pl. of असुर; agent of वृतः
  6. वृतःvṛtaḥsurroundedpast participle (kta) of √वृ ‘to cover, surround’, m. nom. sg.; qualifies महिषासुरः (2.36)
  7. सःsaḥhepronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; subject of ददर्श
  8. ददर्शdadarśasaw√दृश् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to see’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
  10. देवीम्devīmthe Goddessf. acc. sg. of देवी; object of ददर्श
  11. व्याप्तलोकत्रयाम्vyāptalokatrayāmwho pervaded the three worldsf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवीम्
  12. त्विषाtviṣāwith her radiancef. instr. sg. of त्विष् (consonant stem ‘light, splendour’)

Translation

rushed towards that sound, surrounded by all the asuras. Then he saw the Goddess, who pervaded the three worlds with her radiance,

In readable Englishand charged towards the sound with all his asuras around him. Then he saw the Goddess, her radiance filling the three worlds,

The second sentence continues through 2.38 into 2.39a, all describing the Goddess as he sees her.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • शब्दमशेषैरसुरैर्वृतः śabdamaśeṣairasurairvṛtaḥ → शब्दम् + अशेषैः + असुरैः + वृतः

    final m before a vowel; visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    Final m joins the vowel; visarga after ai before a vowel or a voiced consonant appears as r (अशेषैरसुरैर्वृतः).

  • स ददर्श sa dadarśa → सः + ददर्श

    visarga sandhi (special rule for सः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)

    The visarga of सः is dropped before a consonant.

  • ततो देवीं tato devīṃ → ततः + देवीम्

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • व्याप्तलोकत्रयाम् vyāptalokatrayām

    bahuvrīhi (लोकत्रय a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, लोकानां त्रयम्)

    Members
    व्याप्त + लोकत्रय
    Vigraha
    व्याप्तं लोकत्रयं यया सा, ताम् vyāptaṃ lokatrayaṃ yayā sā, tām
    Literally
    “she by whom the triad of worlds is pervaded”
    In the verse
    filling the three worlds (with her light)
Morphology & anvaya

  • अभ्यधावत abhyadhāvata

    Imperfect ātmanepada of √धाव् with अभि (augment a- after the prefix: अभि + अ + धावत → अभ्यधावत). With a goal in the accusative: ‘ran at, charged towards’.

  • ददर्श dadarśa

    Perfect of √दृश् with guṇa of the root vowel (दर्श्) and reduplication द-: ‘he saw’.

Anvaya · prose order

(महिषासुरः) अशेषैः असुरैः वृतः तं शब्दम् अभ्यधावत। ततः सः त्विषा व्याप्तलोकत्रयां देवीं ददर्श।

(mahiṣāsuraḥ) aśeṣaiḥ asuraiḥ vṛtaḥ taṃ śabdam abhyadhāvata | tataḥ saḥ tviṣā vyāptalokatrayāṃ devīṃ dadarśa |

The first line gives Mahiṣāsura’s main verb (subject in 2.36); the second line is a new sentence whose object is described through 2.38–39a.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Mahiṣa runs towards the sound and only then sees its source. The description of her begins with the phrase used for the gods’ gathered light in 2.13 (व्याप्तलोकत्रयं त्विषा): the light that became her body still fills the worlds. The vision runs on through 2.38–39a.

Verse 2.38

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

पादाक्रान्त्या नतभुवं किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बराम्।क्षोभिताशेषपातालां धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेन ताम्॥⁠३८⁠॥

pādākrāntyā natabhuvaṃ kirīṭollikhitāmbarām |kṣobhitāśeṣapātālāṃ dhanurjyāniḥsvanena tām ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

पादाक्रान्त्या · नतभुवम् · किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बराम्क्षोभिताशेषपातालाम् · धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेन · ताम्

pādākrāntyā · natabhuvam · kirīṭollikhitāmbarāmkṣobhitāśeṣapātālām · dhanurjyāniḥsvanena · tām

Word by word

  1. पादाक्रान्त्याpādākrāntyāby the tread of her feetf. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); construed with नतभुवम्
  2. नतभुवम्natabhuvamby whom the earth was bowed downf. acc. sg. of नतभू; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ताम् / देवीम् (2.37)
  3. किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बराम्kirīṭollikhitāmbarāmwhose crown grazed the skyf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ताम्
  4. क्षोभिताशेषपातालाम्kṣobhitāśeṣapātālāmwho shook all the netherworldsf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ताम्
  5. धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेनdhanurjyāniḥsvanenawith the twang of her bowstringm. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); construed with क्षोभिताशेषपातालाम्
  6. ताम्tāmherpronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; resumes देवीम् (2.37)

Translation

her by whose tread the earth was bowed, by whose crown the sky was scraped, by the twang of whose bowstring all the netherworlds were shaken —

In readable Englishthe earth bending beneath her tread, her crown grazing the sky, the twang of her bowstring shaking the underworlds to their depths,

The verse continues the object of ददर्श (2.37); the next verse completes it.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • पादाक्रान्त्या pādākrāntyā → पाद + आक्रान्त्या

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बराम् kirīṭollikhitāmbarām → किरीट + उल्लिखित + अम्बराम्

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa and savarṇa-dīrgha); consonant sandhi inside the prefix · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); तोर्लि (8.4.60)

    a + u → o (किरीटोल्लिखित); a + a → ā (-ताम्बराम्). In उल्लिखित the d of उद् has become l before l (उद् + लिखित).

  • क्षोभिताशेषपातालां kṣobhitāśeṣapātālāṃ → क्षोभित + अशेषपातालाम्

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha); anusvāra · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    a + a → ā; the final m becomes anusvāra before the dh of the next word.

  • धनुर्ज्या dhanurjyā → धनुस् + ज्या

    visarga sandhi inside a compound · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    The s of धनुस् becomes r before the voiced j.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • पादाक्रान्त्या pādākrāntyā

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    पाद + आक्रान्ति
    Vigraha
    पादयोः आक्रान्तिः, तया pādayoḥ ākrāntiḥ, tayā
    Literally
    “by the stepping-on of the feet”
    In the verse
    by the weight of her tread

  • नतभुवम् natabhuvam

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    नत + भू
    Vigraha
    नता भूः यया सा, ताम् natā bhūḥ yayā sā, tām
    Literally
    “she by whom the earth is bowed”
    In the verse
    the earth sinking under her step

  • किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बराम् kirīṭollikhitāmbarām

    bahuvrīhi (किरीटोल्लिखित a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa, किरीटेन उल्लिखितम्)

    Members
    किरीटोल्लिखित + अम्बर
    Vigraha
    किरीटेन उल्लिखितम् अम्बरं यया सा, ताम् kirīṭena ullikhitam ambaraṃ yayā sā, tām
    Literally
    “she by whom the sky was scraped with the crown”
    In the verse
    so tall that her diadem touched the sky

  • क्षोभिताशेषपातालाम् kṣobhitāśeṣapātālām

    bahuvrīhi (अशेषपाताल a karmadhāraya, अशेषाणि पातालानि)

    Members
    क्षोभित + अशेषपाताल
    Vigraha
    क्षोभितानि अशेषाणि पातालानि यया सा, ताम् kṣobhitāni aśeṣāṇi pātālāni yayā sā, tām
    Literally
    “she by whom all the netherworlds were stirred”
    In the verse
    shaking the underworlds to their depths

  • धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेन dhanurjyāniḥsvanena

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (धनुर्ज्या also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, धनुषः ज्या)

    Members
    धनुर्ज्या + निःस्वन
    Vigraha
    धनुर्ज्यायाः निःस्वनः, तेन dhanurjyāyāḥ niḥsvanaḥ, tena
    Literally
    “by the sound of the string of the bow”
    In the verse
    by the twang of her bowstring
Morphology & anvaya

  • नतभुवम् natabhuvam

    The stem भू ‘earth’ keeps its form at the end of a bahuvrīhi; before a vowel ending ū becomes uv (भुवम्), as in the simple noun (भुवम्, भुवि).

  • पादाक्रान्त्या pādākrāntyā

    आक्रान्ति is an action noun (ktin) from आ + √क्रम् ‘to step upon’; its instrumental gives the cause of the earth’s bowing.

Anvaya · prose order

(सः) पादाक्रान्त्या नतभुवं, किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बरां, धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेन क्षोभिताशेषपातालां तां (देवीं ददर्श)।

(saḥ) pādākrāntyā natabhuvaṃ, kirīṭollikhitāmbarāṃ, dhanurjyāniḥsvanena kṣobhitāśeṣapātālāṃ tāṃ (devīṃ dadarśa) |

All accusatives in apposition to देवीम् (2.37); each bahuvrīhi has its own instrumental of cause beside it.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

What Mahiṣa sees is the Goddess as large as the cosmos: her foot presses the earth, her crown touches the sky, and the sound of her bow reaches the netherworlds. The three levels of the world are each touched by her.

Traditional interpretation

Purāṇic cosmology divides the world into the heavens above, the earth, and the pātālas below. The verse touches all three, so that before a blow is struck the asura sees that the one he means to fight is as wide as the world.

Verse 2.39

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

दिशो भुजसहस्रेण समन्ताद् व्याप्य संस्थिताम्।ततः प्रववृते युद्धं तया देव्या सुरद्विषाम्॥⁠३९⁠॥

diśo bhujasahasreṇa samantād vyāpya saṃsthitām |tataḥ pravavṛte yuddhaṃ tayā devyā suradviṣām ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

दिशः · भुजसहस्रेण · समन्तात् · व्याप्य · संस्थिताम्ततः · प्रववृते · युद्धम् · तया · देव्या · सुरद्विषाम्

diśaḥ · bhujasahasreṇa · samantāt · vyāpya · saṃsthitāmtataḥ · pravavṛte · yuddham · tayā · devyā · suradviṣām

Word by word

  1. दिशःdiśaḥthe directionsf. acc. pl. of दिश् (consonant stem); object of व्याप्य
  2. भुजसहस्रेणbhujasahasreṇawith a thousand armsn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument of व्याप्य
  3. समन्तात्samantāton every sideindeclinable (ablative form used as an adverb)
  4. व्याप्यvyāpyahaving pervadedabsolutive (lyap) of वि + √आप् ‘to reach, pervade’
  5. संस्थिताम्saṃsthitāmstandingf. acc. sg. of संस्थित (सम् + √स्था + kta); qualifies ताम् (2.38) / देवीम् (2.37)
  6. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
  7. प्रववृतेpravavṛtebegan, broke outप्र + √वृत् (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to turn, happen’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  8. युद्धम्yuddhamthe battlen. nom. sg. of युद्ध; subject of प्रववृते
  9. तयाtayāwith thatpronoun तद्, f. instr. sg.; qualifies देव्या
  10. देव्याdevyāwith the Goddessf. instr. sg. of देवी; instrumental of the opponent (‘battle with’)
  11. सुरद्विषाम्suradviṣāmof the enemies of the godsm. gen. pl. of सुरद्विष्; compound (see Samāsa); depends on युद्धम्

Translation

standing, having pervaded the directions on every side with a thousand arms. Then the battle of the enemies of the gods with that Goddess began,

In readable Englishstanding with her thousand arms spread through every quarter. Then the battle began between the Goddess and the enemies of the gods,

The second line continues into 2.40, which describes the battle.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • दिशो भुजसहस्रेण diśo bhujasahasreṇa → दिशः + भुजसहस्रेण

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced bh becomes -o.

  • समन्ताद् व्याप्य samantād vyāpya → समन्तात् + व्याप्य

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t → d before the voiced v; written with a virāma and a space.

  • युद्धं तया yuddhaṃ tayā → युद्धम् + तया

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • भुजसहस्रेण bhujasahasreṇa

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    भुज + सहस्र
    Vigraha
    भुजानां सहस्रम्, तेन bhujānāṃ sahasram, tena
    Literally
    “with a thousand of arms”
    In the verse
    with her thousand arms

  • सुरद्विषाम् suradviṣām

    upapada tatpuruṣa (√द्विष् + kvip)

    Members
    सुर + द्विष्
    Vigraha
    सुरान् द्विषन्ति इति सुरद्विषः, तेषाम् surān dviṣanti iti suradviṣaḥ, teṣām
    Literally
    “of the god-haters”
    In the verse
    of the asuras
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्रववृते pravavṛte

    Perfect ātmanepada of √वृत् with प्र: reduplicated व-वृत्-, ending -ए. ‘(The battle) began, set in’.

  • व्याप्य vyāpya

    वि + √आप् (वि → व्य् before आ by yaṇ) with -य: ‘having filled, having spread over’.

Anvaya · prose order

(सः) भुजसहस्रेण समन्तात् दिशः व्याप्य संस्थितां (तां देवीं ददर्श)। ततः तया देव्या (सह) सुरद्विषां युद्धं प्रववृते।

(saḥ) bhujasahasreṇa samantāt diśaḥ vyāpya saṃsthitāṃ (tāṃ devīṃ dadarśa) | tataḥ tayā devyā (saha) suradviṣāṃ yuddhaṃ pravavṛte |

The first line ends the description of the Goddess (object of ददर्श, 2.37). The second line is a new sentence; तया देव्या is the instrumental of the one fought against (सह is understood).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The vision is completed by her arms, a thousand of them, filling the directions; then the battle begins. ‘A thousand’ here means beyond counting, completing the vertical vastness of 2.38 with the horizontal.

Traditional interpretation

The madhyama-carita dhyāna of the Gita Press pāṭha pictures Mahālakṣmī with eighteen objects in her hands; the text’s own vision here is of a thousand arms. The two do not compete: the dhyāna is a form for meditation, the verse a vision of her cosmic extent.

Verse 2.40

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

शस्त्रास्त्रैर्बहुधा मुक्तैरादीपितदिगन्तरम्।महिषासुरसेनानीश्चिक्षुराख्यो महासुरः॥⁠४०⁠॥

śastrāstrairbahudhā muktairādīpitadigantaram |mahiṣāsurasenānīścikṣurākhyo mahāsuraḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

शस्त्रास्त्रैः · बहुधा · मुक्तैः · आदीपितदिगन्तरम्महिषासुरसेनानीः · चिक्षुराख्यः · महासुरः

śastrāstraiḥ · bahudhā · muktaiḥ · ādīpitadigantarammahiṣāsurasenānīḥ · cikṣurākhyaḥ · mahāsuraḥ

Word by word

  1. शस्त्रास्त्रैःśastrāstraiḥby weapons and missilesn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of the participle in आदीपित-
  2. बहुधाbahudhāin many ways, in profusionindeclinable (बहु + taddhita -धा ‘-fold’)
  3. मुक्तैःmuktaiḥdischarged, let flyn. instr. pl. of मुक्त (past participle, kta, of √मुच् ‘to release’); qualifies शस्त्रास्त्रैः
  4. आदीपितदिगन्तरम्ādīpitadigantaramin which the spaces between the quarters were set ablazen. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies युद्धम् (2.39)
  5. महिषासुरसेनानीःmahiṣāsurasenānīḥMahiṣāsura’s generalm. nom. sg. of the ī-stem सेनानी at the end of a compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to चिक्षुराख्यः
  6. चिक्षुराख्यःcikṣurākhyaḥnamed Cikṣuram. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of युयुधे (2.41)
  7. महासुरःmahāsuraḥthe great asuram. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition

Translation

(a battle) in which the spaces between the quarters were lit up by weapons and missiles discharged in many ways. Mahiṣāsura’s general, the great asura named Cikṣura,

In readable Englisha battle in which the weapons and missiles they hurled set the whole horizon ablaze. Cikṣura, the mighty asura, Mahiṣāsura’s commander,

The sentence of the second line is completed by युयुधे in 2.41.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • शस्त्रास्त्रैर्बहुधा śastrāstrairbahudhā → शस्त्रास्त्रैः + बहुधा

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    Visarga after ai before a voiced consonant appears as r; likewise मुक्तैरादीपित- before a vowel.

  • दिगन्तरम् digantaram → दिक् + अन्तरम्

    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    दिश् ends in k at the end of a member (दिक्), and k becomes g before the vowel of अन्तर.

  • सेनानीश्चिक्षुराख्यो महासुरः senānīścikṣurākhyo mahāsuraḥ → सेनानीः + चिक्षुराख्यः + महासुरः

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    Visarga before c → ś (सेनानीश्चि-); -aḥ before the voiced m → -o (चिक्षुराख्यो).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शस्त्रास्त्रैः śastrāstraiḥ

    dvandva (itaretara)

    Members
    शस्त्र + अस्त्र
    Vigraha
    शस्त्राणि च अस्त्राणि च, तैः śastrāṇi ca astrāṇi ca, taiḥ
    Literally
    “by weapons and missiles”
    In the verse
    hand-weapons and weapons hurled

  • आदीपितदिगन्तरम् ādīpitadigantaram

    bahuvrīhi (दिगन्तर a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, दिशाम् अन्तरम्)

    Members
    आदीपित + दिगन्तर
    Vigraha
    आदीपितानि दिगन्तराणि यस्मिन् तत् ādīpitāni digantarāṇi yasmin tat
    Literally
    “that in which the intervals of the directions were kindled”
    In the verse
    a battle whose weapons lit up the whole horizon

  • महिषासुरसेनानीः mahiṣāsurasenānīḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    महिषासुर + सेनानी
    Vigraha
    महिषासुरस्य सेनानीः (सेनानी = सेनां नयति इति, upapada tatpuruṣa with √नी + kvip) mahiṣāsurasya senānīḥ (senānī = senāṃ nayati iti, upapada tatpuruṣa with √nī + kvip)
    Literally
    “army-leader of Mahiṣāsura”
    In the verse
    Mahiṣāsura’s commander-in-chief

  • चिक्षुराख्यः cikṣurākhyaḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    चिक्षुर + आख्या
    Vigraha
    चिक्षुरः आख्या यस्य सः cikṣuraḥ ākhyā yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose name is Cikṣura”
    In the verse
    Cikṣura by name

  • महासुरः mahāsuraḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + असुर
    Vigraha
    महान् चासौ असुरः च mahān cāsau asuraḥ ca
    Literally
    “a great asura”
    In the verse
    a mighty asura (महत् becomes महा- as the first member of a karmadhāraya)
Morphology & anvaya

  • महिषासुरसेनानीः mahiṣāsurasenānīḥ

    सेनानी is a root-noun in -ī (from √नी ‘to lead’); its nominative singular keeps the visarga: सेनानीः. Chapter 3 calls Cikṣura सेनानीः again (3.2) and, after his death, महिषस्य चमूपतिः (3.11).

  • आदीपितदिगन्तरम् ādīpitadigantaram

    Neuter, to agree with युद्धम् of 2.39: the battle is described before its first fighter is named. आदीपित is the past participle of the causative of आ + √दीप् ‘set ablaze’.

Anvaya · prose order

(तत् युद्धं) बहुधा मुक्तैः शस्त्रास्त्रैः आदीपितदिगन्तरम् (आसीत्)। महिषासुरसेनानीः चिक्षुराख्यः महासुरः (2.41: युयुधे)।

(tat yuddhaṃ) bahudhā muktaiḥ śastrāstraiḥ ādīpitadigantaram (āsīt) | mahiṣāsurasenānīḥ cikṣurākhyaḥ mahāsuraḥ (2.41: yuyudhe) |

The first line qualifies युद्धम् (2.39). The second introduces Cikṣura, the subject of युयुधे at the head of 2.41.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The battle is first seen as a whole — the horizon lit by weapons — and then its leaders are named one by one, beginning with Cikṣura, Mahiṣa’s commander. He is the first to attack the Goddess in chapter 3 (3.2).

Traditional interpretation

From here to 2.47 the text gives a muster-roll of Mahiṣa’s generals with the size of their forces. Most of them are killed by name in chapter 3: Cikṣura (3.2ff.), Cāmara (3.11–16), Udagra (3.17), Bāṣkala (3.18), Mahāhanu (3.19), Biḍāla (3.20).

Verse 2.41

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

युयुधे चामरश्चान्यैश्चतुरङ्गबलान्वितः।रथानामयुतैः षड्भिरुदग्राख्यो महासुरः॥⁠४१⁠॥

yuyudhe cāmaraścānyaiścaturaṅgabalānvitaḥ |rathānāmayutaiḥ ṣaḍbhirudagrākhyo mahāsuraḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

युयुधे · चामरः · च · अन्यैः · चतुरङ्गबलान्वितःरथानाम् · अयुतैः · षड्भिः · उदग्राख्यः · महासुरः

yuyudhe · cāmaraḥ · ca · anyaiḥ · caturaṅgabalānvitaḥrathānām · ayutaiḥ · ṣaḍbhiḥ · udagrākhyaḥ · mahāsuraḥ

Word by word

  1. युयुधेyuyudhefought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to fight’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  2. चामरःcāmaraḥCāmaram. nom. sg. of चामर, proper name (as a common noun ‘yak-tail fly-whisk’); subject
  3. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  4. अन्यैःanyaiḥwith otherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. instr. pl.; instrumental of accompaniment
  5. चतुरङ्गबलान्वितःcaturaṅgabalānvitaḥaccompanied by an army of four divisionsm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies चामरः
  6. रथानाम्rathānāmof chariotsm. gen. pl. of रथ; depends on अयुतैः
  7. अयुतैःayutaiḥwith ten-thousandsn. instr. pl. of अयुत (‘ten thousand’); instrumental of accompaniment
  8. षड्भिःṣaḍbhiḥsixnumeral षष्, instr. pl.; qualifies अयुतैः
  9. उदग्राख्यःudagrākhyaḥnamed Udagram. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of अयुध्यत (2.42)
  10. महासुरःmahāsuraḥthe great asuram. nom. sg.; compound (see 2.40); in apposition

Translation

fought; and Cāmara, with others, accompanied by an army of four divisions. The great asura named Udagra, with six ten-thousands of chariots,

In readable Englishjoined battle; so did Cāmara, with others and an army of all four arms. Udagra, the mighty asura, with sixty thousand chariots,

‘Sixty thousand’ renders षड्भिः अयुतैः (six ayutas). The second line continues into 2.42.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • चामरश्चान्यैश्चतुरङ्ग cāmaraścānyaiścaturaṅga → चामरः + च + अन्यैः + चतुरङ्ग-

    visarga sandhi and vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Visarga before c → ś (चामरश्च, अन्यैश्च-); a + a → ā (चान्यैः).

  • रथानामयुतैः rathānāmayutaiḥ → रथानाम् + अयुतैः

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    Final m joins the following vowel.

  • षड्भिरुदग्राख्यो महासुरः ṣaḍbhirudagrākhyo mahāsuraḥ → षड्भिः + उदग्राख्यः + महासुरः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    Visarga after i before a vowel appears as r; -aḥ before m becomes -o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • चतुरङ्गबलान्वितः caturaṅgabalānvitaḥ

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (चतुरङ्गबल a karmadhāraya, चतुरङ्गं बलम्; चतुरङ्ग a bahuvrīhi, चत्वारि अङ्गानि यस्य तत्)

    Members
    चतुरङ्गबल + अन्वित
    Vigraha
    चतुरङ्गबलेन अन्वितः caturaṅgabalena anvitaḥ
    Literally
    “joined with a four-limbed army”
    In the verse
    with a full army of elephants, chariots, cavalry and foot

  • उदग्राख्यः udagrākhyaḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    उदग्र + आख्या
    Vigraha
    उदग्रः आख्या यस्य सः udagraḥ ākhyā yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose name is Udagra (‘towering’)”
    In the verse
    Udagra by name
Morphology & anvaya

  • युयुधे yuyudhe

    Perfect of the ātmanepada root √युध्: reduplication यु- + युध् + -ए. Its subject is Cikṣura (2.40), and it serves Cāmara too.

  • षड्भिः ṣaḍbhiḥ

    Instrumental plural of षष् ‘six’: before -भिः the ṣ appears as ḍ (षड्भिः). Six ayutas = sixty thousand.

Anvaya · prose order

(चिक्षुराख्यः महासुरः) युयुधे; चामरः च चतुरङ्गबलान्वितः अन्यैः (सह युयुधे)। उदग्राख्यः महासुरः रथानां षड्भिः अयुतैः (2.42: अयुध्यत)।

(cikṣurākhyaḥ mahāsuraḥ) yuyudhe; cāmaraḥ ca caturaṅgabalānvitaḥ anyaiḥ (saha yuyudhe) | udagrākhyaḥ mahāsuraḥ rathānāṃ ṣaḍbhiḥ ayutaiḥ (2.42: ayudhyata) |

युयुधे closes the sentence of 2.40b and is understood again with Cāmara. The second line names Udagra, whose verb is अयुध्यत at the head of 2.42.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The first three generals: Cikṣura, Cāmara with a full army, and Udagra with sixty thousand chariots. The fourfold army (चतुरङ्ग) is the classical Indian army of elephants, chariots, horse and foot.

Traditional interpretation

The numbers grow as the roll goes on — tens of thousands, then millions, then crores — to show the scale of what one Goddess faces. The point is the disproportion, not the arithmetic.

Verse 2.42

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

अयुध्यतायुतानां च सहस्रेण महाहनुः।पञ्चाशद्भिश्च नियुतैरसिलोमा महासुरः॥⁠४२⁠॥

ayudhyatāyutānāṃ ca sahasreṇa mahāhanuḥ |pañcāśadbhiśca niyutairasilomā mahāsuraḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

अयुध्यत · अयुतानाम् · च · सहस्रेण · महाहनुःपञ्चाशद्भिः · च · नियुतैः · असिलोमा · महासुरः

ayudhyata · ayutānām · ca · sahasreṇa · mahāhanuḥpañcāśadbhiḥ · ca · niyutaiḥ · asilomā · mahāsuraḥ

Word by word

  1. अयुध्यतayudhyatafought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada; subject Udagra (2.41), and understood again with Mahāhanu
  2. अयुतानाम्ayutānāmof ten-thousandsn. gen. pl. of अयुत; depends on सहस्रेण
  3. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  4. सहस्रेणsahasreṇawith a thousandn. instr. sg. of सहस्र; instrumental of accompaniment
  5. महाहनुःmahāhanuḥMahāhanu (‘Great-Jaw’)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa), used as a proper name; subject
  6. पञ्चाशद्भिःpañcāśadbhiḥwith fiftynumeral पञ्चाशत् (f.), instr. pl.; qualifies नियुतैः
  7. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  8. नियुतैःniyutaiḥwith niyutas (millions)n. instr. pl. of नियुत (a large number, usually a million); instrumental of accompaniment
  9. असिलोमाasilomāAsiloman (‘Sword-Hair’)m. nom. sg. of असिलोमन् (n-stem); compound (see Samāsa), used as a proper name; subject of a supplied verb
  10. महासुरःmahāsuraḥthe great asuram. nom. sg.; compound (see 2.40); in apposition

Translation

fought; and Mahāhanu with a thousand of ten-thousands; and with fifty niyutas the great asura Asiloman,

In readable Englishfought on, and Mahāhanu with ten million; and the mighty Asiloman with fifty million,

A thousand ayutas is ten million; fifty niyutas is rendered ‘fifty million’, taking a niyuta as a million (see Flags).

Textual verification / uncertain: The value of नियुत varies in the lexical tradition (a million in most lists, a hundred thousand in some); fifty niyutas is therefore fifty million or five million.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • अयुध्यतायुतानां ayudhyatāyutānāṃ → अयुध्यत + अयुतानाम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • पञ्चाशद्भिश्च pañcāśadbhiśca → पञ्चाशद्भिः + च

    visarga sandhi; voicing inside the word · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c → ś. In पञ्चाशद्भिः the t of पञ्चाशत् is voiced to d before the ending -भिः.

  • नियुतैरसिलोमा niyutairasilomā → नियुतैः + असिलोमा

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    Visarga after ai before a vowel appears as r.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महाहनुः mahāhanuḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    महत् + हनु
    Vigraha
    महती हनुः यस्य सः mahatī hanuḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose jaw is great”
    In the verse
    ‘Great-Jaw’, a name

  • असिलोमा asilomā

    bahuvrīhi (with an implied comparison)

    Members
    असि + लोमन्
    Vigraha
    असयः इव लोमानि यस्य सः asayaḥ iva lomāni yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose hairs are (like) swords”
    In the verse
    ‘Sword-Hair’, a name
Morphology & anvaya

  • अयुध्यत ayudhyata

    Imperfect of √युध् on the divādi stem युध्य- with augment a-: ‘(he) fought’. Its use beside the perfect युयुधे shows the two tenses alternating freely in narrative.

  • असिलोमा asilomā

    Nominative of an n-stem (लोमन् ‘hair’) at the end of a bahuvrīhi: -मन् → -मा, as in विश्वकर्मा (2.27).

Anvaya · prose order

(उदग्राख्यः महासुरः रथानां षड्भिः अयुतैः) अयुध्यत; महाहनुः च अयुतानां सहस्रेण (अयुध्यत)। असिलोमा महासुरः च पञ्चाशद्भिः नियुतैः (अयुध्यत)।

(udagrākhyaḥ mahāsuraḥ rathānāṃ ṣaḍbhiḥ ayutaiḥ) ayudhyata; mahāhanuḥ ca ayutānāṃ sahasreṇa (ayudhyata) | asilomā mahāsuraḥ ca pañcāśadbhiḥ niyutaiḥ (ayudhyata) |

अयुध्यत completes 2.41b and is understood for Mahāhanu and Asiloman. Each general is given with the instrumental of the force that accompanies him.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Udagra fights; Mahāhanu (‘Great-Jaw’) comes with a thousand ayutas, ten million; Asiloman (‘Sword-Hair’) with fifty niyutas. The names describe monstrous bodies.

Traditional interpretation

Mahāhanu is killed by the Goddess’s trident in 3.19. Asiloman is not named again in the Gita Press text of chapter 3.

Verse 2.43

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

अयुतानां शतैः षड्भिर्बाष्कलो युयुधे रणे।गजवाजिसहस्रौघैरनेकैः परिवारितः॥⁠४३⁠॥

ayutānāṃ śataiḥ ṣaḍbhirbāṣkalo yuyudhe raṇe |gajavājisahasraughairanekaiḥ parivāritaḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

अयुतानाम् · शतैः · षड्भिः · बाष्कलः · युयुधे · रणेगजवाजिसहस्रौघैः · अनेकैः · परिवारितः

ayutānām · śataiḥ · ṣaḍbhiḥ · bāṣkalaḥ · yuyudhe · raṇegajavājisahasraughaiḥ · anekaiḥ · parivāritaḥ

Word by word

  1. अयुतानाम्ayutānāmof ten-thousandsn. gen. pl. of अयुत; depends on शतैः
  2. शतैःśataiḥwith hundredsn. instr. pl. of शत; instrumental of accompaniment
  3. षड्भिःṣaḍbhiḥsixnumeral षष्, instr. pl.; qualifies शतैः
  4. बाष्कलःbāṣkalaḥBāṣkalam. nom. sg., proper name; subject of युयुधे
  5. युयुधेyuyudhefought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  6. रणेraṇein the battlem. loc. sg. of रण
  7. गजवाजिसहस्रौघैःgajavājisahasraughaiḥby hosts of thousands of elephants and horsesm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of परिवारितः
  8. अनेकैःanekaiḥmanym. instr. pl. of अनेक; qualifies -ओघैः
  9. परिवारितःparivāritaḥParivārita (‘the Surrounded’); lit. ‘surrounded’m. nom. sg. of परिवारित (परि + √वृ, causative वारय-, + kta); taken here as a proper name, subject of अयुध्यत (2.44); alternatively the participle ‘surrounded’ (see Morph)

Translation

With six hundreds of ten-thousands Bāṣkala fought in the battle. Parivārita, with many hosts of thousands of elephants and horses,

In readable EnglishBāṣkala fought in the battle with six million. Parivārita, with many thousands of elephants and horses in their hosts,

परिवारितः is rendered as a name (see Morph and Flags); ‘six million’ renders six hundred ayutas.

Textual verification / uncertain: परिवारितः may be a proper name (Parivārita) or the participle ‘surrounded’; the name reading is followed because अयुध्यत in 2.44a has no other subject. No general of that name appears among those slain in chapter 3.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • शतैः षड्भिर्बाष्कलो युयुधे śataiḥ ṣaḍbhirbāṣkalo yuyudhe → शतैः + षड्भिः + बाष्कलः + युयुधे

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    Visarga before ṣ may stay (शतैः षड्भिः); after i before the voiced b it appears as r (षड्भिर्बाष्कलो); -aḥ before y becomes -o (बाष्कलो युयुधे).

  • गजवाजिसहस्रौघैरनेकैः gajavājisahasraughairanekaiḥ → गजवाजिसहस्र + ओघैः + अनेकैः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (vṛddhi); visarga sandhi · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    a + o → au (सहस्रौघ); visarga after ai before a vowel appears as r.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • गजवाजिसहस्रौघैः gajavājisahasraughaiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa; inside it गजवाजिसहस्र is a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (गजवाजिनां सहस्राणि) and गजवाजि an itaretara dvandva (गजाः च वाजिनः च)

    Members
    गजवाजिसहस्र + ओघ
    Vigraha
    गजवाजिसहस्राणाम् ओघाः, तैः gajavājisahasrāṇām oghāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally
    “by floods of thousands of elephants and horses”
    In the verse
    with wave on wave of elephants and cavalry
Morphology & anvaya

  • परिवारितः parivāritaḥ

    Past participle of the causative of परि + √वृ: ‘made surrounded, encircled’. It can be read as a name (Parivārita), the subject of अयुध्यत in 2.44, which otherwise has none; or as the plain participle ‘surrounded’, describing Bāṣkala, with 2.44a then repeating his fighting. The name reading is followed here; in 2.45 the same word is plainly the participle.

  • षड्भिः ṣaḍbhiḥ

    As in 2.41: six hundreds of ayutas = six million.

Anvaya · prose order

बाष्कलः अयुतानां षड्भिः शतैः रणे युयुधे। अनेकैः गजवाजिसहस्रौघैः परिवारितः (2.44: रथानां कोट्या च वृतः तस्मिन् युद्धे अयुध्यत)।

bāṣkalaḥ ayutānāṃ ṣaḍbhiḥ śataiḥ raṇe yuyudhe | anekaiḥ gajavājisahasraughaiḥ parivāritaḥ (2.44: rathānāṃ koṭyā ca vṛtaḥ tasmin yuddhe ayudhyata) |

The first line is complete. The second introduces परिवारितः, taken as the name of the next general, whose verb is अयुध्यत in 2.44.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Bāṣkala comes with six hundred ayutas — six million. He is struck down with a bhindipāla in 3.18, where the Gita Press prints the name as वाष्कल (b and v are interchangeable in many manuscripts).

Traditional interpretation

Translators divide the next lines differently. Taking परिवारितः as a name gives each verb of 2.43–45 its own general (Bāṣkala, Parivārita, Biḍāla); taking it as ‘surrounded’ leaves Bāṣkala, or Biḍāla, as the subject of two verbs.

Verse 2.44

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

वृतो रथानां कोट्या च युद्धे तस्मिन्नयुध्यत।बिडालाख्योऽयुतानां च पञ्चाशद्भिरथायुतैः॥⁠४४⁠॥

vṛto rathānāṃ koṭyā ca yuddhe tasminnayudhyata |biḍālākhyo'yutānāṃ ca pañcāśadbhirathāyutaiḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

वृतः · रथानाम् · कोट्या · च · युद्धे · तस्मिन् · अयुध्यतबिडालाख्यः · अयुतानाम् · च · पञ्चाशद्भिः · अथ · अयुतैः

vṛtaḥ · rathānām · koṭyā · ca · yuddhe · tasmin · ayudhyatabiḍālākhyaḥ · ayutānām · ca · pañcāśadbhiḥ · atha · ayutaiḥ

Word by word

  1. वृतःvṛtaḥsurroundedpast participle (kta) of √वृ ‘to cover, surround’, m. nom. sg.; qualifies परिवारितः (2.43)
  2. रथानाम्rathānāmof chariotsm. gen. pl. of रथ; depends on कोट्या
  3. कोट्याkoṭyāby a crore (ten million)f. instr. sg. of कोटि (i-stem); agent of वृतः
  4. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); adds the chariots to the elephants and horses of 2.43
  5. युद्धेyuddhein the battlen. loc. sg. of युद्ध
  6. तस्मिन्tasminthatpronoun तद्, n. loc. sg.; qualifies युद्धे
  7. अयुध्यतayudhyatafought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  8. बिडालाख्यःbiḍālākhyaḥnamed Biḍālam. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of युयुधे (2.45)
  9. अयुतानाम्ayutānāmof ten-thousandsn. gen. pl. of अयुत; depends on पञ्चाशद्भिः
  10. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  11. पञ्चाशद्भिःpañcāśadbhiḥwith fiftynumeral पञ्चाशत् (f.), instr. pl.; instrumental of accompaniment
  12. अथathaand, moreoverindeclinable (connective particle)
  13. अयुतैःayutaiḥwith ten-thousandsn. instr. pl. of अयुत; resumes अयुतानाम् … पञ्चाशद्भिः (see Morph)

Translation

and surrounded by a crore of chariots, fought in that battle. And the one named Biḍāla, with fifty of ten-thousands, and with ten-thousands —

In readable Englishand a crore of chariots about him, fought in that battle. Biḍāla, with fifty ayutas

The doubled ‘ayuta’ is rendered once (see Morph); ‘a crore’ is ten million. The sentence continues into 2.45.

Textual verification / uncertain: अयुतानां … पञ्चाशद्भिः अथ अयुतैः repeats अयुत; the sense ‘fifty ayutas’ is adopted, but ‘fifty ayutas of ayutas’ is grammatically possible. The sandhi rules out a split into पञ्चाशद्भिः रथायुतैः.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • वृतो रथानां vṛto rathānāṃ → वृतः + रथानाम्

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced r becomes -o.

  • तस्मिन्नयुध्यत tasminnayudhyata → तस्मिन् + अयुध्यत

    consonant sandhi (doubling of n) · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32)

    Final n after a short vowel is doubled before a vowel: तस्मिन्न-.

  • बिडालाख्योऽयुतानां biḍālākhyo'yutānāṃ → बिडालाख्यः + अयुतानाम्

    visarga sandhi (with pūrvarūpa) · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a- becomes -o, and the initial a is absorbed (avagraha).

  • पञ्चाशद्भिरथायुतैः pañcāśadbhirathāyutaiḥ → पञ्चाशद्भिः + अथ + अयुतैः

    visarga sandhi and vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Visarga after i before a vowel appears as r; a + a → ā. The division पञ्चाशद्भिः + रथायुतैः is not possible: -iḥ before r would give पञ्चाशद्भी रथा- (रो रि, 8.3.14; ढ्रलोपे पूर्वस्य दीर्घोऽणः, 6.3.111).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • बिडालाख्यः biḍālākhyaḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    बिडाल + आख्या
    Vigraha
    बिडालः आख्या यस्य सः biḍālaḥ ākhyā yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose name is Biḍāla (‘Cat’)”
    In the verse
    Biḍāla by name
Morphology & anvaya

  • अयुतैः ayutaiḥ

    The phrase अयुतानां च पञ्चाशद्भिः अथ अयुतैः names the ayuta twice. Taken with रथानाम् in 2.45 it is read as ‘with fifty ayutas (of chariots)’, the second अयुतैः resuming the first after the connective अथ; read strictly it could be ‘fifty ayutas of ayutas’, a hyperbole of the kind found in 2.46. The first reading is followed.

  • तस्मिन् tasmin

    Locative singular of the pronoun तद् (-स्मिन्, the pronominal locative), agreeing with the neuter युद्धे.

Anvaya · prose order

(परिवारितः) रथानां कोट्या च वृतः तस्मिन् युद्धे अयुध्यत। बिडालाख्यः च अयुतानां पञ्चाशद्भिः अथ अयुतैः (रथानां परिवारितः, 2.45: युयुधे)।

(parivāritaḥ) rathānāṃ koṭyā ca vṛtaḥ tasmin yuddhe ayudhyata | biḍālākhyaḥ ca ayutānāṃ pañcāśadbhiḥ atha ayutaiḥ (rathānāṃ parivāritaḥ, 2.45: yuyudhe) |

The first line completes 2.43b (subject परिवारितः). The second introduces Biḍāla; his verb and the genitive रथानाम् stand in 2.45.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The general of 2.43b fights encircled by elephants, horses and a crore of chariots; then Biḍāla (‘Cat’) is named, with fifty ayutas — half a million — of chariots.

Traditional interpretation

Biḍāla is beheaded by the Goddess’s sword in 3.20 (बिडालस्यासिना कायात्पातयामास वै शिरः).

Verse 2.45

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

युयुधे संयुगे तत्र रथानां परिवारितः।अन्ये च तत्रायुतशो रथनागहयैर्वृताः॥⁠४५⁠॥

yuyudhe saṃyuge tatra rathānāṃ parivāritaḥ |anye ca tatrāyutaśo rathanāgahayairvṛtāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

युयुधे · संयुगे · तत्र · रथानाम् · परिवारितःअन्ये · च · तत्र · अयुतशः · रथनागहयैः · वृताः

yuyudhe · saṃyuge · tatra · rathānām · parivāritaḥanye · ca · tatra · ayutaśaḥ · rathanāgahayaiḥ · vṛtāḥ

Word by word

  1. युयुधेyuyudhefought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada; subject बिडालाख्यः (2.44)
  2. संयुगेsaṃyugein the battlen. loc. sg. of संयुग (सम् + √युज्, ‘a coming together’, i.e. combat)
  3. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
  4. रथानाम्rathānāmof chariotsm. gen. pl. of रथ; depends on the numbers of 2.44b
  5. परिवारितःparivāritaḥsurroundedpast participle of परि + √वृ (causative वारय-, + kta), m. nom. sg.; qualifies बिडालाख्यः
  6. अन्येanyeotherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. nom. pl.; subject of युयुधुः (2.46)
  7. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  8. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
  9. अयुतशःayutaśaḥby tens of thousandsindeclinable (distributive adverb, अयुत + taddhita -शस्)
  10. रथनागहयैःrathanāgahayaiḥby chariots, elephants and horsesm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of वृताः
  11. वृताःvṛtāḥsurroundedpast participle (kta) of √वृ, m. nom. pl.; qualifies अन्ये

Translation

of chariots, surrounded, fought there in the battle. And others there, surrounded by chariots, elephants and horses by tens of thousands,

In readable Englishof chariots around him, fought in that battle. And other great asuras, each with chariots, elephants and horses by the ten thousand,

‘Great asuras’ is brought forward from 2.46, where the subject is named.

Textual verification / uncertain: Recension note (corrections.json): GRETIL’s Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa has additional verses at this point (in 2.45) not found in the Gita Press text; they are not part of the 700 and are not analysed.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • रथानां परिवारितः rathānāṃ parivāritaḥ → रथानाम् + परिवारितः

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

  • तत्रायुतशो रथ tatrāyutaśo ratha → तत्र + अयुतशः + रथ-

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + a → ā; -aḥ before the voiced r becomes -o.

  • रथनागहयैर्वृताः rathanāgahayairvṛtāḥ → रथनागहयैः + वृताः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    Visarga after ai before a voiced consonant appears as r.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • रथनागहयैः rathanāgahayaiḥ

    dvandva (itaretara)

    Members
    रथ + नाग + हय
    Vigraha
    रथाः च नागाः च हयाः च, तैः rathāḥ ca nāgāḥ ca hayāḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally
    “by chariots and elephants and horses”
    In the verse
    by chariots, elephants and cavalry
Morphology & anvaya

  • अयुतशः ayutaśaḥ

    The taddhita -शस् makes distributive adverbs from numbers: अयुतशः ‘by ten-thousands, in tens of thousands’ (compare शतशः ‘by hundreds’, 2.56).

  • परिवारितः parivāritaḥ

    Here clearly the participle ‘surrounded’, construed with the genitive रथानाम् and the instrumentals of 2.44b. Compare its use in 2.43.

Anvaya · prose order

(बिडालाख्यः, अयुतानां पञ्चाशद्भिः अयुतैः) रथानां परिवारितः तत्र संयुगे युयुधे। अन्ये च तत्र अयुतशः रथनागहयैः वृताः (2.46: महासुराः युयुधुः)।

(biḍālākhyaḥ, ayutānāṃ pañcāśadbhiḥ ayutaiḥ) rathānāṃ parivāritaḥ tatra saṃyuge yuyudhe | anye ca tatra ayutaśaḥ rathanāgahayaiḥ vṛtāḥ (2.46: mahāsurāḥ yuyudhuḥ) |

The first line finishes Biḍāla’s sentence. The second introduces ‘others’, whose verb युयुधुः and noun महासुराः stand in 2.46.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Biḍāla’s entry is completed, and the roll turns to the many unnamed generals, each with his tens of thousands of chariots, elephants and horses.

Traditional interpretation

The muster-roll is longer in the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa as preserved in GRETIL, which carries verses here that the Gita Press text does not; the recited Saptaśatī of seven hundred mantras is followed in this edition, and nothing is added to it.

Verse 2.46

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

युयुधुः संयुगे देव्या सह तत्र महासुराः।कोटिकोटिसहस्रैस्तु रथानां दन्तिनां तथा॥⁠४६⁠॥

yuyudhuḥ saṃyuge devyā saha tatra mahāsurāḥ |koṭikoṭisahasraistu rathānāṃ dantināṃ tathā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

युयुधुः · संयुगे · देव्या · सह · तत्र · महासुराःकोटिकोटिसहस्रैः · तु · रथानाम् · दन्तिनाम् · तथा

yuyudhuḥ · saṃyuge · devyā · saha · tatra · mahāsurāḥkoṭikoṭisahasraiḥ · tu · rathānām · dantinām · tathā

Word by word

  1. युयुधुःyuyudhuḥfought√युध्, liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada (an epic parasmaipada form of an ātmanepada root; classical युयुधिरे)
  2. संयुगेsaṃyugein the battlen. loc. sg. of संयुग
  3. देव्याdevyāthe Goddessf. instr. sg. of देवी; governed by सह
  4. सहsahawith, againstindeclinable (postposition with the instrumental)
  5. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
  6. महासुराःmahāsurāḥgreat asurasm. nom. pl.; compound (see 2.40); subject of युयुधुः, resuming अन्ये (2.45)
  7. कोटिकोटिसहस्रैःkoṭikoṭisahasraiḥby thousands of crores of croresn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of वृतः (2.47)
  8. तुtuand (now)indeclinable (particle marking a turn to a new subject)
  9. रथानाम्rathānāmof chariotsm. gen. pl. of रथ; depends on कोटिकोटिसहस्रैः
  10. दन्तिनाम्dantināmof tuskers, of elephantsm. gen. pl. of दन्तिन् (दन्त + possessive -इन्, ‘having tusks’)
  11. तथाtathāand alsoindeclinable (connective)

Translation

great asuras fought there in the battle with the Goddess. And, by thousands of crores of crores of chariots and likewise of elephants

In readable Englishfought there in battle against the Goddess. And with thousands upon thousands of crores of chariots and elephants

The second line runs into 2.47, where Mahiṣāsura is named.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • युयुधुः संयुगे yuyudhuḥ saṃyuge → युयुधुः + संयुगे

    visarga sandhi (optional retention) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34) is optional before a sibilant

    Before s the visarga may stay or become s; the Gita Press keeps the visarga.

  • कोटिकोटिसहस्रैस्तु koṭikoṭisahasraistu → कोटिकोटिसहस्रैः + तु

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • रथानां दन्तिनां rathānāṃ dantināṃ → रथानाम् + दन्तिनाम्

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • कोटिकोटिसहस्रैः koṭikoṭisahasraiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (कोटिकोटि also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, कोटीनां कोटयः)

    Members
    कोटिकोटि + सहस्र
    Vigraha
    कोटिकोटीनां सहस्राणि, तैः koṭikoṭīnāṃ sahasrāṇi, taiḥ
    Literally
    “by thousands of crores of crores”
    In the verse
    by numbers beyond counting
Morphology & anvaya

  • युयुधुः yuyudhuḥ

    √युध् is ātmanepada (युध्यते, युयुधे in 2.41/2.43/2.45). The 3rd plural perfect युयुधुः has the parasmaipada ending -उः: an epic form (ārṣa) for classical युयुधिरे. The text uses it regularly (2.48, 2.53, 2.63).

  • दन्तिनाम् dantinām

    Genitive plural of an in-stem: दन्तिन् + आम् → दन्तिनाम्.

Anvaya · prose order

(अन्ये च) महासुराः तत्र संयुगे देव्या सह युयुधुः। (महिषासुरः) तु रथानां दन्तिनां तथा (हयानां च) कोटिकोटिसहस्रैः (2.47: वृतः)।

(anye ca) mahāsurāḥ tatra saṃyuge devyā saha yuyudhuḥ | (mahiṣāsuraḥ) tu rathānāṃ dantināṃ tathā (hayānāṃ ca) koṭikoṭisahasraiḥ (2.47: vṛtaḥ) |

The first line closes the sentence of 2.45b. With तु the second turns to Mahiṣāsura himself; the instrumental and genitives belong with वृतः in 2.47.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

All the generals together fight the Goddess. Then तु turns to the king himself: Mahiṣāsura’s own forces are counted in ‘thousands of crores of crores’ — a number meant to be beyond counting.

Traditional interpretation

The army grows from tens of thousands to crores of crores while the Goddess remains one. The disproportion is the point of the roll, and it prepares 2.49–50, where she cuts down their weapons ‘as if in play’ (लीलयैव).

Verse 2.47

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

हयानां च वृतो युद्धे तत्राभून्महिषासुरः।तोमरैर्भिन्दिपालैश्च शक्तिभिर्मुसलैस्तथा॥⁠४७⁠॥

hayānāṃ ca vṛto yuddhe tatrābhūnmahiṣāsuraḥ |tomarairbhindipālaiśca śaktibhirmusalaistathā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

हयानाम् · च · वृतः · युद्धे · तत्र · अभूत् · महिषासुरःतोमरैः · भिन्दिपालैः · च · शक्तिभिः · मुसलैः · तथा

hayānām · ca · vṛtaḥ · yuddhe · tatra · abhūt · mahiṣāsuraḥtomaraiḥ · bhindipālaiḥ · ca · śaktibhiḥ · musalaiḥ · tathā

Word by word

  1. हयानाम्hayānāmof horsesm. gen. pl. of हय; depends on कोटिकोटिसहस्रैः (2.46)
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  3. वृतःvṛtaḥsurroundedpast participle (kta) of √वृ, m. nom. sg.; qualifies महिषासुरः
  4. युद्धेyuddhein the battlen. loc. sg. of युद्ध
  5. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
  6. अभूत्abhūtwas, stood√भू, luṅ (aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. महिषासुरःmahiṣāsuraḥMahiṣāsuram. nom. sg.; compound (see 2.36); subject of अभूत्
  8. तोमरैःtomaraiḥwith javelins (tomaras)m. instr. pl. of तोमर (a lance or javelin); instrument of युयुधुः (2.48)
  9. भिन्दिपालैःbhindipālaiḥwith bhindipālas (short throwing-spears)m. instr. pl. of भिन्दिपाल; instrument
  10. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  11. शक्तिभिःśaktibhiḥwith spears (śaktis)f. instr. pl. of शक्ति (here the weapon, a spear or dart); instrument
  12. मुसलैःmusalaiḥwith clubs (musalas)m./n. instr. pl. of मुसल (‘pestle’, a heavy club); instrument
  13. तथाtathāand likewiseindeclinable (connective)

Translation

and of horses, surrounded, Mahiṣāsura was there in the battle. With javelins and bhindipālas, with spears and likewise with clubs —

In readable Englishand horses around him, Mahiṣāsura himself stood in the battle. With javelins and throwing-spears, with lances and clubs

The second line is completed in 2.48 (‘they fought with the Goddess …’).

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • वृतो युद्धे vṛto yuddhe → वृतः + युद्धे

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced y becomes -o.

  • तत्राभून्महिषासुरः tatrābhūnmahiṣāsuraḥ → तत्र + अभूत् + महिषासुरः

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); consonant sandhi (nasalisation) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    a + a → ā; final t before the nasal m becomes n (optional rule, taken here).

  • तोमरैर्भिन्दिपालैश्च tomarairbhindipālaiśca → तोमरैः + भिन्दिपालैः + च

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga after ai before a voiced consonant appears as r; before c it becomes ś.

  • शक्तिभिर्मुसलैस्तथा śaktibhirmusalaistathā → शक्तिभिः + मुसलैः + तथा

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga after i before m appears as r; before t it becomes s.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • अभूत् abhūt

    Root aorist of √भू, ‘was’; with वृतः and the locative it means ‘stood (in the battle), surrounded’. As in 1.4, the aorist alternates with the perfect in narrative.

  • शक्तिभिः śaktibhiḥ

    शक्ति here is a weapon, the spear or dart, as in the gifts of 2.21 (शक्तिं ददौ … हुताशनः); not the abstract ‘power’.

Anvaya · prose order

(रथानां दन्तिनां तथा) हयानां च (कोटिकोटिसहस्रैः) वृतः महिषासुरः तत्र युद्धे अभूत्। तोमरैः भिन्दिपालैः च शक्तिभिः तथा मुसलैः (2.48: … महासुराः देव्या युयुधुः)।

(rathānāṃ dantināṃ tathā) hayānāṃ ca (koṭikoṭisahasraiḥ) vṛtaḥ mahiṣāsuraḥ tatra yuddhe abhūt | tomaraiḥ bhindipālaiḥ ca śaktibhiḥ tathā musalaiḥ (2.48: … mahāsurāḥ devyā yuyudhuḥ) |

The first line completes the sentence of 2.46b. The second line is a string of instrumentals for युयुधुः in 2.48.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Mahiṣāsura himself is in the field, at the centre of an army counted in crores of crores. The weapon list that follows (javelins, bhindipālas, spears, clubs) begins the asuras’ assault, completed in 2.48.

Traditional interpretation

The bhindipāla, named here among the asuras’ weapons, is also carried by the Goddess’s own attendant hosts (भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैः, 2.53) and is the weapon with which she strikes down Bāṣkala (3.18).

Verse 2.48

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

युयुधुः संयुगे देव्या खड्गैः परशुपट्टिशैः।केचिच्च चिक्षिपुः शक्तीः केचित्पाशांस्तथापरे॥⁠४८⁠॥

yuyudhuḥ saṃyuge devyā khaḍgaiḥ paraśupaṭṭiśaiḥ |kecicca cikṣipuḥ śaktīḥ kecitpāśāṃstathāpare ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

युयुधुः · संयुगे · देव्या · खड्गैः · परशुपट्टिशैःकेचित् · च · चिक्षिपुः · शक्तीः · केचित् · पाशान् · तथा · अपरे

yuyudhuḥ · saṃyuge · devyā · khaḍgaiḥ · paraśupaṭṭiśaiḥkecit · ca · cikṣipuḥ · śaktīḥ · kecit · pāśān · tathā · apare

Word by word

  1. युयुधुःyuyudhuḥthey fought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to fight’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, with a parasmaipada ending (see Morphology); subject: the great asuras of 2.46–47
  2. संयुगेsaṃyugein the battlen. loc. sg. of संयुग ‘conflict, battle’
  3. देव्याdevyāwith the Devīf. instr. sg. of देवी; instrumental of the opponent with a verb of fighting (‘fought with’), सह being understood from 2.46
  4. खड्गैःkhaḍgaiḥwith swordsm. instr. pl. of खड्ग; instrument
  5. परशुपट्टिशैःparaśupaṭṭiśaiḥwith axes and paṭṭiśas (edged spears)m. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument
  6. केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun (किम् + चित्), m. nom. pl. के + चित्; subject of चिक्षिपुः
  7. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  8. चिक्षिपुःcikṣipuḥhurled√क्षिप् (tudādi, 6U ‘to throw’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  9. शक्तीःśaktīḥspears (śakti, the throwing spear)f. acc. pl. of शक्ति (i-stem); object of चिक्षिपुः
  10. केचित्kecitsome (others)indefinite pronoun, m. nom. pl.; subject, चिक्षिपुः understood
  11. पाशान्pāśānnoosesm. acc. pl. of पाश; object of the understood चिक्षिपुः
  12. तथाtathālikewise, and so tooindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  13. अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; subject of the clause that runs on into 2.49

Translation

(With lances, javelins, spears and clubs,) with swords, with axes and paṭṭiśas they fought with the Devī in the battle. And some hurled spears, some nooses; and likewise others —

In readable EnglishWith lances and javelins, with spears and maces, with swords, axes and halberds, they fought the Goddess in that battle. Some hurled spears, others nooses; and still others —

The opening phrase is carried over from 2.47b. ‘Halberds’ renders पट्टिश loosely. The verse ends on अपरे, whose action follows in 2.49.

Textual verification / uncertain: अपरे can also be read within this verse as an elliptical third member (‘and others likewise [hurled other weapons]’). Here it is taken with 2.49, on the pattern of तथापरे in 2.57–58, where the clause clearly runs on.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • केचिच्च kecicca → केचित् + च

    consonant sandhi (palatal assimilation) · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Final t before the palatal c becomes the palatal c: केचित् च → केचिच्च.

  • केचित्पाशांस्तथापरे kecitpāśāṃstathāpare → केचित् + पाशान् + तथा + अपरे

    consonant and vowel sandhi · खरि च (8.4.55); नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    The t of केचित् stays unvoiced before p; final n before t becomes ṃs (पाशान् तथा → पाशांस्तथा); ā + a → ā (तथा अपरे → तथापरे).

  • युयुधुः संयुगे yuyudhuḥ saṃyuge → युयुधुः + संयुगे

    visarga before a sibilant · visarga kept before s (the optional alternative to विसर्जनीयस्य सः, 8.3.34)

    Before an initial sibilant the visarga may stand or be assimilated (युयुधुस्संयुगे); the Gita Press keeps it.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • परशुपट्टिशैः paraśupaṭṭiśaiḥ

    itaretara dvandva

    Members
    परशु + पट्टिश
    Vigraha
    परशवः च पट्टिशाः च, तैः paraśavaḥ ca paṭṭiśāḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally
    “with axes and paṭṭiśas”
    In the verse
    two kinds of edged weapon: the battle-axe and the paṭṭiśa, a spear or long blade with a sharp edge
Morphology & anvaya

  • युयुधुः yuyudhuḥ

    √युध् is ātmanepadin (युध्यते; perfect युयुधे, as in 2.41 and 2.43). The plural here takes the parasmaipada ending -उः instead of the classical युयुधिरे — the usual epic and Purāṇic form, used again in 2.53 and 2.63.

  • चिक्षिपुः cikṣipuḥ

    Perfect with reduplication: क्षि → चि (a guttural in the reduplicated syllable becomes the corresponding palatal), then the weak stem क्षिप् with -उः.

Anvaya · prose order

(ते महासुराः तोमरैः भिन्दिपालैः च शक्तिभिः मुसलैः तथा) खड्गैः परशुपट्टिशैः (च) संयुगे देव्या (सह) युयुधुः। केचित् च शक्तीः चिक्षिपुः, केचित् पाशान् (चिक्षिपुः); तथा अपरे —

(te mahāsurāḥ tomaraiḥ bhindipālaiḥ ca śaktibhiḥ musalaiḥ tathā) khaḍgaiḥ paraśupaṭṭiśaiḥ (ca) saṃyuge devyā (saha) yuyudhuḥ | kecit ca śaktīḥ cikṣipuḥ, kecit pāśān (cikṣipuḥ); tathā apare —

The first line completes the sentence begun in 2.47b (तोमरैः … मुसलैः तथा): the list of instruments runs across the verse boundary. The second line distributes the action: ‘some … some … and others’; अपरे is taken as the subject of 2.49.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The asura host, mustered in 2.40–47, attacks all at once. The verse names a whole armoury — swords, axes, paṭṭiśas, spears, nooses — to show the full weight of the assault the Devī is about to cut down.

Traditional interpretation

The weapons the asuras use are largely those the gods have just given the Devī (2.20–31: spear, noose, axe, sword). The battle is fought weapon against weapon, and the text will show hers prevailing.

Verse 2.49

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

देवीं खड्गप्रहारैस्तु ते तां हन्तुं प्रचक्रमुः।सापि देवी ततस्तानि शस्त्राण्यस्त्राणि चण्डिका॥⁠४९⁠॥

devīṃ khaḍgaprahāraistu te tāṃ hantuṃ pracakramuḥ |sāpi devī tatastāni śastrāṇyastrāṇi caṇḍikā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

देवीम् · खड्गप्रहारैः · तु · ते · ताम् · हन्तुम् · प्रचक्रमुःसा · अपि · देवी · ततः · तानि · शस्त्राणि · अस्त्राणि · चण्डिका

devīm · khaḍgaprahāraiḥ · tu · te · tām · hantum · pracakramuḥsā · api · devī · tataḥ · tāni · śastrāṇi · astrāṇi · caṇḍikā

Word by word

  1. देवीम्devīmthe Devīf. acc. sg. of देवी; object of हन्तुम्
  2. खड्गप्रहारैःkhaḍgaprahāraiḥwith sword-strokesm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument
  3. तुtubut, andindeclinable (particle of contrast or continuation)
  4. तेtetheypronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; resumes अपरे of 2.48; subject of प्रचक्रमुः
  5. ताम्tāmherpronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; in apposition to देवीम्
  6. हन्तुम्hantumto killinfinitive (tumun) of √हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’)
  7. प्रचक्रमुःpracakramuḥset about, beganप्र + √क्रम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to step’; with प्र ‘to begin’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  8. साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject of प्रचिच्छेद (2.50)
  9. अपिapi(she) for her part, in turnindeclinable (particle: ‘also, in turn’)
  10. देवीdevīthe Devīf. nom. sg. of देवी; in apposition to सा
  11. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb, तद् + tasil)
  12. तानिtānithosepronoun तद्, n. acc. pl.; qualifies शस्त्राणि and अस्त्राणि
  13. शस्त्राणिśastrāṇiweapons (held in the hand)n. acc. pl. of शस्त्र; object of प्रचिच्छेद (2.50)
  14. अस्त्राणिastrāṇimissiles (weapons thrown or shot)n. acc. pl. of अस्त्र; object of प्रचिच्छेद (2.50)
  15. चण्डिकाcaṇḍikāCaṇḍikā, the fierce onef. nom. sg. of चण्डिका (चण्डी + क); in apposition to सा देवी

Translation

And they set about to kill her, the Devī, with sword-strokes. She too, the Devī Caṇḍikā, then — those weapons and missiles —

In readable Englishset out to cut the Goddess down with blows of the sword. Then the Goddess Caṇḍikā, in her turn, took those weapons and missiles —

The second line has no verb of its own; ‘took’ in the readable version only bridges to ‘cut to pieces’ in 2.50, where the verb stands.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • खड्गप्रहारैस्तु khaḍgaprahāraistu → खड्गप्रहारैः + तु

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s. So too ततः तानि → ततस्तानि.

  • सापि sāpi → सा + अपि

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā.

  • शस्त्राण्यस्त्राणि śastrāṇyastrāṇi → शस्त्राणि + अस्त्राणि

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    Final i before a dissimilar vowel becomes y: -णि अ- → -ण्य-.

  • देवीं खड्ग devīṃ khaḍga → देवीम् + खड्गप्रहारैः

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra; likewise तां हन्तुं प्रचक्रमुः.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • खड्गप्रहारैः khaḍgaprahāraiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    खड्ग + प्रहार
    Vigraha
    खड्गस्य प्रहाराः, तैः khaḍgasya prahārāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally
    “with blows of the sword”
    In the verse
    with sword-strokes
    Alternative
    Also a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa, खड्गेन प्रहाराः ‘blows (dealt) with the sword’; the sense is the same.
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्रचक्रमुः pracakramuḥ

    Perfect of प्र + √क्रम् (reduplication च-क्रम्, क → च). With an infinitive it means ‘set about (doing)’. Classical grammar puts प्र + √क्रम् ‘to begin’ in the ātmanepada (प्रचक्रमिरे); the parasmaipada is the common epic usage.

  • हन्तुम् hantum

    Infinitive in -तुम् (tumun) of √हन्, expressing purpose with a verb of beginning: ‘began to kill’.

  • चण्डिका caṇḍikā

    चण्डी ‘the fierce (woman)’ with the suffix -क: Caṇḍikā, the name by which the Devī is most often called in the middle and last caritas.

Anvaya · prose order

(अपरे) ते तु खड्गप्रहारैः तां देवीं हन्तुं प्रचक्रमुः। ततः सा देवी चण्डिका अपि तानि शस्त्राणि अस्त्राणि (च) —

(apare) te tu khaḍgaprahāraiḥ tāṃ devīṃ hantuṃ pracakramuḥ | tataḥ sā devī caṇḍikā api tāni śastrāṇi astrāṇi (ca) —

Two sentences. The first ends the asuras’ assault. The second has its subject (सा देवी चण्डिका) and objects (तानि शस्त्राणि अस्त्राणि) here and its verb (प्रचिच्छेद) in 2.50.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The attack reaches its height: the asuras close in with swords to kill her. The second line turns at once to the Devī’s answer, which 2.50 completes: she cuts every weapon down.

Traditional interpretation

The pair शस्त्र and अस्त्र is the standard division of weapons: śastra is held and wielded, astra is released — thrown, shot or discharged. Naming both says that no kind of weapon reached her.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also names her Caṇḍikā (name 755, चण्डिका). In the Devī Māhātmya the name belongs above all to the warrior Goddess of these battle chapters.

Verse 2.50

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

लीलयैव प्रचिच्छेद निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणी।अनायस्तानना देवी स्तूयमाना सुरर्षिभिः॥⁠५०⁠॥

līlayaiva praciccheda nijaśastrāstravarṣiṇī |anāyastānanā devī stūyamānā surarṣibhiḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

लीलया · एव · प्रचिच्छेद · निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणीअनायस्तानना · देवी · स्तूयमाना · सुरर्षिभिः

līlayā · eva · praciccheda · nijaśastrāstravarṣiṇīanāyastānanā · devī · stūyamānā · surarṣibhiḥ

Word by word

  1. लीलयाlīlayāin play, effortlesslyf. instr. sg. of लीला ‘play, sport’; instrumental of manner
  2. एवevamerely, justindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  3. प्रचिच्छेदpracicchedacut to piecesप्र + √छिद् (rudhādi, 7U ‘to cut’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; its objects are तानि शस्त्राणि अस्त्राणि (2.49)
  4. निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणीnijaśastrāstravarṣiṇīraining her own weapons and missilesf. nom. sg. (stem -वर्षिन् + ङीप्); compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सा देवी (2.49)
  5. अनायस्ताननाanāyastānanāher face unweariedf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवी
  6. देवीdevīthe Devīf. nom. sg. of देवी; subject of मुमोच (2.51)
  7. स्तूयमानाstūyamānābeing praisedpresent passive participle (śānac with yak) of √स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies देवी
  8. सुरर्षिभिःsurarṣibhiḥby the gods and seersm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of स्तूयमाना

Translation

Raining her own weapons and missiles, she cut (them) to pieces merely in play. The Devī, her face unwearied, being praised by the gods and seers —

In readable Englishand, raining down her own weapons and missiles, cut them to pieces as if in play. Unwearied of face, praised by gods and seers, the Goddess —

The verse divides between two sentences; the dash marks the subject of 2.51.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • लीलयैव līlayaiva → लीलया + एव

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    ā + e → ai.

  • प्रचिच्छेद praciccheda → प्र + चि + छेद

    consonant sandhi inside the word (tuk before ch) · छे च (6.1.73)

    After the short i of the reduplicated syllable, ch is doubled as cch (चि + छेद → चिच्छेद).

  • अनायस्तानना anāyastānanā → अनायस्त + आनना

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā. The same rule joins शस्त्र + अस्त्र in निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणी.

  • सुरर्षिभिः surarṣibhiḥ → सुर + ऋषिभिः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + ṛ → ar.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणी nijaśastrāstravarṣiṇī

    upapada tatpuruṣa (the second member a kṛt form in -इन्, णिनि, ‘habitually doing’); its first member: शस्त्रास्त्र, itaretara dvandva (शस्त्राणि च अस्त्राणि च), qualified by निज (karmadhāraya)

    Members
    निजशस्त्रास्त्र + वर्षिणी
    Vigraha
    निजानि शस्त्राणि अस्त्राणि च वर्षति इति (तच्छीला) nijāni śastrāṇi astrāṇi ca varṣati iti (tacchīlā)
    Literally
    “she who rains her own weapons and missiles”
    In the verse
    pouring out her own arms against theirs as she cuts theirs down

  • अनायस्तानना anāyastānanā

    bahuvrīhi (its first member अनायस्त is a nañ tatpuruṣa: न आयस्तम्)

    Members
    अनायस्त + आनन
    Vigraha
    अनायस्तम् आननं यस्याः सा anāyastam ānanaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “she whose face is not strained”
    In the verse
    showing no sign of effort

  • सुरर्षिभिः surarṣibhiḥ

    itaretara dvandva

    Members
    सुर + ऋषि
    Vigraha
    सुराः च ऋषयः च, तैः surāḥ ca ṛṣayaḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally
    “by gods and seers”
    In the verse
    the gods who hailed her (2.34) and the sages who praised her (2.35)
    Alternative
    Also read as a karmadhāraya, सुराः च ते ऋषयः च ‘divine seers’ (like देवर्षि). The dvandva fits 2.34–35, where gods and sages praise her separately.
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्रचिच्छेद praciccheda

    Perfect of √छिद्: the reduplicated syllable छि becomes चि (an aspirate is reduplicated unaspirated), and the root vowel takes guṇa (छेद) in the singular.

  • स्तूयमाना stūyamānā

    Passive present stem स्तूय- (√स्तु + yak, with the root vowel lengthened before -य-) and the participle ending -मान: ‘being praised’, with the praisers in the instrumental.

  • निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणी nijaśastrāstravarṣiṇī

    The agent suffix -इन् (णिनि) on √वृष् ‘to rain’ (guṇa वर्ष-) gives वर्षिन् ‘raining (by habit)’; the feminine adds -ई: वर्षिणी (n → ṇ after r).

Anvaya · prose order

(सा देवी चण्डिका तानि शस्त्राणि अस्त्राणि) निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणी लीलया एव प्रचिच्छेद। अनायस्तानना सुरर्षिभिः स्तूयमाना देवी —

(sā devī caṇḍikā tāni śastrāṇi astrāṇi) nijaśastrāstravarṣiṇī līlayā eva praciccheda | anāyastānanā surarṣibhiḥ stūyamānā devī —

The first line completes the sentence of 2.49b with its verb प्रचिच्छेद. The second line opens a new sentence whose verb, मुमोच, stands in 2.51.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The point is effortlessness: लीलया एव ‘merely in play’ and अनायस्तानना ‘her face unstrained’. The whole armoury of 2.48 is undone without the Devī showing effort, while gods and seers look on and praise her.

Traditional interpretation

The same phrase returns in the third carita: Caṇḍikā cuts Śumbha’s weapons चिच्छेद लीलया (8.31). The tradition hears in लीला the Devī’s freedom: what she does in the world is play, not labour or need.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has a close image in its own battle with Bhaṇḍa: name 79, भण्डासुरेन्द्रनिर्मुक्तशस्त्रप्रत्यस्त्रवर्षिणी, ‘she who rains counter-missiles against the weapons released by Bhaṇḍāsura’. Both texts use the same agent noun -वर्षिणी for the warrior Goddess; the battles themselves are different stories.

Verse 2.51

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

मुमोचासुरदेहेषु शस्त्राण्यस्त्राणि चेश्वरी।सोऽपि क्रुद्धो धुतसटो देव्या वाहनकेसरी॥⁠५१⁠॥

mumocāsuradeheṣu śastrāṇyastrāṇi ceśvarī |so'pi kruddho dhutasaṭo devyā vāhanakesarī ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

मुमोच · असुरदेहेषु · शस्त्राणि · अस्त्राणि · च · ईश्वरीसः · अपि · क्रुद्धः · धुतसटः · देव्याः · वाहनकेसरी

mumoca · asuradeheṣu · śastrāṇi · astrāṇi · ca · īśvarīsaḥ · api · kruddhaḥ · dhutasaṭaḥ · devyāḥ · vāhanakesarī

Word by word

  1. मुमोचmumocareleased, let fly√मुच् (tudādi, 6U ‘to release’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  2. असुरदेहेषुasuradeheṣuon the bodies of the asurasm. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); locative of the target
  3. शस्त्राणिśastrāṇiweaponsn. acc. pl. of शस्त्र; object of मुमोच
  4. अस्त्राणिastrāṇimissilesn. acc. pl. of अस्त्र; object of मुमोच
  5. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle); joins शस्त्राणि and अस्त्राणि
  6. ईश्वरीīśvarīthe Sovereign (Goddess)f. nom. sg. of ईश्वरी (fem. of ईश्वर ‘lord’); in apposition to देवी (2.50)
  7. सःsaḥhe, that (lion)pronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; subject of चचार (2.52)
  8. अपिapitoo, for his partindeclinable (particle)
  9. क्रुद्धःkruddhaḥenragedpast participle (kta) of √क्रुध् (divādi, 4P ‘to be angry’), m. nom. sg.; active sense
  10. धुतसटःdhutasaṭaḥwith mane shakingm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सः
  11. देव्याःdevyāḥof the Devīf. gen. sg. of देवी; depends on वाहनकेसरी
  12. वाहनकेसरीvāhanakesarīthe lion that was her mountm. nom. sg. (stem केसरिन्); compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to सः

Translation

the Sovereign released weapons and missiles on the bodies of the asuras. He too, enraged, his mane shaken, the lion that was the Devī’s mount —

In readable Englishloosed her weapons and missiles upon the asuras’ bodies, sovereign that she is. And her lion too, the Goddess’s own mount, raging and tossing his mane —

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • मुमोचासुरदेहेषु mumocāsuradeheṣu → मुमोच + असुरदेहेषु

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • चेश्वरी ceśvarī → च + ईश्वरी

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + ī → e.

  • सोऽपि so'pi → सः + अपि

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    Before a vowel सः is treated like any -aḥ: before a it becomes so, and the a of अपि is elided (avagraha).

  • क्रुद्धो धुतसटो देव्या वाहन kruddho dhutasaṭo devyā vāhana → क्रुद्धः + धुतसटः + देव्याः + वाहनकेसरी

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87); भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)

    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o (twice). -āḥ before the voiced v loses its visarga (देव्याः → देव्या), which is why the genitive looks like the instrumental.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरदेहेषु asuradeheṣu

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    असुर + देह
    Vigraha
    असुराणां देहाः, तेषु asurāṇāṃ dehāḥ, teṣu
    Literally
    “on the bodies of the asuras”
    In the verse
    her weapons now strike the enemy themselves, not only their weapons

  • धुतसटः dhutasaṭaḥ

    bahuvrīhi (the feminine सटा ‘mane’ is shortened to -सट as the last member of a bahuvrīhi qualifying a masculine)

    Members
    धुत + सटा
    Vigraha
    धुताः सटाः यस्य सः dhutāḥ saṭāḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose mane-hairs are shaken”
    In the verse
    tossing his mane in fury

  • वाहनकेसरी vāhanakesarī

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    वाहन + केसरिन्
    Vigraha
    वाहनं च असौ केसरी च vāhanaṃ ca asau kesarī ca
    Literally
    “the mount-lion”
    In the verse
    the lion given by Himavat as her vehicle (2.29)
Morphology & anvaya

  • मुमोच mumoca

    Perfect of √मुच्: reduplication मु-, guṇa of the root vowel in the singular (मोच). Its ātmanepada counterpart मुमुचे follows in 2.52.

  • देव्याः devyāḥ

    Genitive singular of देवी. The verse prints देव्या because -āḥ drops its visarga before a voiced sound; the sense (‘the Devī’s mount’) shows it is the genitive, not the instrumental देव्या.

  • क्रुद्धः kruddhaḥ

    kta of √क्रुध्: dh + t → ddh (क्रुध् + त → क्रुद्ध). Intransitive, so active in sense: ‘angered’.

Anvaya · prose order

(अनायस्तानना सुरर्षिभिः स्तूयमाना देवी) ईश्वरी असुरदेहेषु शस्त्राणि अस्त्राणि च मुमोच। देव्याः वाहनकेसरी सः अपि क्रुद्धः धुतसटः —

(anāyastānanā surarṣibhiḥ stūyamānā devī) īśvarī asuradeheṣu śastrāṇi astrāṇi ca mumoca | devyāḥ vāhanakesarī saḥ api kruddhaḥ dhutasaṭaḥ —

The first line completes the sentence opened in 2.50b. The second begins a new one: subject (सः … वाहनकेसरी) with two qualifiers; its verb, चचार, is in 2.52.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Defence turns to attack: having destroyed the asuras’ weapons, the Devī now looses her own on their bodies. The lion given her by Himavat (2.29) enters the fight in his own right.

Traditional interpretation

ईश्वरी, ‘sovereign’, is chosen at the moment she goes on the offensive: the battle is the exercise of her lordship, not a contest between equals.

Verse 2.52

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

चचारासुरसैन्येषु वनेष्विव हुताशनः।निःश्वासान् मुमुचे यांश्च युध्यमाना रणेऽम्बिका॥⁠५२⁠॥

cacārāsurasainyeṣu vaneṣviva hutāśanaḥ |niḥśvāsān mumuce yāṃśca yudhyamānā raṇe'mbikā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

चचार · असुरसैन्येषु · वनेषु · इव · हुताशनःनिःश्वासान् · मुमुचे · यान् · च · युध्यमाना · रणे · अम्बिका

cacāra · asurasainyeṣu · vaneṣu · iva · hutāśanaḥniḥśvāsān · mumuce · yān · ca · yudhyamānā · raṇe · ambikā

Word by word

  1. चचारcacāraranged, moved through√चर् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to move’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; subject: the lion (2.51)
  2. असुरसैन्येषुasurasainyeṣuamong the asura armiesn. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
  3. वनेषुvaneṣuin forestsn. loc. pl. of वन
  4. इवivalike, asindeclinable (particle of comparison)
  5. हुताशनःhutāśanaḥfire (lit. ‘the eater of oblations’)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); the standard of comparison
  6. निःश्वासान्niḥśvāsānbreaths, exhalationsm. acc. pl. of निःश्वास (निस् + √श्वस् + घञ्); object of मुमुचे
  7. मुमुचेmumucereleased, breathed out√मुच् (tudādi, 6U), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  8. यान्yānwhichrelative pronoun यद्, m. acc. pl.; agrees with निःश्वासान्; correlative ते in 2.53
  9. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle); links the new sentence to the narrative
  10. युध्यमानाyudhyamānāfightingpresent participle (śānac) of √युध् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to fight’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies अम्बिका
  11. रणेraṇein battlem. loc. sg. of रण
  12. अम्बिकाambikāAmbikā, the Motherf. nom. sg. of अम्बिका; subject of मुमुचे

Translation

ranged among the asura armies like fire in forests. And the breaths which Ambikā, fighting in battle, released —

In readable Englishraged through the asura ranks like a fire through the forests. And every breath that Ambikā breathed out as she fought —

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • चचारासुरसैन्येषु cacārāsurasainyeṣu → चचार + असुरसैन्येषु

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • वनेष्विव vaneṣviva → वनेषु + इव

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    u before the dissimilar i becomes v.

  • यांश्च yāṃśca → यान् + च

    consonant sandhi (n before c) · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Final n before c becomes anusvāra + s, and the s becomes the palatal ś before c: यांश्च.

  • रणेऽम्बिका raṇe'mbikā → रणे + अम्बिका

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is elided (avagraha).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरसैन्येषु asurasainyeṣu

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    असुर + सैन्य
    Vigraha
    असुराणां सैन्यानि, तेषु asurāṇāṃ sainyāni, teṣu
    Literally
    “among the armies of the asuras”
    In the verse
    through the ranks of the enemy

  • हुताशनः hutāśanaḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    हुत + अशन
    Vigraha
    हुतम् अशनं यस्य सः hutam aśanaṃ yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose food is what is offered (into the fire)”
    In the verse
    Agni, fire
    Alternative
    Also analysed as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa with अशन as an agent noun, हुतस्य अशनः ‘the eater of the oblation’; both analyses give the same name for fire.
Morphology & anvaya

  • मुमुचे mumuce

    The ātmanepada perfect of √मुच्, beside the parasmaipada मुमोच of 2.51: √मुच् is ubhayapadin. The ātmanepada suits an action whose fruit stays with the agent — here, her own breath.

  • युध्यमाना yudhyamānā

    Present middle participle of √युध्: the divādi stem युध्य- + -मान (śānac). It is not passive; √युध् is itself ātmanepadin.

Anvaya · prose order

(सः) वनेषु हुताशनः इव असुरसैन्येषु चचार। रणे युध्यमाना अम्बिका यान् च निःश्वासान् मुमुचे —

(saḥ) vaneṣu hutāśanaḥ iva asurasainyeṣu cacāra | raṇe yudhyamānā ambikā yān ca niḥśvāsān mumuce —

The first line completes the lion’s sentence from 2.51b, with a simile. The second line is a relative clause (यान् … मुमुचे) whose main clause (ते एव … गणाः) is in 2.53.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Two images of the Devī’s power multiplying. The lion goes through the army like a forest fire; and the Devī’s very breathing becomes a weapon, as the next verse shows.

Traditional interpretation
