The contrast with Mahiṣa is drawn by the text itself: in the next chapter Mahiṣāsura fells the Devī’s troops with the wind of his breath (निःश्वासपवनेन, 3.23). Her breath brings forth an army; his only destroys.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also names her Ambikā (name 295, अम्बिका), ‘the Mother’ — the name the Devī Māhātmya uses most often in its battle scenes.

Verse 2.53

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

त एव सद्यः सम्भूता गणाः शतसहस्रशः।युयुधुस्ते परशुभिर्भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैः॥⁠५३⁠॥

ta eva sadyaḥ sambhūtā gaṇāḥ śatasahasraśaḥ |yuyudhuste paraśubhirbhindipālāsipaṭṭiśaiḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ते · एव · सद्यः · सम्भूताः · गणाः · शतसहस्रशःयुयुधुः · ते · परशुभिः · भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैः

te · eva · sadyaḥ · sambhūtāḥ · gaṇāḥ · śatasahasraśaḥyuyudhuḥ · te · paraśubhiḥ · bhindipālāsipaṭṭiśaiḥ

Word by word

  1. तेtethose (breaths)pronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; correlative of यान् (2.52), referring to निःश्वासाः; subject of सम्भूताः
  2. एवevavery, themselvesindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  3. सद्यःsadyaḥat once, instantlyindeclinable (adverb of time)
  4. सम्भूताःsambhūtāḥcame into being, becamepast participle (kta) of सम् + √भू (bhvādi, 1P), m. nom. pl.; active sense, used as the finite verb
  5. गणाःgaṇāḥtroops, hosts (gaṇas)m. nom. pl. of गण; predicate nominative with सम्भूताः
  6. शतसहस्रशःśatasahasraśaḥby hundreds of thousandsindeclinable (शतसहस्र + taddhita -शस् ‘by …s’)
  7. युयुधुःyuyudhuḥfought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada ending (as in 2.48)
  8. तेtethey (the gaṇas)pronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; subject of युयुधुः
  9. परशुभिःparaśubhiḥwith axesm. instr. pl. of परशु (u-stem); instrument
  10. भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैःbhindipālāsipaṭṭiśaiḥwith javelins, swords and paṭṭiśasm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument

Translation

those very (breaths) at once became gaṇas by hundreds of thousands. They fought with axes, with javelins, swords and paṭṭiśas,

In readable Englishbecame at once her hosts, hundreds of thousands strong. They fought with axes, javelins, swords and halberds,

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • त एव ta eva → ते + एव

    vowel sandhi (ayādi with y dropped) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)

    Final e before a vowel other than a becomes ay; the y is dropped and the hiatus remains (त एव).

  • युयुधुस्ते yuyudhuste → युयुधुः + ते

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • परशुभिर्भिन्दि paraśubhirbhindi → परशुभिः + भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After a vowel other than a/ā, the visarga (रु) appears as r before a voiced sound.

  • भिन्दिपालासि bhindipālāsi → भिन्दिपाल + असि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैः bhindipālāsipaṭṭiśaiḥ

    itaretara dvandva

    Members
    भिन्दिपाल + असि + पट्टिश
    Vigraha
    भिन्दिपालाः च असयः च पट्टिशाः च, तैः bhindipālāḥ ca asayaḥ ca paṭṭiśāḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally
    “with javelins and swords and paṭṭiśas”
    In the verse
    three kinds of weapon: the bhindipāla (a short throwing javelin), the sword and the edged spear
Morphology & anvaya

  • सम्भूताः sambhūtāḥ

    kta of an intransitive verb, active in sense and standing for the finite verb: ‘(they) came into being’. The predicate गणाः says what they became.

  • शतसहस्रशः śatasahasraśaḥ

    The numeral शतसहस्र ‘a hundred thousand’ (शत ‘hundred’ + सहस्र ‘thousand’) with the taddhita -शस्, which makes distributive adverbs from numbers and quantities (शतशः ‘by hundreds’, 2.56). It is indeclinable.

Anvaya · prose order

ते एव (निःश्वासाः) सद्यः शतसहस्रशः गणाः सम्भूताः। ते (गणाः) परशुभिः भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैः (च) युयुधुः।

te eva (niḥśvāsāḥ) sadyaḥ śatasahasraśaḥ gaṇāḥ sambhūtāḥ | te (gaṇāḥ) paraśubhiḥ bhindipālāsipaṭṭiśaiḥ (ca) yuyudhuḥ |

The main clause answers the relative clause of 2.52 (यान् … ते एव). The second line begins a new sentence whose participle (नाशयन्तः) runs into 2.54.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The Devī’s breath becomes an army. She does not summon allies from outside: her own exhalations take form as gaṇas, hundreds of thousands of them, and go into battle with weapons of their own.

Traditional interpretation

The text itself later states the principle behind this: when Śumbha taunts her for relying on others, the Devī answers that she alone is there, and the powers he saw were hers, returning into her (10.5, एकैवाहं जगत्यत्र). The gaṇas here are an early instance: her helpers are her own power.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) likewise gives its Goddess an army that is her own power: name 65, भण्डासुरवधोद्युक्तशक्तिसेनासमन्विता, ‘accompanied by an army of śaktis intent on slaying Bhaṇḍāsura’. The idea is shared; the stories are separate.

Verse 2.54

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

नाशयन्तोऽसुरगणान् देवीशक्त्युपबृंहिताः।अवादयन्त पटहान् गणाः शङ्खांस्तथापरे॥⁠५४⁠॥

nāśayanto'suragaṇān devīśaktyupabṛṃhitāḥ |avādayanta paṭahān gaṇāḥ śaṅkhāṃstathāpare ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

नाशयन्तः · असुरगणान् · देवीशक्त्युपबृंहिताःअवादयन्त · पटहान् · गणाः · शङ्खान् · तथा · अपरे

nāśayantaḥ · asuragaṇān · devīśaktyupabṛṃhitāḥavādayanta · paṭahān · gaṇāḥ · śaṅkhān · tathā · apare

Word by word

  1. नाशयन्तःnāśayantaḥdestroyingpresent participle (śatṛ) of the causative of √नश् (divādi, 4P ‘to perish’; causative नाशय- ‘to destroy’), m. nom. pl.; qualifies ते (the gaṇas, 2.53)
  2. असुरगणान्asuragaṇānthe hosts of asurasm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of नाशयन्तः
  3. देवीशक्त्युपबृंहिताःdevīśaktyupabṛṃhitāḥstrengthened by the Devī’s powerm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ते (the gaṇas)
  4. अवादयन्तavādayantasounded, playedcausative of √वद् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to speak’; causative वादय- ‘to make sound, to play’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, ātmanepada
  5. पटहान्paṭahānwar-drumsm. acc. pl. of पटह; object of अवादयन्त
  6. गणाःgaṇāḥ(some of the) gaṇasm. nom. pl. of गण; subject of अवादयन्त
  7. शङ्खान्śaṅkhānconchesm. acc. pl. of शङ्ख; object of the understood अवादयन्त
  8. तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  9. अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; subject of the understood अवादयन्त

Translation

destroying the hosts of the asuras, strengthened by the Devī’s power. (Some) gaṇas sounded war-drums, and likewise others conches,

In readable Englishdestroying the asura hosts, made mighty by the Goddess’s power. Some of the hosts beat the war-drums, others blew the conches,

‘Beat’ and ‘blew’ render the one verb अवादयन्त ‘sounded’ for each instrument.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • नाशयन्तोऽसुरगणान् nāśayanto'suragaṇān → नाशयन्तः + असुरगणान्

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a- becomes -o, and the following a is elided (avagraha).

  • शक्त्युप śaktyupa → शक्ति + उपबृंहिताः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    i before the dissimilar u becomes y.

  • शङ्खांस्तथापरे śaṅkhāṃstathāpare → शङ्खान् + तथा + अपरे

    consonant and vowel sandhi · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Final n before t becomes ṃs; ā + a → ā. The same pattern as पाशांस्तथापरे in 2.48.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरगणान् asuragaṇān

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    असुर + गण
    Vigraha
    असुराणां गणाः, तान् asurāṇāṃ gaṇāḥ, tān
    Literally
    “the hosts of the asuras”
    In the verse
    the enemy troops — set against the Devī’s own गणाः

  • देवीशक्त्युपबृंहिताः devīśaktyupabṛṃhitāḥ

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (देवीशक्ति)

    Members
    देवीशक्ति + उपबृंहित
    Vigraha
    देवीशक्त्या उपबृंहिताः; देवीशक्तिः = देव्याः शक्तिः devīśaktyā upabṛṃhitāḥ; devīśaktiḥ = devyāḥ śaktiḥ
    Literally
    “made strong by the power of the Devī”
    In the verse
    the gaṇas fight with the strength of the one who breathed them out
Morphology & anvaya

  • नाशयन्तः nāśayantaḥ

    Causative stem नाशय- (√नश् with vṛddhi + णिच्) and the present participle ending -अत् (śatṛ): ‘causing to perish’, i.e. destroying.

  • अवादयन्त avādayanta

    Imperfect of the causative वादय- ‘to make speak’, the regular verb for playing an instrument. Causatives take either voice; the ātmanepada ending -अन्त is used here.

  • देवीशक्त्युपबृंहिताः devīśaktyupabṛṃhitāḥ

    Its last member उपबृंहित is the kta of उप + √बृंह् ‘to make great, strengthen’: ‘reinforced’.

Anvaya · prose order

(ते गणाः) देवीशक्त्युपबृंहिताः असुरगणान् नाशयन्तः (युयुधुः)। गणाः पटहान् अवादयन्त, तथा अपरे शङ्खान् (अवादयन्त),

(te gaṇāḥ) devīśaktyupabṛṃhitāḥ asuragaṇān nāśayantaḥ (yuyudhuḥ) | gaṇāḥ paṭahān avādayanta, tathā apare śaṅkhān (avādayanta),

The first line belongs with 2.53b: participle and qualifier of the fighting gaṇas. The second line starts the list of instruments, which continues in 2.55a.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gaṇas both fight and make music. Their strength is not their own: it is the Devī’s power (देवीशक्ति) that swells them. Drums and conches are the sounds of an army sure of victory.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the same beings destroy and celebrate in one breath. The text does not separate the battle from the festival — 2.55 will call it a ‘great festival of war’.

Verse 2.55

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

मृदङ्गांश्च तथैवान्ये तस्मिन् युद्धमहोत्सवे।ततो देवी त्रिशूलेन गदया शक्तिवृष्टिभिः॥⁠५५⁠॥

mṛdaṅgāṃśca tathaivānye tasmin yuddhamahotsave |tato devī triśūlena gadayā śaktivṛṣṭibhiḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

मृदङ्गान् · च · तथा · एव · अन्ये · तस्मिन् · युद्धमहोत्सवेततः · देवी · त्रिशूलेन · गदया · शक्तिवृष्टिभिः

mṛdaṅgān · ca · tathā · eva · anye · tasmin · yuddhamahotsavetataḥ · devī · triśūlena · gadayā · śaktivṛṣṭibhiḥ

Word by word

  1. मृदङ्गान्mṛdaṅgānmṛdaṅga drumsm. acc. pl. of मृदङ्ग; object of the understood अवादयन्त (2.54)
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  3. तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  4. एवevajust soindeclinable (emphatic particle); तथा एव ‘in just the same way’
  5. अन्येanyeotherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. nom. pl.; subject of the understood अवादयन्त
  6. तस्मिन्tasminin thatpronoun तद्, m. loc. sg.; qualifies युद्धमहोत्सवे
  7. युद्धमहोत्सवेyuddhamahotsavegreat festival of battlem. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  8. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb, तद् + tasil)
  9. देवीdevīthe Devīf. nom. sg. of देवी; subject of निजघान (2.56)
  10. त्रिशूलेनtriśūlenawith the tridentn. instr. sg. of त्रिशूल; compound (see Samāsa); instrument
  11. गदयाgadayāwith the macef. instr. sg. of गदा; instrument
  12. शक्तिवृष्टिभिःśaktivṛṣṭibhiḥwith showers of spearsf. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument

Translation

and others, just so, (sounded) mṛdaṅgas in that great festival of battle. Then the Devī, with the trident, with the mace, with showers of spears —

In readable Englishand others the mṛdaṅga drums, in that great festival of war. Then the Goddess, with trident and mace and showers of spears,

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • मृदङ्गांश्च mṛdaṅgāṃśca → मृदङ्गान् + च

    consonant sandhi (n before c) · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    n before c becomes ṃś, as in यांश्च (2.52).

  • तथैवान्ये tathaivānye → तथा + एव + अन्ये

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi, then savarṇa-dīrgha) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + e → ai (तथैव); a + a → ā (एवान्ये).

  • युद्धमहोत्सवे yuddhamahotsave → युद्ध + महा + उत्सवे

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    ā + u → o (महा + उत्सव → महोत्सव).

  • ततो देवी tato devī → ततः + देवी

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • युद्धमहोत्सवे yuddhamahotsave

    karmadhāraya (a metaphor, rūpaka: the battle is identified with a festival) containing a karmadhāraya (महोत्सव)

    Members
    युद्ध + महोत्सव
    Vigraha
    युद्धम् एव महोत्सवः, तस्मिन्; महोत्सवः = महान् उत्सवः yuddham eva mahotsavaḥ, tasmin; mahotsavaḥ = mahān utsavaḥ
    Literally
    “in the battle that was a great festival”
    In the verse
    the war, celebrated with drums and conches, is a festival for the Devī’s side
    Alternative
    Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, युद्धस्य महोत्सवः ‘the great festival of battle’; the identifying karmadhāraya fits the drums and conches of 2.54–55.

  • त्रिशूलेन triśūlena

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    त्रि + शूल
    Vigraha
    त्रीणि शूलानि यस्मिन् तत्, तेन trīṇi śūlāni yasmin tat, tena
    Literally
    “that which has three points”
    In the verse
    the trident, the gift of Śiva (2.20)

  • शक्तिवृष्टिभिः śaktivṛṣṭibhiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    शक्ति + वृष्टि
    Vigraha
    शक्तीनां वृष्टयः, ताभिः śaktīnāṃ vṛṣṭayaḥ, tābhiḥ
    Literally
    “with rains of spears”
    In the verse
    with volleys of thrown spears
Morphology & anvaya

  • तस्मिन् tasmin

    Locative singular of the pronoun तद् (masculine/neuter), with the pronominal ending -स्मिन्.

Anvaya · prose order

अन्ये च तथा एव तस्मिन् युद्धमहोत्सवे मृदङ्गान् (अवादयन्त)। ततः देवी त्रिशूलेन गदया शक्तिवृष्टिभिः —

anye ca tathā eva tasmin yuddhamahotsave mṛdaṅgān (avādayanta) | tataḥ devī triśūlena gadayā śaktivṛṣṭibhiḥ —

The first line closes the sentence of 2.54b (the verb अवादयन्त is carried over). The second line opens a new sentence whose verb, निजघान, is in 2.56.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The orchestra is completed — war-drums, conches, mṛdaṅgas — and the battle is named a ‘great festival’. Then the Devī herself takes up her weapons: the trident, the mace and the spear-showers begin the list that runs into 2.56.

Traditional interpretation

Several of these weapons are the gods’ gifts of 2.20–24: Śiva’s trident, Agni’s śakti, and the sword (2.56) given by Kāla. The armed form the gods made is now at work.

Verse 2.56

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

खड्गादिभिश्च शतशो निजघान महासुरान्।पातयामास चैवान्यान् घण्टास्वनविमोहितान्॥⁠५६⁠॥

khaḍgādibhiśca śataśo nijaghāna mahāsurān |pātayāmāsa caivānyān ghaṇṭāsvanavimohitān ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

खड्गादिभिः · च · शतशः · निजघान · महासुरान्पातयामास · च · एव · अन्यान् · घण्टास्वनविमोहितान्

khaḍgādibhiḥ · ca · śataśaḥ · nijaghāna · mahāsurānpātayāmāsa · ca · eva · anyān · ghaṇṭāsvanavimohitān

Word by word

  1. खड्गादिभिःkhaḍgādibhiḥwith sword and the restm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  3. शतशःśataśaḥby hundredsindeclinable (शत + taddhita -शस्)
  4. निजघानnijaghānastruck down, slewनि + √हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; subject देवी (2.55)
  5. महासुरान्mahāsurānthe great asurasm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of निजघान
  6. पातयामासpātayāmāsafelled, made to fallcausative of √पत् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to fall’), periphrastic liṭ (perfect with आम्), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  8. एवevaindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  9. अन्यान्anyānotherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. acc. pl.; object of पातयामास
  10. घण्टास्वनविमोहितान्ghaṇṭāsvanavimohitānstupefied by the sound of her bellm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अन्यान्

Translation

and with the sword and the rest, struck down the great asuras by hundreds; and she felled others, stupefied by the sound of the bell.

In readable Englishwith sword and every other weapon, struck down the great asuras by the hundred; and others, dazed by the ringing of her bell, she brought to the ground.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • खड्गादिभिश्च khaḍgādibhiśca → खड्गादिभिः + च

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c becomes s, which turns palatal ś.

  • शतशो निजघान śataśo nijaghāna → शतशः + निजघान

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced n becomes -o.

  • चैवान्यान् caivānyān → च + एव + अन्यान्

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi, then savarṇa-dīrgha) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + e → ai; a + a → ā.

  • खड्गादिभि khaḍgādibhi → खड्ग + आदि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • खड्गादिभिः khaḍgādibhiḥ

    bahuvrīhi (tadguṇasaṃvijñāna: the sword is itself included)

    Members
    खड्ग + आदि
    Vigraha
    खड्गः आदिः येषां तैः khaḍgaḥ ādiḥ yeṣāṃ taiḥ
    Literally
    “with those of which the sword is the first”
    In the verse
    with the sword and her other weapons

  • महासुरान् mahāsurān

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + असुर
    Vigraha
    महान्तः असुराः, तान् mahāntaḥ asurāḥ, tān
    Literally
    “great asuras”
    In the verse
    the asura champions (महत् takes the form महा- as the first member of a karmadhāraya)

  • घण्टास्वनविमोहितान् ghaṇṭāsvanavimohitān

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (घण्टास्वन)

    Members
    घण्टास्वन + विमोहित
    Vigraha
    घण्टास्वनेन विमोहिताः, तान्; घण्टास्वनः = घण्टायाः स्वनः ghaṇṭāsvanena vimohitāḥ, tān; ghaṇṭāsvanaḥ = ghaṇṭāyāḥ svanaḥ
    Literally
    “stupefied by the sound of the bell”
    In the verse
    dazed by the ringing of the bell Indra gave her (2.22)
Morphology & anvaya

  • निजघान nijaghāna

    Perfect of √हन्: in the reduplicated form the h appears as j in the reduplicating syllable and as gh in the root (ज-घान), with vṛddhi of the root vowel in the 3rd singular.

  • पातयामास pātayāmāsa

    Periphrastic perfect: the causative stem पातय- + -आम् + the perfect of √अस् (आस). Causatives form their perfect only this way.

Anvaya · prose order

(ततः देवी त्रिशूलेन गदया शक्तिवृष्टिभिः) खड्गादिभिः च शतशः महासुरान् निजघान। घण्टास्वनविमोहितान् अन्यान् च एव पातयामास।

(tataḥ devī triśūlena gadayā śaktivṛṣṭibhiḥ) khaḍgādibhiḥ ca śataśaḥ mahāsurān nijaghāna | ghaṇṭāsvanavimohitān anyān ca eva pātayāmāsa |

The first line completes the sentence of 2.55b with its object and verb. The second adds a parallel clause with the same subject.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The Devī’s own slaughter begins. Even her bell is a weapon: its sound stupefies the asuras before they are felled.

Traditional interpretation

The bell is Indra’s gift, taken from Airāvata (2.22). It returns in the gods’ hymn of the eleventh chapter, which asks that the bell that fills the world with its sound and destroys the daityas’ might protect them from sin, as one guards one’s children (11.27).

Verse 2.57

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

असुरान् भुवि पाशेन बद्ध्वा चान्यानकर्षयत्।केचिद् द्विधा कृतास्तीक्ष्णैः खड्गपातैस्तथापरे॥⁠५७⁠॥

asurān bhuvi pāśena baddhvā cānyānakarṣayat |kecid dvidhā kṛtāstīkṣṇaiḥ khaḍgapātaistathāpare ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

असुरान् · भुवि · पाशेन · बद्ध्वा · च · अन्यान् · अकर्षयत्केचित् · द्विधा · कृताः · तीक्ष्णैः · खड्गपातैः · तथा · अपरे

asurān · bhuvi · pāśena · baddhvā · ca · anyān · akarṣayatkecit · dvidhā · kṛtāḥ · tīkṣṇaiḥ · khaḍgapātaiḥ · tathā · apare

Word by word

  1. असुरान्asurānasurasm. acc. pl. of असुर; object of बद्ध्वा and अकर्षयत्, with अन्यान्
  2. भुविbhuvion the groundf. loc. sg. of भू ‘earth’
  3. पाशेनpāśenawith the noosem. instr. sg. of पाश; instrument
  4. बद्ध्वाbaddhvāhaving boundabsolutive (ktvā) of √बन्ध् (kryādi, 9P ‘to bind’)
  5. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  6. अन्यान्anyānotherpronominal adjective अन्य, m. acc. pl.; qualifies असुरान्
  7. अकर्षयत्akarṣayatdraggedcausative stem of √कृष् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to draw, drag’), used in the simple sense; laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun (किम् + चित्), m. nom. pl.; subject
  9. द्विधाdvidhāin twoindeclinable (द्वि + taddhita -धा ‘in … ways/parts’)
  10. कृताःkṛtāḥwere made, were cutpast passive participle (kta) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U ‘to do, make’), m. nom. pl.; used as the finite verb
  11. तीक्ष्णैःtīkṣṇaiḥby sharpadjective तीक्ष्ण, m. instr. pl.; qualifies खड्गपातैः
  12. खड्गपातैःkhaḍgapātaiḥby sword-strokesm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument with कृताः
  13. तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  14. अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; subject of शेरते in 2.58

Translation

And binding other asuras with the noose, she dragged (them) on the ground. Some were made in two by sharp sword-strokes; likewise others —

In readable EnglishOthers she bound with her noose and dragged along the ground. Some were cut in two by the keen strokes of her sword; and others —

भुवि may also go with बद्ध्वा (‘bound on the ground’); the readable version joins it to the dragging.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • चान्यानकर्षयत् cānyānakarṣayat → च + अन्यान् + अकर्षयत्

    vowel sandhi and a consonant joined to a vowel · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā (चान्यान्); the final n of अन्यान् simply joins the following vowel (no doubling after the long ā).

  • केचिद् द्विधा kecid dvidhā → केचित् + द्विधा

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before the voiced d; the Gita Press writes it with a virāma and a space.

  • कृतास्तीक्ष्णैः kṛtāstīkṣṇaiḥ → कृताः + तीक्ष्णैः

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s; so too खड्गपातैः तथा → खड्गपातैस्तथा.

  • खड्गपातैस्तथापरे khaḍgapātaistathāpare → खड्गपातैः + तथा + अपरे

    visarga and vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Visarga → s before t; ā + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • खड्गपातैः khaḍgapātaiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    खड्ग + पात
    Vigraha
    खड्गस्य पाताः, तैः khaḍgasya pātāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally
    “by the fallings of the sword”
    In the verse
    by sword-strokes coming down
Morphology & anvaya

  • बद्ध्वा baddhvā

    Absolutive of √बन्ध्: the nasal drops (बध्), and dh + t → ddh (बद्ध्वा). The binding precedes the dragging.

  • अकर्षयत् akarṣayat

    A causative form (कर्षय-) with the meaning of the simple verb ‘dragged’; the causative suffix here adds no ‘make someone do’. The simple imperfect would be अकर्षत्.

  • द्विधा dvidhā

    Numeral adverb with -धा: द्विधा ‘in two (parts)’, here with कृताः ‘were cut in two’.

Anvaya · prose order

अन्यान् च असुरान् पाशेन बद्ध्वा भुवि अकर्षयत्। केचित् तीक्ष्णैः खड्गपातैः द्विधा कृताः। तथा अपरे —

anyān ca asurān pāśena baddhvā bhuvi akarṣayat | kecit tīkṣṇaiḥ khaḍgapātaiḥ dvidhā kṛtāḥ | tathā apare —

First line: absolutive + finite verb with the Devī as subject. Second: a passive participle as verb with an instrumental of means. अपरे opens the sentence completed in 2.58.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Each weapon has its own work: the noose binds and drags, the sword cuts in two. The verse begins the catalogue of the asuras’ deaths that runs to 2.64.

Traditional interpretation

The noose is Varuṇa’s gift (2.23). In iconography the pāśa is the binding power that the Goddess holds over those who oppose her.

Verse 2.58

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

विपोथिता निपातेन गदया भुवि शेरते।वेमुश्च केचिद्रुधिरं मुसलेन भृशं हताः॥⁠५८⁠॥

vipothitā nipātena gadayā bhuvi śerate |vemuśca kecidrudhiraṃ musalena bhṛśaṃ hatāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

विपोथिताः · निपातेन · गदया · भुवि · शेरतेवेमुः · च · केचित् · रुधिरम् · मुसलेन · भृशम् · हताः

vipothitāḥ · nipātena · gadayā · bhuvi · śeratevemuḥ · ca · kecit · rudhiram · musalena · bhṛśam · hatāḥ

Word by word

  1. विपोथिताःvipothitāḥcrushedpast passive participle (kta) of वि + √पुथ् (causative stem पोथय- ‘to crush’), m. nom. pl.; qualifies अपरे (2.57)
  2. निपातेनnipātenaby the blow, by the downward strokem. instr. sg. of निपात (नि + √पत् + घञ्); instrument
  3. गदयाgadayāwith the macef. instr. sg. of गदा; instrument, beside निपातेन
  4. भुविbhuvion the groundf. loc. sg. of भू
  5. शेरतेśeratelie√शी (adādi, 2Ā ‘to lie’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, ātmanepada
  6. वेमुःvemuḥvomited√वम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to vomit, spew’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  7. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  8. केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun (किम् + चित्), m. nom. pl.; subject of वेमुः
  9. रुधिरम्rudhirambloodn. acc. sg. of रुधिर; object of वेमुः
  10. मुसलेनmusalenaby the club (musala)m. instr. sg. of मुसल ‘pestle, club’; instrument with हताः
  11. भृशम्bhṛśamviolently, hardindeclinable (adverbial acc. of भृश)
  12. हताःhatāḥstruckpast passive participle (kta) of √हन् (adādi, 2P), m. nom. pl.; qualifies केचित्

Translation

(others,) crushed by the blow, by the mace, lie on the ground. And some, struck hard by the club, vomited blood.

In readable Englishothers lie on the ground, crushed by the blows of her mace; and some, struck hard by her club, spewed blood.

निपातेन गदया, two instrumentals side by side (‘by the blow, with the mace’), are rendered as one phrase.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • विपोथिता निपातेन vipothitā nipātena → विपोथिताः + निपातेन

    visarga sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)

    -āḥ before a voiced consonant loses its visarga.

  • वेमुश्च vemuśca → वेमुः + च

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c → s → ś.

  • केचिद्रुधिरं kecidrudhiraṃ → केचित् + रुधिरम्

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before the voiced r. The final m of रुधिरम् becomes anusvāra before m (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • शेरते śerate

    Present of √शी ‘to lie’: the root takes guṇa throughout (शे-), and the 3rd plural inserts r before the ending (शे-र-ते). The present here is the narrative (historic) present: ‘they lie’ amid perfects.

  • वेमुः vemuḥ

    Perfect of √वम् in the form without reduplication, with e in the root (वेमुः, like पेचुः from √पच्); grammarians allow it for √वम् beside the reduplicated ववमुः.

  • विपोथिताः vipothitāḥ

    kta from the causative stem पोथय- of √पुथ् (the causative suffix drops before -इत): ‘made to be crushed’, i.e. pounded flat.

Anvaya · prose order

(अपरे) गदया निपातेन विपोथिताः भुवि शेरते। केचित् च मुसलेन भृशं हताः रुधिरं वेमुः।

(apare) gadayā nipātena vipothitāḥ bhuvi śerate | kecit ca musalena bhṛśaṃ hatāḥ rudhiraṃ vemuḥ |

The first sentence has its subject, अपरे, at the end of 2.57. Each clause: participle(s) + instrumental of means + finite verb.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Two more weapons, two more deaths: the mace crushes, the musala (club) makes the asuras spew blood. The verse moves into the present (शेरते ‘they lie’), as if the narrator were looking at the field.

Traditional interpretation

The mace (गदा) is among the weapons held by Mahālakṣmī in the Gita Press dhyāna for this carita (… गदेषुकुलिशं …); that the middle carita is presided over by Mahālakṣmī is the liturgical tradition of the pāṭha, not a statement of the Purāṇa.

Verse 2.59

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

केचिन्निपतिता भूमौ भिन्नाः शूलेन वक्षसि।निरन्तराः शरौघेण कृताः केचिद्रणाजिरे॥⁠५९⁠॥

kecinnipatitā bhūmau bhinnāḥ śūlena vakṣasi |nirantarāḥ śaraugheṇa kṛtāḥ kecidraṇājire ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

केचित् · निपतिताः · भूमौ · भिन्नाः · शूलेन · वक्षसिनिरन्तराः · शरौघेण · कृताः · केचित् · रणाजिरे

kecit · nipatitāḥ · bhūmau · bhinnāḥ · śūlena · vakṣasinirantarāḥ · śaraugheṇa · kṛtāḥ · kecit · raṇājire

Word by word

  1. केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun (किम् + चित्), m. nom. pl.; subject
  2. निपतिताःnipatitāḥfell, lay fallenpast participle (kta) of नि + √पत् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to fall’), m. nom. pl.; active sense, used as the finite verb
  3. भूमौbhūmauon the groundf. loc. sg. of भूमि (i-stem)
  4. भिन्नाःbhinnāḥpiercedpast passive participle (kta) of √भिद् (rudhādi, 7U ‘to split, pierce’), m. nom. pl.
  5. शूलेनśūlenaby the spear (śūla)m./n. instr. sg. of शूल; instrument
  6. वक्षसिvakṣasiin the chestn. loc. sg. of वक्षस् (s-stem)
  7. निरन्तराःnirantarāḥwithout a gap, riddled throughm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate with कृताः
  8. शरौघेणśaraugheṇaby a flood of arrowsm. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); agent/instrument
  9. कृताःkṛtāḥwere madepast passive participle (kta) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U), m. nom. pl.; used as the finite verb
  10. केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun, m. nom. pl.; subject
  11. रणाजिरेraṇājireon the field of battlen. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

Translation

Some fell on the ground, pierced in the chest by the spear. Some were made gapless by a flood of arrows on the battlefield.

In readable EnglishSome, pierced through the chest by her spear, fell to the ground; some, on the field of battle, were riddled through and through by her storm of arrows.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • केचिन्निपतिता kecinnipatitā → केचित् + निपतिताः

    consonant sandhi (nasal before nasal) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    Final t before the nasal n becomes the nasal n (optional; the text takes it). -āḥ then loses its visarga before the voiced bh.

  • केचिद्रणाजिरे kecidraṇājire → केचित् + रणाजिरे

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t → d before the voiced r.

  • शरौघेण śaraugheṇa → शर + ओघेण

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    a + o → au.

  • रणाजिरे raṇājire → रण + अजिरे

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • निरन्तराः nirantarāḥ

    bahuvrīhi (with the prefix निर् as first member)

    Members
    निर् + अन्तर
    Vigraha
    निर्गतम् अन्तरं येभ्यः ते nirgatam antaraṃ yebhyaḥ te
    Literally
    “those from whom (every) gap has gone”
    In the verse
    so densely covered with arrows that no space was left on their bodies

  • शरौघेण śaraugheṇa

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    शर + ओघ
    Vigraha
    शराणाम् ओघः, तेन śarāṇām oghaḥ, tena
    Literally
    “by a flood of arrows”
    In the verse
    by her storm of arrows (from the bow and quiver Vāyu gave her, 2.21)

  • रणाजिरे raṇājire

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    रण + अजिर
    Vigraha
    रणस्य अजिरम्, तस्मिन् raṇasya ajiram, tasmin
    Literally
    “in the courtyard of battle”
    In the verse
    on the battlefield
Morphology & anvaya

  • भिन्नाः bhinnāḥ

    kta of √भिद् with -न instead of -त (d + n → nn): भिन्न ‘split, pierced’.

  • निपतिताः nipatitāḥ

    kta of √पत् with the connecting vowel i (पतित). Intransitive, so active in sense: ‘fallen’.

Anvaya · prose order

केचित् वक्षसि शूलेन भिन्नाः भूमौ निपतिताः। केचित् रणाजिरे शरौघेण निरन्तराः कृताः।

kecit vakṣasi śūlena bhinnāḥ bhūmau nipatitāḥ | kecit raṇājire śaraugheṇa nirantarāḥ kṛtāḥ |

Two parallel sentences, each with केचित् and a participle for the verb; the second has a predicate adjective (निरन्तराः) with कृताः.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The catalogue continues: the śūla pierces the chest, the arrows leave no space unwounded. निरन्तर, ‘without interval’, pictures bodies covered with arrows from end to end.

Verse 2.60

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

श्येनानुकारिणः प्राणान् मुमुचुस्त्रिदशार्दनाः।केषांचिद् बाहवश्छिन्नाश्छिन्नग्रीवास्तथापरे॥⁠६०⁠॥

śyenānukāriṇaḥ prāṇān mumucustridaśārdanāḥ |keṣāṃcid bāhavaśchinnāśchinnagrīvāstathāpare ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

श्येनानुकारिणः · प्राणान् · मुमुचुः · त्रिदशार्दनाःकेषांचित् · बाहवः · छिन्नाः · छिन्नग्रीवाः · तथा · अपरे

śyenānukāriṇaḥ · prāṇān · mumucuḥ · tridaśārdanāḥkeṣāṃcit · bāhavaḥ · chinnāḥ · chinnagrīvāḥ · tathā · apare

Word by word

  1. श्येनानुकारिणःśyenānukāriṇaḥimitating hawks, like hawksm. nom. pl. (stem -अनुकारिन्); compound (see Samāsa); taken with त्रिदशार्दनाः (the same form is acc. pl., see Samāsa alt)
  2. प्राणान्prāṇāntheir life-breathsm. acc. pl. of प्राण (used in the plural for ‘life’); object of मुमुचुः
  3. मुमुचुःmumucuḥgave up, released√मुच् (tudādi, 6U), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  4. त्रिदशार्दनाःtridaśārdanāḥthe tormentors of the godsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of मुमुचुः
  5. केषांचित्keṣāṃcitof someindefinite pronoun (केषाम्, gen. pl. of किम्, + चित्), m. gen. pl.; one word; possessor of बाहवः
  6. बाहवःbāhavaḥarmsm. nom. pl. of बाहु (u-stem)
  7. छिन्नाःchinnāḥ(were) cut offpast passive participle (kta) of √छिद् (rudhādi, 7U ‘to cut’), m. nom. pl.; predicate of बाहवः
  8. छिन्नग्रीवाःchinnagrīvāḥwith severed necksm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of अपरे
  9. तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  10. अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; subject

Translation

The tormentors of the gods, imitating hawks, released their life-breaths. Of some the arms were cut off; likewise others had their necks cut.

In readable EnglishLike hawks dropping from the sky, the tormentors of the gods gave up their lives. Some had their arms hewn off, and others their heads struck from their necks.

‘Dropping from the sky’ interprets श्येनानुकारिणः ‘imitating hawks’, which the Sanskrit leaves unexplained; see Samāsa for the other construction.

Textual verification / uncertain: The point of comparison in श्येनानुकारिणः is not stated: it may describe the asuras (falling as hawks drop) or, as an accusative, their departing breaths (flying off like hawks). Both constructions are recorded; the choice is interpretive.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • मुमुचुस्त्रिदशार्दनाः mumucustridaśārdanāḥ → मुमुचुः + त्रिदशार्दनाः

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • केषांचिद् बाहवश्छिन्नाश्छिन्नग्रीवास्तथापरे keṣāṃcid bāhavaśchinnāśchinnagrīvāstathāpare → केषांचित् + बाहवः + छिन्नाः + छिन्नग्रीवाः + तथा + अपरे

    consonant, visarga and vowel sandhi · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    t → d before b; visarga → s → ś before ch (twice); visarga → s before t; ā + a → ā.

  • श्येनानुकारिणः śyenānukāriṇaḥ → श्येन + अनुकारिणः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā; likewise त्रिदश + अर्दन → त्रिदशार्दन.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • श्येनानुकारिणः śyenānukāriṇaḥ

    upapada tatpuruṣa (second member the agent noun अनुकारिन्, अनु + √कृ + णिनि)

    Members
    श्येन + अनुकारिन्
    Vigraha
    श्येनम् अनुकुर्वन्ति इति (श्येनानुकारिणः) śyenam anukurvanti iti (śyenānukāriṇaḥ)
    Literally
    “those who imitate the hawk”
    In the verse
    falling as a hawk drops — said of the dying asuras
    Alternative
    अनुकारिणः is also the accusative plural, so it can agree with प्राणान्: ‘they released their breaths, which (flew off) like hawks’. Both constructions are grammatical; this edition takes it as an epithet of the dying asuras and records the other.

  • त्रिदशार्दनाः tridaśārdanāḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (the second member the agent noun अर्दन ‘tormenting’, from √अर्द्)

    Members
    त्रिदश + अर्दन
    Vigraha
    त्रिदशान् अर्दयन्ति इति (त्रिदशानाम् अर्दनाः) tridaśān ardayanti iti (tridaśānām ardanāḥ)
    Literally
    “tormentors of the Thirty”
    In the verse
    the asuras, oppressors of the gods (त्रिदश ‘the Thirty’ is a standard name for the gods, from the round number of the thirty-three)

  • छिन्नग्रीवाः chinnagrīvāḥ

    bahuvrīhi (feminine ग्रीवा shortened to -ग्रीव at the end of a bahuvrīhi qualifying a masculine)

    Members
    छिन्न + ग्रीवा
    Vigraha
    छिन्ना ग्रीवा येषां ते chinnā grīvā yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “those whose necks are cut”
    In the verse
    beheaded
Morphology & anvaya

  • केषांचित् keṣāṃcit

    The indefinite pronoun is the interrogative किम् declined + the particle चित्: genitive plural केषाम् + चित् ‘of some’. The final m is written as anusvāra before c inside this one word.

  • मुमुचुः mumucuḥ

    Perfect 3rd plural of √मुच् (weak stem मुमुच्), beside मुमोच (3rd sg., 2.51) and मुमुचे (ātmanepada, 2.52).

Anvaya · prose order

श्येनानुकारिणः त्रिदशार्दनाः प्राणान् मुमुचुः। केषांचित् बाहवः छिन्नाः, तथा अपरे छिन्नग्रीवाः (अभवन्)।

śyenānukāriṇaḥ tridaśārdanāḥ prāṇān mumucuḥ | keṣāṃcit bāhavaḥ chinnāḥ, tathā apare chinnagrīvāḥ (abhavan) |

The first line is a complete sentence (subject at the end). The second gives two short clauses with the verb ‘to be’ understood.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The asuras are called त्रिदशार्दनाः, ‘tormentors of the Thirty’, at the moment they die: the epithet recalls why the battle is fought (2.6–8). The second line goes on cataloguing wounds — arms, necks.

Verse 2.61

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

शिरांसि पेतुरन्येषामन्ये मध्ये विदारिताः।विच्छिन्नजङ्घास्त्वपरे पेतुरुर्व्यां महासुराः॥⁠६१⁠॥

śirāṃsi peturanyeṣāmanye madhye vidāritāḥ |vicchinnajaṅghāstvapare petururvyāṃ mahāsurāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

शिरांसि · पेतुः · अन्येषाम् · अन्ये · मध्ये · विदारिताःविच्छिन्नजङ्घाः · तु · अपरे · पेतुः · उर्व्याम् · महासुराः

śirāṃsi · petuḥ · anyeṣām · anye · madhye · vidāritāḥvicchinnajaṅghāḥ · tu · apare · petuḥ · urvyām · mahāsurāḥ

Word by word

  1. शिरांसिśirāṃsiheadsn. nom. pl. of शिरस् (s-stem); subject of पेतुः
  2. पेतुःpetuḥfell√पत् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to fall’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  3. अन्येषाम्anyeṣāmof otherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. gen. pl.; possessor of शिरांसि
  4. अन्येanyeotherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. nom. pl.; subject
  5. मध्येmadhyein the middle, at the waistn. loc. sg. of मध्य
  6. विदारिताःvidāritāḥ(were) torn apartpast passive participle (kta) of the causative of वि + √दॄ (kryādi, 9P ‘to tear’; causative दारय-), m. nom. pl.; used as the finite verb
  7. विच्छिन्नजङ्घाःvicchinnajaṅghāḥwith their legs cut offm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अपरे
  8. तुtuand, butindeclinable (particle of contrast or continuation)
  9. अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; qualifies महासुराः
  10. पेतुःpetuḥfell√पत् (bhvādi, 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  11. उर्व्याम्urvyāmon the earthf. loc. sg. of उर्वी ‘the broad one’, the earth
  12. महासुराःmahāsurāḥgreat asurasm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of the second पेतुः

Translation

The heads of others fell; others were torn apart in the middle; and other great asuras, their legs cut off, fell on the earth.

In readable EnglishOthers’ heads fell; others were split through the middle; and still other great asuras, their legs hewn away, fell to the earth.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • पेतुरन्येषामन्ये peturanyeṣāmanye → पेतुः + अन्येषाम् + अन्ये

    visarga sandhi; final m before a vowel · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After u the visarga (रु) appears as r before a vowel; the final m of अन्येषाम् simply joins the following vowel.

  • विच्छिन्नजङ्घास्त्वपरे vicchinnajaṅghāstvapare → विच्छिन्नजङ्घाः + तु + अपरे

    visarga and vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    Visarga → s before t; u before a becomes v (तु अपरे → त्वपरे).

  • पेतुरुर्व्यां petururvyāṃ → पेतुः + उर्व्याम्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    As above, -uḥ before a vowel → -ur.

  • विच्छिन्न vicchinna → वि + छिन्न

    consonant sandhi inside the word (tuk before ch) · छे च (6.1.73)

    After the short i of the prefix, ch becomes cch.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • विच्छिन्नजङ्घाः vicchinnajaṅghāḥ

    bahuvrīhi (feminine जङ्घा shortened to -जङ्घ at the end of a bahuvrīhi qualifying a masculine)

    Members
    विच्छिन्न + जङ्घा
    Vigraha
    विच्छिन्नाः जङ्घाः येषां ते vicchinnāḥ jaṅghāḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “those whose shanks are severed”
    In the verse
    hamstrung, their legs cut from under them

  • महासुराः mahāsurāḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + असुर
    Vigraha
    महान्तः असुराः mahāntaḥ asurāḥ
    Literally
    “great asuras”
    In the verse
    the asura champions
Morphology & anvaya

  • पेतुः petuḥ

    Perfect 3rd plural of √पत् without reduplication: the root vowel becomes e (पेत्-), as regularly in roots of the shape C-a-C with an unchanged initial consonant (compare वेमुः, 2.58).

  • विदारिताः vidāritāḥ

    kta from the causative दारय- of √दॄ ‘to burst, tear’: ‘torn open’. मध्ये ‘in the middle’ says where: cut through at the waist.

Anvaya · prose order

अन्येषां शिरांसि पेतुः। अन्ये मध्ये विदारिताः। अपरे तु महासुराः विच्छिन्नजङ्घाः उर्व्यां पेतुः।

anyeṣāṃ śirāṃsi petuḥ | anye madhye vidāritāḥ | apare tu mahāsurāḥ vicchinnajaṅghāḥ urvyāṃ petuḥ |

Three clauses, each with its own subject: heads fell; others were split; still others fell hamstrung.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The anatomy of the slaughter continues — heads, waists, legs. The repetition of पेतुः ‘fell’ frames the verse.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the unflinching detail of these verses is not cruelty for its own sake. The text wants the defeat of what oppresses the gods to be total and visible, limb by limb.

Verse 2.62

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

एकबाह्वक्षिचरणाः केचिद्देव्या द्विधा कृताः।छिन्नेऽपि चान्ये शिरसि पतिताः पुनरुत्थिताः॥⁠६२⁠॥

ekabāhvakṣicaraṇāḥ keciddevyā dvidhā kṛtāḥ |chinne'pi cānye śirasi patitāḥ punarutthitāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

एकबाह्वक्षिचरणाः · केचित् · देव्या · द्विधा · कृताःछिन्ने · अपि · च · अन्ये · शिरसि · पतिताः · पुनः · उत्थिताः

ekabāhvakṣicaraṇāḥ · kecit · devyā · dvidhā · kṛtāḥchinne · api · ca · anye · śirasi · patitāḥ · punaḥ · utthitāḥ

Word by word

  1. एकबाह्वक्षिचरणाःekabāhvakṣicaraṇāḥwith one arm, one eye, one footm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies केचित्
  2. केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun (किम् + चित्), m. nom. pl.; subject
  3. देव्याdevyāby the Devīf. instr. sg. of देवी; agent with the passive कृताः
  4. द्विधाdvidhāin twoindeclinable (द्वि + taddhita -धा)
  5. कृताःkṛtāḥwere made, were cutpast passive participle (kta) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U), m. nom. pl.; used as the finite verb
  6. छिन्नेchinnecut offpast passive participle (kta) of √छिद् (rudhādi, 7U), n. loc. sg.; with शिरसि in a locative absolute
  7. अपिapieven, thoughindeclinable (concessive particle)
  8. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  9. अन्येanyeotherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. nom. pl.; subject
  10. शिरसिśirasithe headn. loc. sg. of शिरस्; locative absolute with छिन्ने
  11. पतिताःpatitāḥhaving fallenpast participle (kta) of √पत् (bhvādi, 1P), m. nom. pl.; active sense
  12. पुनःpunaḥagainindeclinable (adverb)
  13. उत्थिताःutthitāḥrose uppast participle (kta) of उद् + √स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’), m. nom. pl.; active sense, used as the finite verb

Translation

Some, cut in two by the Devī, (were left) with one arm, one eye, one foot. And others, even when the head was cut off, having fallen rose up again.

In readable EnglishSome the Goddess cleft in two, leaving each half one arm, one eye, one foot. And others, though their heads were struck off, fell and rose again —

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • केचिद्देव्या keciddevyā → केचित् + देव्या

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t → d before the voiced d.

  • छिन्नेऽपि चान्ये chinne'pi cānye → छिन्ने + अपि + च + अन्ये

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa; savarṇa-dīrgha) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    -e + a- → -e’- (avagraha); a + a → ā.

  • पुनरुत्थिताः punarutthitāḥ → पुनः + उत्थिताः

    visarga sandhi (r-visarga) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    The visarga of पुनः stands for r and reappears as r before a vowel.

  • बाह्वक्षि bāhvakṣi → बाहु + अक्षि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    u before the dissimilar a becomes v.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • एकबाह्वक्षिचरणाः ekabāhvakṣicaraṇāḥ

    bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (बाह्वक्षिचरण)

    Members
    एक + बाह्वक्षिचरण
    Vigraha
    एकः बाहुः एकम् अक्षि एकः चरणः च येषां ते; बाह्वक्षिचरणाः = बाहुः च अक्षि च चरणः च ekaḥ bāhuḥ ekam akṣi ekaḥ caraṇaḥ ca yeṣāṃ te; bāhvakṣicaraṇāḥ = bāhuḥ ca akṣi ca caraṇaḥ ca
    Literally
    “those whose arm, eye and foot are (each) one”
    In the verse
    split lengthwise, so that each half has a single arm, eye and foot
Morphology & anvaya

  • छिन्ने chinne

    Locative absolute (sati saptamī): छिन्ने शिरसि ‘the head having been cut off’, with अपि ‘even when …’. The participle agrees with शिरसि in neuter locative singular.

  • उत्थिताः utthitāḥ

    kta of उद् + √स्था: स्था weakens to स्थि (स्थित), and the s is lost after the prefix उद् (उद् + स्थित → उत्थित).

Anvaya · prose order

केचित् देव्या द्विधा कृताः एकबाह्वक्षिचरणाः (अभवन्)। अन्ये च शिरसि छिन्ने अपि पतिताः पुनः उत्थिताः।

kecit devyā dvidhā kṛtāḥ ekabāhvakṣicaraṇāḥ (abhavan) | anye ca śirasi chinne api patitāḥ punaḥ utthitāḥ |

The first sentence explains the compound: cut in two, they were left with one arm, eye and foot. The second sentence continues into 2.63, where the risen trunks fight.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The battle turns grotesque: asuras split lengthwise, and headless bodies that fall and stand up again. The next two verses describe these headless trunks (कबन्ध) fighting on.

Traditional interpretation

Headless trunks (कबन्ध) rising and fighting on are a familiar image in epic battle descriptions. Here it marks the asuras’ ferocity: they resist even past death, and it is still not enough.

Verse 2.63

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

कबन्धा युयुधुर्देव्या गृहीतपरमायुधाः।ननृतुश्चापरे तत्र युद्धे तूर्यलयाश्रिताः॥⁠६३⁠॥

kabandhā yuyudhurdevyā gṛhītaparamāyudhāḥ |nanṛtuścāpare tatra yuddhe tūryalayāśritāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

कबन्धाः · युयुधुः · देव्या · गृहीतपरमायुधाःननृतुः · च · अपरे · तत्र · युद्धे · तूर्यलयाश्रिताः

kabandhāḥ · yuyudhuḥ · devyā · gṛhītaparamāyudhāḥnanṛtuḥ · ca · apare · tatra · yuddhe · tūryalayāśritāḥ

Word by word

  1. कबन्धाःkabandhāḥ(as) headless trunksm. nom. pl. of कबन्ध ‘a headless body’; in apposition to अन्ये (2.62), subject of युयुधुः
  2. युयुधुःyuyudhuḥfought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada ending (as in 2.48)
  3. देव्याdevyāwith the Devīf. instr. sg. of देवी; instrumental of the opponent with a verb of fighting
  4. गृहीतपरमायुधाःgṛhītaparamāyudhāḥgrasping excellent weaponsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies कबन्धाः
  5. ननृतुःnanṛtuḥdanced√नृत् (divādi, 4P ‘to dance’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  6. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  7. अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; subject of ननृतुः
  8. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place, तद् + त्रल्)
  9. युद्धेyuddhein the battlen. loc. sg. of युद्ध
  10. तूर्यलयाश्रिताःtūryalayāśritāḥkeeping to the rhythm of the instrumentsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अपरे

Translation

As headless trunks, grasping excellent weapons, they fought with the Devī; and others danced there in the battle, keeping to the rhythm of the instruments.

In readable Englishand fought the Goddess as headless trunks, still gripping their finest weapons; and others danced there on the battlefield to the beat of the war-music.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • कबन्धा युयुधुर्देव्या kabandhā yuyudhurdevyā → कबन्धाः + युयुधुः + देव्या

    visarga sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    -āḥ loses its visarga before the voiced y; -uḥ becomes -ur before the voiced d.

  • ननृतुश्चापरे nanṛtuścāpare → ननृतुः + च + अपरे

    visarga and vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Visarga before c → ś; a + a → ā.

  • परमायुधाः paramāyudhāḥ → परम + आयुधाः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā; likewise लय + आश्रित → लयाश्रित.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • गृहीतपरमायुधाः gṛhītaparamāyudhāḥ

    bahuvrīhi containing a karmadhāraya (परमायुध)

    Members
    गृहीत + परमायुध
    Vigraha
    गृहीतानि परमायुधानि यैः ते; परमायुधम् = परमम् आयुधम् gṛhītāni paramāyudhāni yaiḥ te; paramāyudham = paramam āyudham
    Literally
    “those by whom excellent weapons are grasped”
    In the verse
    still gripping their finest weapons

  • तूर्यलयाश्रिताः tūryalayāśritāḥ

    dvitīyā tatpuruṣa (with the participle आश्रित ‘resorting to’) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (तूर्यलय)

    Members
    तूर्यलय + आश्रित
    Vigraha
    तूर्यलयम् आश्रिताः; तूर्यलयः = तूर्याणां लयः tūryalayam āśritāḥ; tūryalayaḥ = tūryāṇāṃ layaḥ
    Literally
    “resorting to the rhythm of the instruments”
    In the verse
    dancing in time to the war-music of 2.54–55
Morphology & anvaya

  • ननृतुः nanṛtuḥ

    Perfect of √नृत् with reduplication न- (ṛ is reduplicated as a): न-नृत्-उः.

  • गृहीतपरमायुधाः gṛhītaparamāyudhāḥ

    In a bahuvrīhi the participle stands first (गृहीत-, kta of √ग्रह् with i and samprasāraṇa: ग्रह् → गृह् + ई + त).

Anvaya · prose order

(ते) कबन्धाः गृहीतपरमायुधाः देव्या (सह) युयुधुः। अपरे च तत्र युद्धे तूर्यलयाश्रिताः ननृतुः।

(te) kabandhāḥ gṛhītaparamāyudhāḥ devyā (saha) yuyudhuḥ | apare ca tatra yuddhe tūryalayāśritāḥ nanṛtuḥ |

The first sentence continues 2.62b (अन्ये … पुनः उत्थिताः): the risen bodies, now कबन्धाः, fight. The second is parallel: others dance.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The headless bodies of 2.62 fight on, and some even dance to the rhythm of the drums and conches the gaṇas are playing (2.54–55). The festival of battle (युद्धमहोत्सव) has its macabre dancers.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: what has lost its head can still move to the music of battle. Resistance can go on long after its reason is gone.

Verse 2.64

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

कबन्धाश्छिन्नशिरसः खड्गशक्त्यृष्टिपाणयः।तिष्ठ तिष्ठेति भाषन्तो देवीमन्ये महासुराः॥⁠६४⁠॥

kabandhāśchinnaśirasaḥ khaḍgaśaktyṛṣṭipāṇayaḥ |tiṣṭha tiṣṭheti bhāṣanto devīmanye mahāsurāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

कबन्धाः · छिन्नशिरसः · खड्गशक्त्यृष्टिपाणयःतिष्ठ · तिष्ठ · इति · भाषन्तः · देवीम् · अन्ये · महासुराः

kabandhāḥ · chinnaśirasaḥ · khaḍgaśaktyṛṣṭipāṇayaḥtiṣṭha · tiṣṭha · iti · bhāṣantaḥ · devīm · anye · mahāsurāḥ

Word by word

  1. कबन्धाःkabandhāḥheadless trunksm. nom. pl. of कबन्ध; in apposition to अन्ये महासुराः
  2. छिन्नशिरसःchinnaśirasaḥwith severed headsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies कबन्धाः
  3. खड्गशक्त्यृष्टिपाणयःkhaḍgaśaktyṛṣṭipāṇayaḥwith sword, spear and ṛṣṭi in handm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies कबन्धाः
  4. तिष्ठtiṣṭhastop!, stand!√स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’; present stem तिष्ठ-), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  5. तिष्ठtiṣṭhastop!√स्था, loṭ, madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; repeated for emphasis
  6. इतिiti(saying) thus — closes the quotationindeclinable (quotative particle)
  7. भाषन्तःbhāṣantaḥsaying, cryingpresent participle (śatṛ) of √भाष् (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to speak’), m. nom. pl.; with a parasmaipada suffix (see Morphology)
  8. देवीम्devīmto the Devīf. acc. sg. of देवी; addressee of भाषन्तः (verbs of speaking take the person addressed in the accusative)
  9. अन्येanyeotherpronominal adjective अन्य, m. nom. pl.; qualifies महासुराः
  10. महासुराःmahāsurāḥgreat asurasm. nom. pl.; compound (karmadhāraya, as in 2.61); subject

Translation

Other great asuras — headless trunks, their heads severed, swords, spears and ṛṣṭis in their hands — (were) saying to the Devī, ‘Stop! Stop!’

In readable EnglishOther great asuras, headless trunks with their heads struck off, swords and spears and lances still in their hands, kept crying to the Goddess, ‘Stand! Stand and fight!’

‘Kept crying’ supplies the missing finite verb. The headless shout although they have no heads — the text does not explain it, and the translation does not either.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • कबन्धाश्छिन्नशिरसः kabandhāśchinnaśirasaḥ → कबन्धाः + छिन्नशिरसः

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before ch → s → ś.

  • तिष्ठेति tiṣṭheti → तिष्ठ + इति

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + i → e.

  • भाषन्तो देवीमन्ये bhāṣanto devīmanye → भाषन्तः + देवीम् + अन्ये

    visarga sandhi; final m before a vowel · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced d → -o; the final m of देवीम् joins the following vowel.

  • शक्त्यृष्टि śaktyṛṣṭi → शक्ति + ऋष्टि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    i before the dissimilar ṛ becomes y.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • छिन्नशिरसः chinnaśirasaḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    छिन्न + शिरस्
    Vigraha
    छिन्नं शिरः येषां ते chinnaṃ śiraḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “those whose head is cut off”
    In the verse
    beheaded — restating कबन्धाः

  • खड्गशक्त्यृष्टिपाणयः khaḍgaśaktyṛṣṭipāṇayaḥ

    bahuvrīhi (vyadhikaraṇa: the members stand in different cases in the vigraha) containing an itaretara dvandva

    Members
    खड्गशक्त्यृष्टि + पाणि
    Vigraha
    खड्गशक्त्यृष्टयः पाणिषु येषां ते; खड्गशक्त्यृष्टयः = खड्गः च शक्तिः च ऋष्टिः च khaḍgaśaktyṛṣṭayaḥ pāṇiṣu yeṣāṃ te; khaḍgaśaktyṛṣṭayaḥ = khaḍgaḥ ca śaktiḥ ca ṛṣṭiḥ ca
    Literally
    “those in whose hands are swords, spears and ṛṣṭis”
    In the verse
    fully armed though headless (ṛṣṭi: a double-edged sword or lance)
Morphology & anvaya

  • भाषन्तः bhāṣantaḥ

    √भाष् is ātmanepadin, and its classical participle is भाषमाणाः (śānac). The form with the parasmaipada -अत् (śatṛ) is an epic and Purāṇic usage, like युयुधुः (2.48).

  • तिष्ठ tiṣṭha

    Imperative 2nd sg. of √स्था, whose present stem is the reduplicated तिष्ठ-. Doubled, it is the battlefield challenge ‘Stop! Stop!’ — ‘stand and fight’.

Anvaya · prose order

अन्ये महासुराः छिन्नशिरसः कबन्धाः खड्गशक्त्यृष्टिपाणयः (सन्तः) देवीं ‘तिष्ठ तिष्ठ’ इति भाषन्तः (अभवन्)।

anye mahāsurāḥ chinnaśirasaḥ kabandhāḥ khaḍgaśaktyṛṣṭipāṇayaḥ (santaḥ) devīṃ ‘tiṣṭha tiṣṭha’ iti bhāṣantaḥ (abhavan) |

The verse has no finite verb: the participle भाषन्तः serves as predicate (‘were crying’), or युयुधुः is carried over from 2.63.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The grotesque reaches its climax: headless asuras, still armed, challenge the Devī with the warrior’s cry तिष्ठ तिष्ठ. The image shows a defiance that outlives the body.

Traditional interpretation

The challenge ‘Stop! Stop!’ is the formula of epic combat, addressed to an opponent who is withdrawing or must stand and fight. Here it is spoken by the dead to the one who cannot be stopped.

Verse 2.65

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

पातितै रथनागाश्वैरसुरैश्च वसुन्धरा।अगम्या साभवत्तत्र यत्राभूत्स महारणः॥⁠६५⁠॥

pātitai rathanāgāśvairasuraiśca vasundharā |agamyā sābhavattatra yatrābhūtsa mahāraṇaḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

पातितैः · रथनागाश्वैः · असुरैः · च · वसुन्धराअगम्या · सा · अभवत् · तत्र · यत्र · अभूत् · सः · महारणः

pātitaiḥ · rathanāgāśvaiḥ · asuraiḥ · ca · vasundharāagamyā · sā · abhavat · tatra · yatra · abhūt · saḥ · mahāraṇaḥ

Word by word

  1. पातितैःpātitaiḥfelledpast passive participle (kta) of the causative of √पत् (पातय- ‘to fell’), m. instr. pl.; qualifies रथनागाश्वैः and असुरैः
  2. रथनागाश्वैःrathanāgāśvaiḥby chariots, elephants and horsesm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of cause
  3. असुरैःasuraiḥby asurasm. instr. pl. of असुर; instrumental of cause
  4. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  5. वसुन्धराvasundharāthe earthf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of अभवत्
  6. अगम्याagamyāimpassablef. nom. sg. of अगम्य (nañ + गम्य, the gerundive of √गम् with यत्); predicate with अभवत्
  7. साsāthat (earth)pronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; resumes वसुन्धरा
  8. अभवत्abhavatbecame√भू (bhvādi, 1P), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place); correlative of यत्र
  10. यत्रyatrawhereindeclinable (relative adverb of place)
  11. अभूत्abhūttook place, was√भू, luṅ (aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. सःsaḥthatpronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies महारणः
  13. महारणःmahāraṇaḥgreat battlem. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of अभूत्

Translation

By felled chariots, elephants and horses, and by asuras, the earth — it became impassable there, where that great battle took place.

In readable EnglishWhere that great battle was fought, the earth became impassable, strewn with fallen chariots, elephants, horses and asuras.

‘Strewn with’ renders the instrumental of cause (‘by …’).

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • पातितै रथ pātitai ratha → पातितैः + रथनागाश्वैः

    visarga sandhi before r · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), रो रि (8.3.14)

    After ai the visarga becomes r, and r before r is dropped; ai, already long, cannot lengthen further, so पातितै stands alone.

  • रथनागाश्वैरसुरैश्च rathanāgāśvairasuraiśca → रथनागाश्वैः + असुरैः + च

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    After ai the visarga appears as r before a vowel; before c it becomes ś.

  • साभवत्तत्र यत्राभूत्स महारणः sābhavattatra yatrābhūtsa mahāraṇaḥ → सा + अभवत् + तत्र + यत्र + अभूत् + सः + महारणः

    vowel, consonant and visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); खरि च (8.4.55); एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)

    ā + a → ā (twice); t stays t before t and s; सः loses its visarga before m.

  • नागाश्वै nāgāśvai → नाग + अश्व

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • रथनागाश्वैः rathanāgāśvaiḥ

    itaretara dvandva

    Members
    रथ + नाग + अश्व
    Vigraha
    रथाः च नागाः च अश्वाः च, तैः rathāḥ ca nāgāḥ ca aśvāḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally
    “by chariots and elephants and horses”
    In the verse
    the three wings of the fourfold army (the fourth is the asuras on foot, named next)

  • वसुन्धरा vasundharā

    upapada tatpuruṣa (with the inserted m: वसुम्-धरा)

    Members
    वसु + धरा
    Vigraha
    वसूनि धारयति इति vasūni dhārayati iti
    Literally
    “she who holds wealth”
    In the verse
    the earth

  • महारणः mahāraṇaḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + रण
    Vigraha
    महान् रणः mahān raṇaḥ
    Literally
    “a great battle”
    In the verse
    the great battle
Morphology & anvaya

  • अगम्या agamyā

    Gerundive (kṛtya) of √गम् with यत् (गम्य ‘to be gone over’), negated with अ-: ‘not to be traversed’, impassable.

  • अभवत् abhavat

    Imperfect of √भू, beside the aorist अभूत् in the same line: the two pasts are used side by side in narrative.

Anvaya · prose order

यत्र सः महारणः अभूत्, तत्र सा वसुन्धरा पातितैः रथनागाश्वैः असुरैः च अगम्या अभवत्।

yatra saḥ mahāraṇaḥ abhūt, tatra sā vasundharā pātitaiḥ rathanāgāśvaiḥ asuraiḥ ca agamyā abhavat |

Main clause (वसुन्धरा … अगम्या सा अभवत् तत्र) with a relative clause of place (यत्र … महारणः). The instrumentals give the cause.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The camera pulls back from single deaths to the whole field: so many chariots, elephants, horses and asuras lie fallen that the ground cannot be crossed.

Traditional interpretation

GRETIL’s Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa carries further verse material near this point that the Gita Press text does not (corrections.json, recension notes). It belongs to the Purāṇa recension, not to the recited 700, and is not added here.

Verse 2.66

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

शोणितौघा महानद्यः सद्यस्तत्र प्रसुस्रुवुः।मध्ये चासुरसैन्यस्य वारणासुरवाजिनाम्॥⁠६६⁠॥

śoṇitaughā mahānadyaḥ sadyastatra prasusruvuḥ |madhye cāsurasainyasya vāraṇāsuravājinām ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

शोणितौघाः · महानद्यः · सद्यः · तत्र · प्रसुस्रुवुःमध्ये · च · असुरसैन्यस्य · वारणासुरवाजिनाम्

śoṇitaughāḥ · mahānadyaḥ · sadyaḥ · tatra · prasusruvuḥmadhye · ca · asurasainyasya · vāraṇāsuravājinām

Word by word

  1. शोणितौघाःśoṇitaughāḥtorrents of bloodm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of प्रसुस्रुवुः
  2. महानद्यःmahānadyaḥ(as) great riversf. nom. pl. of महानदी; compound (see Samāsa); predicate in apposition
  3. सद्यःsadyaḥat onceindeclinable (adverb of time)
  4. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
  5. प्रसुस्रुवुःprasusruvuḥflowed forthप्र + √स्रु (bhvādi, 1P ‘to flow’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  6. मध्येmadhyein the midstn. loc. sg. of मध्य; governs the genitives
  7. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  8. असुरसैन्यस्यasurasainyasyaof the asura armyn. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); with मध्ये
  9. वारणासुरवाजिनाम्vāraṇāsuravājināmof elephants, asuras and horsesm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); with मध्ये, specifying असुरसैन्यस्य

Translation

Torrents of blood, great rivers, at once flowed forth there, in the midst of the asura army, of elephants, asuras and horses.

In readable EnglishAt once torrents of blood flowed there like great rivers, through the midst of the asura host, among its elephants, warriors and horses.

‘Like’ makes explicit the identification of the torrents with great rivers, which the Sanskrit states by apposition.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • शोणितौघा महानद्यः śoṇitaughā mahānadyaḥ → शोणितौघाः + महानद्यः

    visarga sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)

    -āḥ before the voiced m loses its visarga.

  • सद्यस्तत्र sadyastatra → सद्यः + तत्र

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • चासुरसैन्यस्य cāsurasainyasya → च + असुरसैन्यस्य

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • शोणितौघा śoṇitaughā → शोणित + ओघाः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    a + o → au.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शोणितौघाः śoṇitaughāḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    शोणित + ओघ
    Vigraha
    शोणितस्य ओघाः śoṇitasya oghāḥ
    Literally
    “floods of blood”
    In the verse
    streams of blood that run like rivers
    Alternative
    Also a bahuvrīhi qualifying महानद्यः, शोणितम् ओघः यासां ताः ‘great rivers whose flood is blood’ (feminine nominative plural, same spelling). The tatpuruṣa in apposition is the simpler construction; the sense is the same.

  • महानद्यः mahānadyaḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + नदी
    Vigraha
    महत्यः नद्यः mahatyaḥ nadyaḥ
    Literally
    “great rivers”
    In the verse
    rivers in flood

  • असुरसैन्यस्य asurasainyasya

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    असुर + सैन्य
    Vigraha
    असुराणां सैन्यम्, तस्य asurāṇāṃ sainyam, tasya
    Literally
    “of the army of the asuras”
    In the verse
    of the enemy host

  • वारणासुरवाजिनाम् vāraṇāsuravājinām

    itaretara dvandva

    Members
    वारण + असुर + वाजिन्
    Vigraha
    वारणाः च असुराः च वाजिनः च, तेषाम् vāraṇāḥ ca asurāḥ ca vājinaḥ ca, teṣām
    Literally
    “of elephants and asuras and horses”
    In the verse
    of the beasts and warriors of that army
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्रसुस्रुवुः prasusruvuḥ

    Perfect of √स्रु with reduplication सु- (the reduplicated syllable keeps only the first consonant and the vowel); before the vowel ending -उः the root’s u becomes uv: सु-स्रुव्-उः.

Anvaya · prose order

तत्र असुरसैन्यस्य वारणासुरवाजिनां च मध्ये शोणितौघाः महानद्यः (इव) सद्यः प्रसुस्रुवुः।

tatra asurasainyasya vāraṇāsuravājināṃ ca madhye śoṇitaughāḥ mahānadyaḥ (iva) sadyaḥ prasusruvuḥ |

One sentence: subject (शोणितौघाः) with a predicate noun (महानद्यः), place (तत्र … मध्ये with genitives), verb (प्रसुस्रुवुः).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The scale of the slaughter is measured in rivers of blood running through the asura army itself — among its own elephants, warriors and horses.

Verse 2.67

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

क्षणेन तन्महासैन्यमसुराणां तथाम्बिका।निन्ये क्षयं यथा वह्निस्तृणदारुमहाचयम्॥⁠६७⁠॥

kṣaṇena tanmahāsainyamasurāṇāṃ tathāmbikā |ninye kṣayaṃ yathā vahnistṛṇadārumahācayam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

क्षणेन · तत् · महासैन्यम् · असुराणाम् · तथा · अम्बिकानिन्ये · क्षयम् · यथा · वह्निः · तृणदारुमहाचयम्

kṣaṇena · tat · mahāsainyam · asurāṇām · tathā · ambikāninye · kṣayam · yathā · vahniḥ · tṛṇadārumahācayam

Word by word

  1. क्षणेनkṣaṇenain a momentm. instr. sg. of क्षण; instrumental of time within which
  2. तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. acc. sg.; qualifies महासैन्यम्
  3. महासैन्यम्mahāsainyamgreat armyn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of निन्ये
  4. असुराणाम्asurāṇāmof the asurasm. gen. pl. of असुर; depends on महासैन्यम्
  5. तथाtathāso, in the same wayindeclinable (correlative adverb of manner, answering यथा)
  6. अम्बिकाambikāAmbikāf. nom. sg. of अम्बिका; subject of निन्ये
  7. निन्येninyeled, brought√नी (bhvādi, 1U ‘to lead’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  8. क्षयम्kṣayamto destructionm. acc. sg. of क्षय; the second object of निन्ये (‘lead X to Y’)
  9. यथाyathāasindeclinable (relative adverb of manner)
  10. वह्निःvahniḥfirem. nom. sg. of वह्नि (i-stem); subject of the simile (निनयति understood)
  11. तृणदारुमहाचयम्tṛṇadārumahācayama great heap of grass and woodm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object in the simile

Translation

In a moment Ambikā so led that great army of the asuras to destruction, as fire (does) a great heap of grass and wood.

In readable EnglishIn a moment Ambikā brought that vast army of the asuras to ruin, as fire consumes a great heap of straw and timber.

‘Consumes’ renders the verb supplied in the simile (‘leads to destruction’).

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तन्महासैन्यमसुराणां tanmahāsainyamasurāṇāṃ → तत् + महासैन्यम् + असुराणाम्

    consonant sandhi (nasal before nasal); final m before a vowel · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    Final t before the nasal m becomes the nasal n; the final m of महासैन्यम् joins the next vowel; असुराणाम् ends in anusvāra before t (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • तथाम्बिका tathāmbikā → तथा + अम्बिका

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā.

  • वह्निस्तृण vahnistṛṇa → वह्निः + तृणदारुमहाचयम्

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महासैन्यम् mahāsainyam

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + सैन्य
    Vigraha
    महत् सैन्यम् mahat sainyam
    Literally
    “a great army”
    In the verse
    the whole vast host mustered in 2.40–47

  • तृणदारुमहाचयम् tṛṇadārumahācayam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a dvandva (तृणदारु) and a karmadhāraya (महाचय)

    Members
    तृणदारु + महाचय
    Vigraha
    तृणदारूणां महाचयः, तम्; तृणदारु = तृणानि च दारूणि च; महाचयः = महान् चयः tṛṇadārūṇāṃ mahācayaḥ, tam; tṛṇadāru = tṛṇāni ca dārūṇi ca; mahācayaḥ = mahān cayaḥ
    Literally
    “a great pile of grass and wood”
    In the verse
    dry fuel that fire consumes at once
    Alternative
    The dvandva तृणदारु may be taken as samāhāra (a collective, ‘grass-and-wood’) or itaretara (‘grasses and woods’); as the first member of a compound the two cannot be told apart, and the sense is the same.
Morphology & anvaya

  • निन्ये ninye

    Perfect ātmanepada of √नी: reduplication नि-, and the root ī becomes y before the vowel ending (नि-न्य्-ए). √नी takes two accusatives: the thing led (महासैन्यम्) and the goal (क्षयम्); क्षयं नी ‘to lead to destruction’ means ‘to destroy’.

Anvaya · prose order

यथा वह्निः तृणदारुमहाचयं (क्षयं नयति), तथा अम्बिका असुराणां तत् महासैन्यं क्षणेन क्षयं निन्ये।

yathā vahniḥ tṛṇadārumahācayaṃ (kṣayaṃ nayati), tathā ambikā asurāṇāṃ tat mahāsainyaṃ kṣaṇena kṣayaṃ ninye |

A correlative simile: यथा (the fire) … तथा (Ambikā). The verb of the simile is supplied from निन्ये.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The summary verse of the battle: the whole army is gone in a moment (क्षणेन), as easily as fire takes dry fuel. The simile returns to the fire-image used of the lion in 2.52.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the asuras’ vast numbers — tens of thousands of chariots, crores of elephants — are only fuel. Size is no defence against what the text presents as the power of the Goddess.

Verse 2.68

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

स च सिंहो महानादमुत्सृजन्धुतकेसरः।शरीरेभ्योऽमरारीणामसूनिव विचिन्वति॥⁠६८⁠॥

sa ca siṃho mahānādamutsṛjandhutakesaraḥ |śarīrebhyo'marārīṇāmasūniva vicinvati ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सः · च · सिंहः · महानादम् · उत्सृजन् · धुतकेसरःशरीरेभ्यः · अमरारीणाम् · असून् · इव · विचिन्वति

saḥ · ca · siṃhaḥ · mahānādam · utsṛjan · dhutakesaraḥśarīrebhyaḥ · amarārīṇām · asūn · iva · vicinvati

Word by word

  1. सःsaḥthatpronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies सिंहः
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  3. सिंहःsiṃhaḥlionm. nom. sg. of सिंह; subject of विचिन्वति
  4. महानादम्mahānādama great roarm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of उत्सृजन्
  5. उत्सृजन्utsṛjanletting outpresent participle (śatṛ) of उद् + √सृज् (tudādi, 6P ‘to let go, emit’), m. nom. sg.; qualifies सिंहः
  6. धुतकेसरःdhutakesaraḥhis mane shakingm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सिंहः
  7. शरीरेभ्यःśarīrebhyaḥfrom the bodiesn. abl. pl. of शरीर; source with विचिन्वति
  8. अमरारीणाम्amarārīṇāmof the enemies of the immortalsm. gen. pl. (stem -अरि, i-stem); compound (see Samāsa); depends on शरीरेभ्यः
  9. असून्asūnthe life-breathsm. acc. pl. of असु ‘vital breath’ (used in the plural for ‘life’); object of विचिन्वति
  10. इवivaas if, as it wereindeclinable (particle of comparison, here of seeming)
  11. विचिन्वतिvicinvatisearches out, picks outवि + √चि (svādi, 5U ‘to gather, pick’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada, from a thematic stem चिन्व- (see Morphology)

Translation

And that lion, letting out a great roar, his mane shaking, as it were picks out the life-breaths from the bodies of the enemies of the immortals.

In readable EnglishAnd the lion, shaking his mane and roaring mightily, ranged as if he were plucking the very lives out of the bodies of the gods’ enemies.

The readable version puts the historic present into the past and adds ‘ranged’ to carry the image; the Sanskrit has only ‘as it were picks out’.

Textual verification / uncertain: विचिन्वति is taken as a finite verb (3rd sg. present, thematic ārṣa form). Grammatically it could also be the locative singular of the participle विचिन्वत्, but nothing in the verse agrees with a locative, so that reading does not construe.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • स च सिंहो महानादमुत्सृजन्धुतकेसरः sa ca siṃho mahānādamutsṛjandhutakesaraḥ → सः + च + सिंहः + महानादम् + उत्सृजन् + धुतकेसरः

    visarga sandhi; final m and n · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    सः loses its visarga before c; सिंहः becomes सिंहो before the voiced m; the final m of महानादम् joins the vowel; the final n of उत्सृजन् stands unchanged before dh and is only written together with it.

  • शरीरेभ्योऽमरारीणामसूनिव śarīrebhyo'marārīṇāmasūniva → शरीरेभ्यः + अमरारीणाम् + असून् + इव

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa; final consonants before vowels · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a- becomes -o and the a is elided (avagraha); final m and n (after the long ū, so not doubled) join the following vowels. Inside the compound, अमर + अरि → अमरारि by अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महानादम् mahānādam

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + नाद
    Vigraha
    महान्तं नादम् mahāntaṃ nādam
    Literally
    “a great roar”
    In the verse
    a mighty roar

  • धुतकेसरः dhutakesaraḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    धुत + केसर
    Vigraha
    धुताः केसराः यस्य सः dhutāḥ kesarāḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose mane-hairs are shaken”
    In the verse
    tossing his mane (compare धुतसटः, 2.51)

  • अमरारीणाम् amarārīṇām

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अमर + अरि
    Vigraha
    अमराणाम् अरयः, तेषाम् amarāṇām arayaḥ, teṣām
    Literally
    “of the enemies of the immortals”
    In the verse
    of the asuras
Morphology & anvaya

  • विचिन्वति vicinvati

    The classical present of √चि is चिनोति (svādi, stem चिनु-/चिनो-); विचिन्वति is formed as if from a thematic stem चिन्व- (abstracted from forms like चिन्वन्ति ‘they gather’). Such thematized forms are an epic and Purāṇic (ārṣa) usage. The present tense is the historic present.

  • उत्सृजन् utsṛjan

    Present participle of उद् + √सृज्: the tudādi stem सृज- + -अत्, nom. sg. m. -अन्; the d of the prefix becomes t before s (खरि च, 8.4.55).

Anvaya · prose order

सः च सिंहः धुतकेसरः महानादम् उत्सृजन् अमरारीणां शरीरेभ्यः असून् विचिन्वति इव।

saḥ ca siṃhaḥ dhutakesaraḥ mahānādam utsṛjan amarārīṇāṃ śarīrebhyaḥ asūn vicinvati iva |

Subject with two qualifiers (one a participial phrase), then the verb; इव makes the whole action an ‘as it were’.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The lion returns (2.51–52). He roars and tosses his mane, and moves among the fallen as if searching out any life still left in their bodies — the last stroke of the battle.

Traditional interpretation

The Devī’s lion is more than a mount in the Devī Māhātmya: it fights in its own right here (2.51–52, 2.68), against Mahiṣa’s generals (3.14), against Dhūmralocana’s army (6.15–19) and against Niśumbha’s forces (9.37). It shares in the work of the Goddess who rides it.

Verse 2.69

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

देव्या गणैश्च तैस्तत्र कृतं युद्धं महासुरैः।यथैषां तुतुषुर्देवाः पुष्पवृष्टिमुचो दिवि॥⁠६९⁠॥ॐ

devyā gaṇaiśca taistatra kṛtaṃ yuddhaṃ mahāsuraiḥ |yathaiṣāṃ tutuṣurdevāḥ puṣpavṛṣṭimuco divi ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

देव्या · गणैः · च · तैः · तत्र · कृतम् · युद्धम् · महासुरैःयथा · एषाम् · तुतुषुः · देवाः · पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः · दिवि

devyā · gaṇaiḥ · ca · taiḥ · tatra · kṛtam · yuddham · mahāsuraiḥyathā · eṣām · tutuṣuḥ · devāḥ · puṣpavṛṣṭimucaḥ · divi

Word by word

  1. देव्याdevyāby the Devīf. instr. sg. of देवी; agent of the passive कृतम्
  2. गणैःgaṇaiḥby the gaṇasm. instr. pl. of गण; agent of कृतम्
  3. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle); joins देव्या and गणैः
  4. तैःtaiḥthosepronoun तद्, m. instr. pl.; qualifies गणैः (the gaṇas born of her breath, 2.53)
  5. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
  6. कृतम्kṛtamwas made, was wagedpast passive participle (kta) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U), n. nom. sg.; agrees with युद्धम्; used as the finite verb
  7. युद्धम्yuddhambattlen. nom. sg. of युद्ध; subject of the passive कृतम्
  8. महासुरैःmahāsuraiḥwith the great asurasm. instr. pl.; compound (karmadhāraya, as in 2.61); instrumental of the opponent
  9. यथाyathāso that, in such a way thatindeclinable (relative adverb introducing a clause of result)
  10. एषाम्eṣāmwith them, at thempronoun इदम्, m. gen. pl.; genitive of reference (see anvaya)
  11. तुतुषुःtutuṣuḥwere pleased, rejoiced√तुष् (divādi, 4P ‘to be pleased’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  12. देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of तुतुषुः
  13. पुष्पवृष्टिमुचःpuṣpavṛṣṭimucaḥshowering rains of flowersm. nom. pl. (stem -मुच्, a root-noun); compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवाः
  14. दिविdiviin heavenf. loc. sg. of दिव् ‘sky, heaven’

Translation

There the battle was waged by the Devī and by those gaṇas with the great asuras in such a way that the gods, releasing rains of flowers in heaven, were pleased with them.

In readable EnglishSo the Goddess and her hosts fought the great asuras there, and fought so well that the gods in heaven rejoiced in them and rained down flowers.

‘Fought so well’ makes explicit the result-clause यथा (‘in such a way that’).

Textual verification / uncertain: Variant reading (Textual Verification Required): महासुरैः (Gita Press, kept) against तथासुरैः (GRETIL, Freund). With तथासुरैः the line reads ‘battle was waged by the Devī and her gaṇas, and likewise by the asuras’ or ‘with the asuras likewise’. The construction of एषाम् (with तुतुषुः or with पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः) is also open.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • गणैश्च तैस्तत्र gaṇaiśca taistatra → गणैः + च + तैः + तत्र

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga → ś before c; visarga → s before t.

  • यथैषां yathaiṣāṃ → यथा + एषाम्

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    ā + e → ai. The final m becomes anusvāra before t (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • तुतुषुर्देवाः tutuṣurdevāḥ → तुतुषुः + देवाः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After u the visarga appears as r before the voiced d.

  • पुष्पवृष्टिमुचो दिवि puṣpavṛṣṭimuco divi → पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः + दिवि

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः puṣpavṛṣṭimucaḥ

    upapada tatpuruṣa (second member the root-noun -मुच्, क्विप्) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (पुष्पवृष्टि)

    Members
    पुष्पवृष्टि + मुच्
    Vigraha
    पुष्पवृष्टिं मुञ्चन्ति इति; पुष्पवृष्टिः = पुष्पाणां वृष्टिः puṣpavṛṣṭiṃ muñcanti iti; puṣpavṛṣṭiḥ = puṣpāṇāṃ vṛṣṭiḥ
    Literally
    “releasers of a rain of flowers”
    In the verse
    the gods scattering flowers from the sky in celebration

  • महासुरैः mahāsuraiḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + असुर
    Vigraha
    महान्तः असुराः, तैः mahāntaḥ asurāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally
    “with great asuras”
    In the verse
    with the asura champions (the variant तथासुरैः reads ‘and with the asuras’)
Morphology & anvaya

  • कृतम् kṛtam

    Impersonal-looking passive: युद्धं कृतम् ‘battle was made’ with the agents in the instrumental (देव्या गणैः च) and the opponents also in the instrumental (महासुरैः). The two instrumentals are told apart by sense.

  • तुतुषुः tutuṣuḥ

    Perfect of √तुष् with reduplication तु-: ‘they were gratified’.

  • पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः puṣpavṛṣṭimucaḥ

    A root-noun (the bare root √मुच् with the zero suffix क्विप्) as the last member of a compound: ‘-releasing, releaser of …’. Nominative plural -मुचः.

Anvaya · prose order

तत्र देव्या तैः गणैः च महासुरैः (सह) युद्धं (तथा) कृतं, यथा दिवि पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः देवाः एषां तुतुषुः।

tatra devyā taiḥ gaṇaiḥ ca mahāsuraiḥ (saha) yuddhaṃ (tathā) kṛtaṃ, yathā divi puṣpavṛṣṭimucaḥ devāḥ eṣāṃ tutuṣuḥ |

Main clause in the passive (युद्धं कृतम् with instrumental agents), followed by a यथा-clause of result. एषाम् ‘of them’ is a loose genitive of reference: the gods were pleased with them (with the Devī and her gaṇas); it can also be taken with पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः, ‘showering flowers on them’.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The chapter closes on the gods’ response: the battle of the Devī and her gaṇas against the asura champions is such that the gods, watching from heaven, shower flowers. The asura army is destroyed; Mahiṣāsura himself remains for the next chapter (महिषासुरवधः).

Traditional interpretation

The text has a variant here: the Gita Press reads कृतं युद्धं महासुरैः ‘with the great asuras’; GRETIL and Freund read कृतं युद्धं तथासुरैः ‘and likewise with the asuras’ (corrections.json, variants). The sense of the verse is the same. In the Gita Press pāṭha the chapter’s last mantra closes with the bīja ॐ, which belongs to the recitation, not to the Purāṇa.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the chapter of slaughter ends not with corpses but with flowers falling from the sky — the gods’ gratitude. The violence is told as the clearing of the way for what the gods could not do for themselves.

❁

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयपुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहात्म्ये महिषासुरसैन्यवधो नाम द्वितीयोऽध्यायः॥

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyapurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhātmye mahiṣāsurasainyavadho nāma dvitīyo'dhyāyaḥ ||

Reflecting on the chapter

The gods have lost heaven to Mahiṣāsura and wander the earth. Their report rouses Viṣṇu and Śiva to anger, and from that anger and from every god the radiance pours out that becomes a woman, the auspicious Śivā. The gods arm and adorn her from their own weapons and treasures; her roar shakes the worlds, and Mahiṣa, rushing to the sound, sees her as wide as the cosmos. His generals and their uncountable armies attack; she cuts down their weapons as if in play, her breath becomes an army, and in a moment the whole host is consumed. The chapter ends with flowers falling from heaven and Mahiṣāsura himself still to be faced (2.69).

Verses for study and memorisation

  • 2.8The gods end their complaint: they have come for refuge and ask that Mahiṣa’s death be thought out.
